30.41666pt\hbox{\vbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 11.79341pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathcal{D}_{1}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{}\hbox{\vbox{\vskip 1.0pt}}\lower 0.0pt\hbox{}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$[e_{1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}f_{1}]$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\hbox{$\;\;\ldots\;\;$}\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 12.27202pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathcal{D}_{n}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{}\hbox{\vbox{\vskip 1.0pt}}\lower 0.0pt\hbox{}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$[e_{n}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}f_{n}]$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{}\hbox{\vbox{\vskip 1.0pt}}\lower 0.0pt\hbox{}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 44.42528pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathcal{D}_{\iota}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 39.38248pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{}\hbox{\vbox{\vskip 1.0pt}}\lower 0.0pt\hbox{}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 39.38248pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$e\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}f$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}\quad\right\}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}

(1)

(where
[e_{1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}f_{1}]
, …
[e_{n}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}f_{n}]

denote the assumption classes of
e_{1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}f_{n}
, …,
e_{1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}f_{n}

in leafs at the top of the prooftree
\mathcal{D}_{\iota}
).
The result of this step is a derivation
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
in
{\mathcal{S}}
with the same conclusion as
\mathcal{D}
.
Since
\mathcal{D}
was chosen arbitrary in this statement but with a bottommost instance of
R
and immediate subderivations in
{\mathcal{S}}
,
we have shown the desired transformation statement, which guarantees that
R
is correct for
{\mathcal{S}}
.

If a rule
R
is correct and admissible in an
\mathcal{E\hskip-1.0ptL}
-based proof system
{\mathcal{S}}
,
then
R
does not need to be derivable.
This is because correctness of
R
in
{\mathcal{S}}

cannot be used to mimic such instances of
R
that do not have theorems of
{\mathcal{S}}
as conclusion by derivations in
{\mathcal{S}}
.
As a trivial example we take
{\mathcal{S}}={\text{$\mathcal{E\hskip-1.0ptL}$}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)
. In
{\mathcal{S}}
only reflexivity axioms are theorems.
A
1
-premise rule that leaves its premise unchanged is clearly admissible in
{\mathcal{S}}
,
but not derivable, because instances with formulas
e\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}f
where
e\mathrel{{\not\equiv}}f
cannot be mimicked
by derivations in
{\mathcal{S}}
.

Now we introduce Milner’s proof system
Mil
, and two of its variants
Mil
′
and
\overline{\sf Mil}{}^{\prime}
.
Afterwards we gather basic connections between these systems.

{defi}

[Milner’s system
Mil
, variants and subsystems]
Let
\mathit{A}
be a set of actions.

By the proof system
{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)

we mean
the
{\text{$\mathcal{E\hskip-1.0ptL}$}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)
-based proof system for star expressions over
\mathit{A}

with the following
axiom schemes
:

\displaystyle({\text{assoc}}(\hskip-0.5pt{+}\hskip-0.5pt))\quad

\displaystyle{({e}+{f})}+{g}

\displaystyle\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}{e}+{({f}+{g})}

\displaystyle\qquad({\text{id}_{\text{l}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{{\cdot}}\hskip-0.5pt))\quad

\displaystyle{1}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{e}

\displaystyle\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}e

\displaystyle({\text{neutr}}(\hskip-0.5pt{+}\hskip-0.5pt))\quad

\displaystyle{e}+{0}

\displaystyle\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}e

\displaystyle\qquad({\text{id}_{\text{r}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{{\cdot}}\hskip-0.5pt))\quad

\displaystyle{e}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{1}

\displaystyle\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}e

\displaystyle({\text{comm}}(\hskip-0.5pt{+}\hskip-0.5pt))\quad

\displaystyle{e}+{f}

\displaystyle\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}{f}+{e}

\displaystyle\qquad(\text{deadlock})\quad

\displaystyle{0}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{e}

\displaystyle\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}0

\displaystyle({\text{idempot}}(\hskip-0.5pt{+}\hskip-0.5pt))\quad

\displaystyle{e}+{e}

\displaystyle\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}e

\displaystyle\qquad({\text{rec}}(\hskip-0.5pt{{}^{*}}\hskip-0.5pt))\quad

\displaystyle{e^{*}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}{1}+{{e}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{e^{*}}}}

\displaystyle({\text{assoc}}(\hskip-0.5pt{{\cdot}}\hskip-0.5pt))\quad

\displaystyle{({e}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{f})}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{g}

\displaystyle\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}{e}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{({f}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{g})}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad({\text{trm-body}}(\hskip-0.5pt{{}^{*}}\hskip-0.5pt))\quad

\displaystyle{e^{*}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}{({1}+{e})^{*}}

\displaystyle({\text{r-distr}}(\hskip-0.5pt{+,{\cdot}}\hskip-0.5pt))\quad

\displaystyle{({e}+{f})}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{g}

\displaystyle\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}{{e}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{g}}+{{f}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{g}}

where
e,f,g\in{\textit{StExp}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)
,
and with the
rules
of the system
{\text{$\mathcal{E\hskip-1.0ptL}$}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)
of equational logic.

The
recursive specification principle for star iteration

\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt
,
the
unique solvability principle for star iteration

\textrm{USP}\hskip-0.5pt_{1}
,
and the
general unique solvability principle

USP
are the schematically defined rules with side-conditions of the following forms:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 10.40616pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{e}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}\mathord{}$}\hbox{${{f}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{e}}+{g}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\vskip-2.06607pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to49.52435pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 1.81606pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\text{$\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt$}\ $\,${\small(if ${f}{\ndownarrow}$)}}}\vskip-2.06606pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{e}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}\mathord{}$}\hbox{${{f^{*}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{g}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\ignorespaces&\quad&\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 10.43243pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$e_{1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}{{f}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{e_{1}}}+{g}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$e_{2}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}{{f}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{e_{2}}}+{g}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip-2.0398pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to131.44699pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 1.78978pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\text{$\textrm{USP}\hskip-0.5pt_{1}$}\ $\,${\small(if ${f}{\ndownarrow}$)}}}\vskip-2.03978pt\hbox{\hskip 47.30334pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$e_{1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}e_{2}\rule{0.0pt}{7.0pt}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 12.1547pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Bigl\{\,e_{i,1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}\bigl(\,\sum_{j=1}^{n_{i}}f_{i,j}\,{{\cdot}}\,e_{j,1}\,\bigr)+g_{i}\,\bigr)\quad e_{i,2}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}\bigl(\,\sum_{j=1}^{n_{i}}f_{i,j}\,{{\cdot}}\,e_{j,2}\,\bigr)+g_{i}\,\bigr)\,\Bigr\}_{i=1,\ldots,n}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\vskip-25.99814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to310.95285pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 1.74812pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt{USP}\ $\,$ \parbox{0.0pt}{\small(if ${f_{i,j}}{\ndownarrow}$
\\
\phantom{(}for all $i,j$)}}}\vskip-25.99812pt\hbox{\hskip 130.80624pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$e_{1,1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}e_{1,2}\rule{0.0pt}{7.5pt}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}

Milner’s proof system

{\text{\sf Mil}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)
is the extension of
{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)
by adding the rule
\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt
. Its variant systems
{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\prime}$}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)
, and
{\text{$\overline{\sf Mil}{}^{\prime}$}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)
,
arise from
{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)
by adding (instead of
\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt
) the rule
\textrm{USP}\hskip-0.5pt_{1}
, and respectively, the rule
USP
.

{\text{\sf ACI}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)
is the system with the axioms for
a
ssociativity,
c
ommutativity, and
i
dempotency for
+
.
We will keep the action set
\mathit{A}
implicit in the notation.

Lemma 2
.

Milner’s system
Mil
and its variants
Mil
′
and
\overline{\sf Mil}{}^{\prime}
are related as follows:

(i)

\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\prime}$}\mathrel{{\precsim}}\text{$\overline{\sf Mil}{}^{\prime}$}
,

(ii)

\text{\sf Mil}\mathrel{\sim}\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\prime}$}
.

Proof 2.2
.

Statement
(i)

of the lemma is due to the fact that instances the rule
\textrm{USP}\hskip-0.5pt_{1}
are also instances of
USP
,
and therefore

\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\prime}$}={\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}{+}{\text{$\textrm{USP}\hskip-0.5pt_{1}$}}\mathrel{{\precsim}}{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}{+}{\textrm{USP}}=\text{$\overline{\sf Mil}{}^{\prime}$}
follows.

For establishing statement
(ii)

we show that
\textrm{USP}\hskip-0.5pt_{1}
is a derivable rule in
Mil
,
and that
\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt
is a derivable rule in
Mil
′
.
Then, by Lemma
1
,
(ii)
,

\textrm{USP}\hskip-0.5pt_{1}
is an admissible rule of
Mil
,
thus
{\text{\sf Mil}}{+}{\text{$\textrm{USP}\hskip-0.5pt_{1}$}}\mathrel{\sim}\text{\sf Mil}
,
and
\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt
is an admissible rule of
Mil
′
,
hence
{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\prime}$}}{+}{\text{$\textrm{USP}\hskip-0.5pt_{1}$}}\mathrel{\sim}\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\prime}$}
.
With this we can argue as follows:

\text{\sf Mil}\mathrel{\sim}{\text{\sf Mil}}{+}{\text{$\textrm{USP}\hskip-0.5pt_{1}$}}={({\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}{+}{\text{$\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt$}})}{+}{\text{$\textrm{USP}\hskip-0.5pt_{1}$}}={({\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}{+}{\text{$\textrm{USP}\hskip-0.5pt_{1}$}})}{+}{\text{$\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt$}}={\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\prime}$}}{+}{\text{$\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt$}}\mathrel{\sim}\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\prime}$}

Then we obtain
\text{\sf Mil}\mathrel{\sim}\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\prime}$}
by transitivity of theorem equivalence
{\sim}
.

For showing that
\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt
is derivable in
Mil
′
,
we consider an instance of
\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt
as in Definition
2
,
for fixed star expressions
e
,
f
with
{f}{\ndownarrow}
, and
g
.
From its premise

e={{f}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{e}}+{g}

we have to show that the conclusion

e={{f^{*}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{g}
of the
\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt
instance can be derived by inferences in
\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\prime}$}={\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}{+}{\text{$\textrm{USP}\hskip-0.5pt_{1}$}}
.
By stepwise use of axioms of
Mil
-
we obtain:

\displaystyle{{f^{*}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{g}

\displaystyle{}\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}{({1}+{{f}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{f^{*}}}})}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{g}\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}{{1}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{g}}+{{({f}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{f^{*}}})}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{g}}

\displaystyle{}\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}{{1}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{g}}+{{f}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{({{f^{*}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{g})}}\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}{g}+{{f}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{({{f^{*}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{g})}}\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}{{f}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{({{f^{*}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{g})}}+{g}

Hence there is a derivation of

{{f^{*}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{g}={{f}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{({{f^{*}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{g})}}+{g}

in
Mil
′
.
This derivation can be extended, due to
{f}{\ndownarrow}
,
by an instance of
\textrm{USP}\hskip-0.5pt_{1}
that is applied
to
e={{f}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{e}}+{g}

and

{{f^{*}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{g}={{f}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{({{f^{*}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{g})}}+{g}
.
We obtain a derivation of

e={{f^{*}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{g}
in
Mil
′

from the assumption

e={{f}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{e}}+{g}
.

For showing that
\textrm{USP}\hskip-0.5pt_{1}
is derivable in
Mil
,
we consider an instance of
\textrm{USP}\hskip-0.5pt_{1}
as in Definition
2
,
with premises

e_{1}={{f}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{e_{1}}}+{g}
,
and

e_{2}={{f}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{e_{2}}}+{g}
,
for fixed star expressions
e_{1}
,
e_{2}
,
f
with
{f}{\ndownarrow}
, and
g
.
By two instances of
\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt
we get

e_{1}={{f^{*}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{g}
,
and

e_{2}={{f^{*}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{g}
.
By applying
Symm
below
e_{2}={{f^{*}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{g}
, we obtain

{{f^{*}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{g}=e_{2}
.
Then by applying
Trans
to
e_{1}={{f^{*}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{g}
and
{{f^{*}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{g}=e_{2}

we obtain the conclusion
e_{1}=e_{2}
of the
\textrm{USP}\hskip-0.5pt_{1}
instance.

Now we define soundness and completeness of equation-based proof systems for star expressions
with respect to equivalence relations on star expressions.
Then we formulate soundness of Milner’s system from
[
Mil84
]
,
and recall Milner’s completeness question.

{defi}

Let
{\mathcal{S}}
be an
{\textit{Eq}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)
-based proof system and let
\mathrel{{\simeq}}
be an equivalence relation on
{\textit{StExp}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)
.
We say that
{\mathcal{S}}
is sound for
\mathrel{{\simeq}}

if, for all
e,f\in{\textit{StExp}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)
,
e\mathrel{{=_{{\mathcal{S}}}\hskip 1.0pt}}f
implies
e\mathrel{{\simeq}}f
.
We say that
{\mathcal{S}}
is complete for
\mathrel{{\simeq}}

if, for all
e,f\in{\textit{StExp}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)
,
e\mathrel{{\simeq}}f
implies
e\mathrel{{=_{{\mathcal{S}}}\hskip 1.0pt}}f
.

{propC}

[
[
Mil84
]
]

Mil
is sound for process semantics equality
{=_{\scriptscriptstyle\llbracket{\cdot}\rrbracket_{\hskip-0.5ptP}}}
on regular expressions.
That is, for all
e,f\in{\textit{StExp}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)
it holds:

(\,e\mathrel{{=_{\text{\sf Mil}}\hskip 1.0pt}}f\;\;\Longrightarrow\;\;e\mathrel{{=_{\scriptscriptstyle\llbracket{\cdot}\rrbracket_{\hskip-0.5ptP}}}}f\,)\hskip 1.5pt{,}

and hence

(\,e\mathrel{{=_{\text{\sf Mil}}\hskip 1.0pt}}f\;\;\Longrightarrow\;\;e\mathrel{{=_{\scriptscriptstyle\llbracket{\cdot}\rrbracket_{\hskip-0.5ptP}}}}f\,)\hskip 1.5pt{.}

Question 3

(
[
Mil84
]
)
.

Is
Mil
complete for bisimilarity of process interpretations?
That is, does for all
e,f\in{\textit{StExp}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)
the implication

(\,e\mathrel{{=_{\text{\sf Mil}}\hskip 1.0pt}}f\;\;\Longleftarrow\;\;{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)\mathrel{\hbox{\kern 0.43057pt\vbox{\hbox{\raise 0.43057pt\hbox{\kern-0.43057pt{$\leftrightarrow$}\kern-0.43057pt}}\hrule}\kern 0.43057pt}\hskip 1.0pt}{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{f}\hskip-0.5pt)\,)
hold?

Finally we define the crucial concept of provable solution of a
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart with respect to an
\mathcal{E\hskip-1.0ptL}
-based proof system.
Intuitively, a ‘provable solution’ of a
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
is a provable solution of some recursive specification
{\mathcal{S}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.5pt)

that is associated with
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
in a natural way (see for example the two examples on page
1
).
Since associating specifications
{\mathcal{S}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.5pt)
to
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-charts
\underline{\mathcal{C}}

presupposes the use of some list representation for the set
{T_{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{v}\hskip-0.5pt)
of transitions from vertex
v
,
for every vertex
v
of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
,
any such association map cannot be unique.
The definition of provable solutions of
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-charts below
uses such list representations implicitly, and assumes that associativity, commutativity, and reflexivity axioms are present in the underlying proof system.
In this way the concept of provable solution permits us to avoid defining associated specifications for
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-charts in some canonical (but still necessarily arbitrary) way.
In the next section we explain an alternative characterization of provable solutions.

{defi}

[provable solutions]
Let
{\mathcal{S}}
be an
\mathcal{E\hskip-1.0ptL}
-based proof system for star expressions over
\mathit{A}

that extends
ACI
.
Let
\underline{\mathcal{C}}=\langle V,\mathit{A},{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1},v_{\hskip-0.5pt\text{s}},{{\to}},{{\downarrow}}\rangle
be a
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart.

By a
star expression function on
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
we mean a function
s\mathrel{:}V\to{\textit{StExp}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)

on the vertices of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
.
Let
v\in V
.
We say that such a star expression function
s
on
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
is an
{\mathcal{S}}
-provable solution of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
at
v
if it holds that:

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:def:provable:solution}{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{v}\hskip-0.5pt)\,\mathrel{{=_{{\mathcal{S}}}\hskip 1.0pt}}\,{{\tau_{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{v}\hskip-0.5pt)}+{\sum_{i=1}^{n}{{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{a}}_{i}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{v_{i}}\hskip-0.5pt)}}\hskip 1.5pt{,}

(2)

given the (possibly redundant) list representation

{T_{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{v}\hskip-0.5pt)=\bigl\{{v\mathrel{{\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{a}}_{i}}}}}v_{i}}\mathrel{\big|}{i\in\left\{{1,\ldots,n}\right\}}\bigr\}
,
of transitions from
v
in
\underline{\mathcal{C}}

and where
{\tau_{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{v}\hskip-0.5pt)

is the
termination constant
{\tau_{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{v}\hskip-0.5pt)
of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
at
v

defined as
0
if
{v}{\ndownarrow}
,
and as
1
if
{v}{{\downarrow}}
.
This definition does not depend on the specifically chosen list representation of
{T_{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{v}\hskip-0.5pt)
, because
{\mathcal{S}}
extends
ACI
, and therefore it contains the associativity, commutativity, and idempotency axioms for
+
.

By an
{\mathcal{S}}
-provable solution of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}

(with
principal value
{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{v_{\hskip-0.5pt\text{s}}}\hskip-0.5pt)
at the start vertex
v_{\hskip-0.5pt\text{s}}
)
we mean a star expression function
s
on
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
that is an
{\mathcal{S}}
-provable solution of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
at every vertex of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
.

3.
Characterization of provable solutions of
1
-charts

This section is an intermezzo in which we link
to an elegant coalgebraic formulation of the concept of provable solution
by Schmid, Rot, and Silva in
[
SRS21
]
.
Their observation is a crucial first part of a detailed and beautiful coalgebraic analysis
of the completeness proof in
[
GF20b
,
GF20a
]

by Fokkink and myself for a tailored restriction of Milner’s system
Mil
to ‘
1
-free star expressions’.
Here we reformulate their characterization of provable solution by means of the terminology that we are using here,
and explain the connection, but do not prove the statements in detail.
This is because we will only use this characterization later as
an additional motivation for our concept of ‘coinductive proof’,
but not for developing the proof transformations to and from the coinductive reformulation
cMil
of Milner’s system
Mil
.

Schmid, Rot, and Silva construe the operational process semantics that a transition system like that in Definition
2

induces on the set
StExp
of all star expressions as a coalgebra (also denoted by)
StExp
.
Charts can also be represented as (finite) coalgebras due to their structure as transition graphs.
On this basis,
they obtain the following characterization of provable solutions for proof systems
{\mathcal{S}}
like
Mil
and
Mil
-
.

Lemma 4

(
\sim\,
Lemma 2.2 in
[
SRS21
]
)
.

For every chart
\mathcal{C}
, for every star-expression function
s\mathrel{:}{V\hskip-1.0pt}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathcal{C}}\hskip-0.5pt)\to\textit{StExp}
,
and for
{\mathcal{S}}\in\left\{{\text{\sf Mil},\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\right\}
,
the following two statements are equivalent:

(i)

s

is an
{\mathcal{S}}
-provable solution of a chart
\mathcal{C}
.

(ii)

\mathcal{C}\overset{\left[{s}\right]_{{=_{{\mathcal{S}}}\hskip 1.0pt}}}{\longrightarrow}{{\textit{StExp}}/_{{=_{{\mathcal{S}}}\hskip 1.0pt}}}

is a coalgebra homomorphism,
where
\begin{aligned} \left[{s}\right]_{{=_{{\mathcal{S}}}\hskip 1.0pt}}\!\!\mathrel{:}{V\hskip-1.0pt}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathcal{C}}\hskip-0.5pt)&{}\to{{\textit{StExp}}/_{{=_{{\mathcal{S}}}\hskip 1.0pt}}}\\[-2.15277pt]
v&{}\mapsto\left[{{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{v}\hskip-0.5pt)}\right]_{{=_{{\mathcal{S}}}\hskip 1.0pt}}\hskip 1.5pt{.}\end{aligned}

By analyzing the proof of this statement on the basis of terminology we use here, we find the following.
Schmid, Rot, and Silva noticed that statements
(a)
and
(b)
below hold, and used them in conjunction with
(c)

(which is analogous to Proposition 2.9 in
[
GF20b
,
GF20a
]
)
to obtain the characterization for charts above. We extend it to
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-charts here in
(d)
:

(a)

Provability in a system
{\mathcal{S}}
like Milner’s defines a bisimulation relation
on the the set of star expressions when that is endowed with the process semantics.
(See Lemma
5
).

(b)

Due to
(a)
a factor chart
{{{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)}/_{\mathrel{{=_{{\mathcal{S}}}\hskip 1.0pt}}}}
can be defined such
that
{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)\hskip 0.0pt\mathrel{{\hbox{\kern 0.21529pt\vbox{\hbox{\raise 0.43057pt\hbox{\kern-0.43057pt{$\rightarrow$}\kern-0.43057pt}}\hrule}\kern 0.43057pt}}}{{{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)}/_{{=_{{\mathcal{S}}}\hskip 1.0pt}}}
holds,
that is, there is a functional bisimulation from
{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)
to
{{{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)}/_{{=_{{\mathcal{S}}}\hskip 1.0pt}}}
.
(See Lemma
7
).

(c)

Every star expression
e
is the principal value of a
Mil
-provable solution of
the chart interpretation
{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)
of
e
.
(See Lemma
9
).
Equivalently, every star expression
e
can be
Mil
-provably reconstructed
from the transitions to its derivatives in the process semantics. (See Lemma
8
.)

(d)

A star expression function
s
with principal value
e
is a
Mil
-provable solution of a
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart
\underline{\mathcal{C}}

if and only if
the relativization
\left[{s}\right]_{{=_{\text{\sf Mil}}\hskip 1.0pt}}
of
s
to
{{=_{\text{\sf Mil}}\hskip 1.0pt}}
-equivalence classes
defines a
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-bisimulation from
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
to
{{{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)}/_{{=_{{\mathcal{S}}}\hskip 1.0pt}}}
. (See Proposition
10
.)

We formulate these statements more precisely here below. We start with a general definition of factor charts.
But the property
(b)
of factor charts of the chart interpretation with respect to provability
will then only be shown in Lemma
6
below,
after the formulation of the property
(a)
in Lemma
5
.

{defi}

[factor chart]
Let
\mathcal{C}=\langle V,\mathit{A},{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1},v_{\hskip-0.5pt\text{s}},{{\to}},{{\downarrow}}\rangle

be a chart (that is, a
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-transition free
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart).
Let
{\simeq}
be an equivalence relation on
V
. Then we define the
factor chart

{{\mathcal{C}}/_{{\simeq}}}
of
\mathcal{C}
with respect to
{\simeq}
by:

\displaystyle{{\mathcal{C}}/_{{\simeq}}}=\langle{{V}/_{{\simeq}}},\mathit{A},{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1},\left[{v_{\hskip-0.5pt\text{s}}}\right]_{{\simeq}},{{{{\to}}}/_{{\simeq}}},{{{{\downarrow}}}/_{{\simeq}}}\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\text{ where: }\begin{aligned} &{{V}/_{{\simeq}}}\mathrel{{:=}}\bigl\{{\left[{v}\right]_{{\simeq}}}\mathrel{\big|}{v\in V}\bigr\}\hskip 1.5pt{,}\text{ and for all $v,v_{1},v_{2}\in V$ and $a\in\mathit{A}$: }\\
&\left[{v_{1}}\right]_{{\simeq}}\mathrel{{{{\xrightarrow{a}}/_{{\simeq}}}}}\left[{v_{2}}\right]_{{\simeq}}\;\mathrel{:}\>\Longleftrightarrow\;\begin{aligned} &\text{there are $\tilde{v}_{1},\tilde{v}_{2}\in V$ such that }v_{1}\mathrel{{\simeq}}\tilde{v}_{1}\mathrel{{\xrightarrow{a}}}\tilde{v}_{2}\mathrel{{\simeq}}v_{2}\hskip 1.5pt{,}\end{aligned}\\
&{({\left[{v}\right]_{{\simeq}}})}{{{{\downarrow}/_{{\simeq}}}}}\;\mathrel{:}\>\Longleftrightarrow\;{v}{{\downarrow}}\hskip 1.5pt{.}\end{aligned}

By the projection function from
\mathcal{C}
to
{{\mathcal{C}}/_{{\simeq}}}

we mean the function
\pi_{{\simeq}}\mathrel{:}V\to{{V}/_{{\simeq}}}
,
v\mapsto\left[{v}\right]_{{\simeq}}
.

Lemma 5
.

Let
{\mathcal{S}}\in\left\{{\text{${\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)$},\text{${\text{\sf Mil}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)$}}\right\}
.
Then provability
\mathrel{{=_{{\mathcal{S}}}\hskip 1.0pt}}
with respect to
{\mathcal{S}}
is a bisimulation on the chart interpretation
{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)
of
e
, for every
e\in{\textit{StExp}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)
.

Proof 3.1

(Proof (Idea))
.

By verifying the bisimulation conditions (forth), (back), (termination)
for conclusions of derivations in
{\mathcal{S}}
, proceeding by induction on the depth of derivations in
{\mathcal{S}}
.
In the base case, this is settled for the axioms of
Mil
-
.
In the induction step, it is settled for the conclusions of the reflexivity, symmetry, and transitivity rules of
\mathcal{E\hskip-1.0ptL}
,
and of the fixed-point rule
\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt
in
Mil
.
(The arguments are similar to the proof of Theorem 2.1 in
[
SRS21
]
.)

Lemma 6
.

Let
\mathcal{C}=\langle V,\mathit{A},{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1},v_{\hskip-0.5pt\text{s}},{{\to}},{{\downarrow}}\rangle

be a (
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-free) chart. Let
{\simeq}
be an equivalence relation on
V
that is a bisimulation on
\mathcal{C}
.

Then

\mathcal{C}\hskip-0.5pt\mathrel{{{\hbox{\kern 0.21529pt\vbox{\hbox{\raise 0.43057pt\hbox{\kern-0.43057pt{$\rightarrow$}\kern-0.43057pt}}\hrule}\kern 0.43057pt}}_{\pi_{{\simeq}}}}}{{\mathcal{C}}/_{{\simeq}}}

holds,
that is,
\pi_{{\simeq}}
defines a functional bisimulation from
\mathcal{C}
to
{{\mathcal{C}}/_{{\simeq}}}
.

Proof 3.2

(Proof (hint))
.

The (forth) and (termination) conditions for the graph
{\mathit{graph}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\pi_{{\simeq}}}\hskip-0.5pt)
of
\pi_{{\simeq}}

to be a bisimulation are easy to verify.
For demonstrating also the (back) condition for
{\mathit{graph}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\pi_{{\simeq}}}\hskip-0.5pt)
to be a bisimulation
it is crucial to use the assumption that
{\simeq}
is a bisimulation on
\mathcal{C}
.

Lemma 7
.

{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)\hskip-0.5pt\mathrel{{{\hbox{\kern 0.21529pt\vbox{\hbox{\raise 0.43057pt\hbox{\kern-0.43057pt{$\rightarrow$}\kern-0.43057pt}}\hrule}\kern 0.43057pt}}_{\pi_{{=_{{\mathcal{S}}}\hskip 1.0pt}}}}}{{{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)}/_{{=_{{\mathcal{S}}}\hskip 1.0pt}}}

for every
e\in{\textit{StExp}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)
, and
{\mathcal{S}}\in\left\{{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$},\text{\sf Mil}}\right\}
.

Proof 3.3
.

Let
{\mathcal{S}}\in\left\{{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$},\text{\sf Mil}}\right\}
.
Due to Lemma
5
,
\mathrel{{=_{{\mathcal{S}}}\hskip 1.0pt}}
is a bisimulation on
{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)
.
Then
we obtain

{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)\hskip-0.5pt\mathrel{{{\hbox{\kern 0.21529pt\vbox{\hbox{\raise 0.43057pt\hbox{\kern-0.43057pt{$\rightarrow$}\kern-0.43057pt}}\hrule}\kern 0.43057pt}}_{\pi_{{=_{{\mathcal{S}}}\hskip 1.0pt}}}}}{{{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)}/_{{=_{{\mathcal{S}}}\hskip 1.0pt}}}

by applying Lemma
6
.

The following lemma states that every star expression
e
can be reconstructed, provably in
Mil
-
,
from the transitions that it facilitates in the process semantics, and the targets of these transitions.
Statements like this are frequently viewed as being analogous to the fundamental theorem of calculus,
which states that every differentiable function can be reconstructed from its derivative function via integration.

Lemma 8
.

e\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}{\tau_{{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)+\sum_{i=1}^{n}{a_{i}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{e^{\prime}_{i}}
holds,
given a list representation

{T_{{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)}}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)=\bigl\{{e\mathrel{{\xrightarrow{a_{i}}}}e^{\prime}_{i}}\mathrel{\big|}{i\in\left\{{1,\ldots,n}\right\}}\bigr\}

of the transitions from
e
in the chart interpretation
{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)
of
e
.

Proof 3.4

(Proof (hint))
.

The proof proceeds by induction on the structure of the star expression
e
.
All axioms of
Mil
-
(and hence all axioms of
Mil
) are necessary in the arguments.
An analogous statement that can be viewed as the restriction of the statement of Lemma
8

for ‘
1
-free star expressions’
was proved as Lemma A.2 in
[
GF20b
]
,
and as Theorem 2.2 in
[
SRS21
]
.
Here we will prove an analogous statement, Lemma
16
, in the next section.

Lemma 9
.

For every star expression
e\in{\textit{StExp}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)

with chart interpretation
{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)=\langle{V\hskip-1.0pt}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt),\mathit{A},{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1},e,{{\to}},{{\downarrow}}\rangle

the identical star-expression function
\textrm{id}_{{V\hskip-1.0pt}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)}\mathrel{:}{V\hskip-1.0pt}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)\to{\textit{StExp}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)
,
e\mapsto e

is a
Mil
-
-provable solution of
{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)
with principal value
e
.

Proof 3.5
.

The correctness conditions for the star-expression function
\textrm{id}_{{V\hskip-1.0pt}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)}

to be a
Mil
-
-provable solution of the chart interpretation
{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)
of
e

are guaranteed by Lemma
8
.

On the basis of these preparations we now reformulate the characterization of provable solutions of charts by Schmid, Rot, and Silva
as a characterization of provable solutions of
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-charts via functional
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-bisimulations to factor charts of appropriate chart interpretations.

Proposition 10
.

Let
\underline{\mathcal{C}}=\langle V,\mathit{A},v_{\hskip-0.5pt\text{s}},{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1},{{\to}},{{\downarrow}}\rangle
be a
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart.
Let
{\mathcal{S}}\in\left\{{\text{${\text{\sf Mil}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)$},\text{${\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)$}}\right\}
.
Then for all star expression functions
s\mathrel{:}V\to{\textit{StExp}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)
it holds:

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:prop:char:solution:SRS}\text{$s$ is {${\mathcal{S}}$}-pro\-vable solution of $\underline{\mathcal{C}}$}\;\;\>\;\Longleftrightarrow\;\;\;\;\underline{\mathcal{C}}\>\hskip-1.5pt\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\hbox{\kern 0.21529pt\vbox{\hbox{\raise 0.43057pt\hbox{\kern-0.43057pt{$\rightarrow$}\kern-0.43057pt}}\hrule}\kern 0.43057pt}}_{\left[{s}\right]_{\mathrel{{=_{{\mathcal{S}}}\hskip 1.0pt}}}}}}\!\!{{{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{v_{\hskip-0.5pt\text{s}}}\hskip-0.5pt)}\hskip-0.5pt)}/_{\mathrel{{=_{{\mathcal{S}}}\hskip 1.0pt}}}}\hskip 1.5pt{.}

(3)

where
\left[{s}\right]_{\mathrel{{=_{{\mathcal{S}}}\hskip 1.0pt}}}\mathrel{{:=}}{\pi_{{=_{{\mathcal{S}}}\hskip 1.0pt}}}\circ{s}

with the projection
\pi_{{=_{{\mathcal{S}}}\hskip 1.0pt}}\mathrel{:}{\textit{StExp}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)\to{{{\textit{StExp}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)}/_{{=_{{\mathcal{S}}}\hskip 1.0pt}}}
,
e\mapsto\left[{e}\right]_{{=_{{\mathcal{S}}}\hskip 1.0pt}}
.

Proof 3.6

(Proof (sketch))
.

A technical part of the proof consists in showing that
for every star-expression function
s\mathrel{:}V\to{\textit{StExp}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)

on a
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart
\underline{\mathcal{C}}=\langle V,\mathit{A},{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1},v_{\hskip-0.5pt\text{s}},{{\to}},{{\downarrow}}\rangle

the following two statements are equivalent:

(i)

s
is an
{\mathcal{S}}
-provable solution of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
,

(ii)

s
is an
{\mathcal{S}}
-provable solution of the ‘induced chart’
\underline{\mathcal{C}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8047}\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{({\cdot}]}}}=\langle V,\mathit{A},{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1},{\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8047}{\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{(}}\hskip 0.0pt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cdot}}\hskip 0.4pt{\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{]}}}}},{\downarrow^{\hskip-1.5pt{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptstyle(1)$}}}}\rangle
of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}

that results by using induced transitions as transitions, and induced termination as termination, that is, with:

\displaystyle{\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8047}{\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{(}}\hskip 0.0pt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cdot}}\hskip 0.4pt{\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{]}}}}}

\displaystyle{}\,\mathrel{{:=}}\,\bigl\{{\langle v,\hskip 0.5pta,\hskip 0.5ptv^{\prime}\rangle}\mathrel{\big|}{a\in\mathit{A},\,v,v^{\prime}\in V,\>v\mathrel{{\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8047}{\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{(}}\hskip 0.0pt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}a}}\hskip 0.4pt{\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{]}}}}}}v^{\prime}}\bigr\}\hskip 1.5pt{,}

\displaystyle{\downarrow^{\hskip-1.5pt{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptstyle(1)$}}}}

\displaystyle{}\,\mathrel{{:=}}\,\bigl\{{v}\mathrel{\big|}{v\in V,\>{v}{{\downarrow^{\hskip-1.5pt{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptstyle(1)$}}}}}}\bigr\}\hskip 1.5pt{.}

For the implication ‘
\Rightarrow
’ in (
3
)
we assume that
s
is a
{\mathcal{S}}
-provable solution of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
.
By the auxiliary statement above,
s
is then also a
{\mathcal{S}}
-provable solution of the induced chart
\underline{\mathcal{C}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8047}\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{({\cdot}]}}}
of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
.
Then it is not difficult to verify the
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-bisimulation conditions (forth), (back), and (termination)
for
\left[{s}\right]_{{=_{{\mathcal{S}}}\hskip 1.0pt}}
to define a
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-bisimulation from
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
to
{{{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{v_{\hskip-0.5pt\text{s}}}\hskip-0.5pt)}\hskip-0.5pt)}/_{\mathrel{{=_{{\mathcal{S}}}\hskip 1.0pt}}}}
.

For the converse implication ‘
\Leftarrow
’ in (
3
),
we assume
s\mathrel{:}V\to{\textit{StExp}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)
as a star expression function
with
\underline{\mathcal{C}}\>\hskip-1.5pt\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\hbox{\kern 0.21529pt\vbox{\hbox{\raise 0.43057pt\hbox{\kern-0.43057pt{$\rightarrow$}\kern-0.43057pt}}\hrule}\kern 0.43057pt}}_{\left[{s}\right]_{\mathrel{{=_{{\mathcal{S}}}\hskip 1.0pt}}}}}}\!\!{{{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{v_{\hskip-0.5pt\text{s}}}\hskip-0.5pt)}\hskip-0.5pt)}/_{\mathrel{{=_{{\mathcal{S}}}\hskip 1.0pt}}}}
.
Here Lemma
8
,
the possibility to
{\mathcal{S}}
-provably reconstruct a star expression
e
from the transitions to its derivatives,
can be employed in order to recognize
s
as a
{\mathcal{S}}
-provable solution of the induced chart
\underline{\mathcal{C}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8047}\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{({\cdot}]}}}
of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
.
Then by applying the auxiliary statement,
we obtain that
s
is also a
{\mathcal{S}}
-provable solution of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
.

4.
Layered loop existence and elimination, and LLEE-witnesses

In this subsection we recall definitions from
[
GF20a
,
Gra21c
]

of the loop existence and elimination condition
LEE
,
its ‘layered’ version
LLEE
, and of chart labelings that witness these conditions.
Specifically we will use the adaptation of these concepts to
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-charts that has been introduced in
[
Gra21c
]
,
because the use of
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-charts with
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-transitions will be crucial for the concept of ‘LLEE-witnessed coinductive proof’ in Section
5
.
For this purpose we also recall the ‘
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart interpretation’ of star expressions
as introduced in
[
Gra21c
]

for which the property
LLEE
is guaranteed in contrast to the chart interpretation from Definition
2
.
We will keep formalities to a minimum as these are necessary for our purpose here,
and have to refer to

[
GF20a
,
Gra21c
]

and the appertaining reports

[
GF20b
,
Gra20
]
for more details.

We start with the definitions of loop
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-charts, and of loop sub(-
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
)charts, and examples for these concepts.

{defi}

[loop
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart]
A
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart
\underline{\mathcal{L}}=\langle V,\mathit{A},{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1},v_{\hskip-0.5pt\text{s}},{{\to}},{{\downarrow}}\rangle
is called a
loop
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart
if
it satisfies three conditions:

(L1)

There is an infinite path from the start vertex
v_{\hskip-0.5pt\text{s}}
.

(L2)

Every infinite path from
v_{\hskip-0.5pt\text{s}}
returns to
v_{\hskip-0.5pt\text{s}}
after a positive number of transitions.

(L3)

Immediate termination is only permitted at the start vertex, that is,
{{\downarrow}}\subseteq\left\{{v_{\hskip-0.5pt\text{s}}}\right\}
.

Transitions from
v_{\hskip-0.5pt\text{s}}
are called
loop-entry transitions
,
all other transitions
loop-body transitions
.

(L1)

{v_{0}}
{v_{1}}
{v_{2}}
(L1)
,
(L2)
,
(L3)

(L1)
,
(L2)
,
(L3)

{v_{0}}
{v_{1}}
{v_{2}}
(L1)
,
(L2)
,
(L3)
loop
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart

{v_{0}}
{v_{1}}
{v_{2}}
loop sub-
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}\underline{\mathcal{L}}}

Figure 3.

Four
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-charts (action labels ignored)
that violate at least one loop
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart condition
(L1)
,
(L2)
, or
(L3)
,
and a loop
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
with one of its loop sub-
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-charts
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}\underline{\mathcal{L}}}
.

{exa}

In Figure
3
we have gathered, on the left,
four examples of
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-charts (with action labels ignored) that are
not
loop
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-charts:
each of them violates one of the conditions
(L1)
,
(L2)
, or
(L3)
.
The paths in red indicate violations of
(L2)
, and
(L3)
, respectively,
where the thicker arrows from the start vertex indicate transitions that would need to be (but are not) loop-entry transitions.
However, the
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
in Figure
3
is indeed a loop
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart.

{defi}

[loop sub-
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart of
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart]
Let
\underline{\mathcal{C}}=\langle V,\mathit{A},{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1},v_{\hskip-0.5pt\text{s}},{{\to}},{{\downarrow}}\rangle

be a
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart.

A
loop sub-
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart of
a
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart
\underline{\mathcal{C}}

is a loop
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart
\underline{\mathcal{L}}

that is a sub-
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}

with some vertex
v\in V
of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
as start vertex
such that
\underline{\mathcal{L}}
is formed, for a nonempty set
U
of transitions of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
from
v
,
by all vertices and transitions on paths that start with a transition in
U
and continue onward until
v
is reached again;
in this case the transitions in
U
are the loop-entry transitions of
\underline{\mathcal{L}}
,
and we say that the transitions in
U

induce

\underline{\mathcal{L}}
.

{exa}

In the
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
in Figure
3

we have illustrated (in the right copy of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
)
a loop sub-
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}\underline{\mathcal{L}}}
of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
with start vertex
v_{2}

that is induced by the set
U\mathrel{{:=}}\left\{{\langle v_{2},\hskip 0.5pta,\hskip 0.5ptv_{0}\rangle}\right\}

that consists of the single loop-entry transition from
v_{2}
to
v_{0}
, assuming that its action label is
a
.
Then
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}\underline{\mathcal{L}}}
consists of all
colored
transitions.
– We note that also the generated sub-
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}{\downarrow}^{\hskip-0.75ptv_{2}}_{\hskip-0.5pt*}
of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}

that is rooted at
v_{2}
is a loop sub-
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
,
because it is a loop
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart, and it
that is generated by the set of both of the two transitions from
v_{2}
.

{defi}

[(single-/multi-step) loop elimination]
Let
\underline{\mathcal{C}}=\langle V,\mathit{A},{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1},v_{\hskip-0.5pt\text{s}},{{\to}},{{\downarrow}}\rangle
be a
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart.
Suppose that
\underline{\mathcal{L}}
,
\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{1}
, …,
\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{n}
are loop sub-
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-charts with sets
U,U_{1},\ldots,U_{n}
of loop-entry transitions
from their start vertices
v,v_{1},\ldots,v_{n}\in V
, respectively,
for
n\in{\rm Nature}\setminus\left\{{0}\right\}
.

The
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart
\underline{\mathcal{C}}\hskip-0.4pt^{\prime}
that results by the
elimination of (the loop sub-
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart)
\underline{\mathcal{L}}
from
\underline{\mathcal{C}}

arises by removing all loop-entry transitions in
U
of
\underline{\mathcal{L}}
from
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
,
and then also removing all vertices and transitions that become unreachable;
in this case we write
\underline{\mathcal{C}}\mathrel{\Rightarrow_{\text{{elim}}}}\underline{\mathcal{C}}\hskip-0.4pt^{\prime}
,
and also say that

\underline{\mathcal{C}}\hskip-0.4pt^{\prime}
results by a
single-step loop elimination
from
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
.

Suppose that the loop sub-
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-charts
\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{1}
, …,
\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{n}

satisfy the following two conditions:

(ms-1)

their sets
U_{1},\ldots,U_{n}
of loop-entry transitions are disjoint (that is,
U_{i}\cap U_{j}\neq\varnothing

for all
i,j\in\left\{{1,\ldots,n}\right\}
with
i\neq j
),

(ms-2)

no start vertex of a loop sub-
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart
\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{i}
is in the body of another one
\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{j}
,
for all
i,j\in\left\{{1,\ldots,n}\right\}
with
i\neq j
.

Then we say
that a
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart
\underline{\mathcal{C}}\hskip-0.4pt^{\prime}
results by the
multi-step loop elimination of
\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{1}
, …,
\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{n}
from
\underline{\mathcal{C}}

if
\underline{\mathcal{C}}\hskip-0.4pt^{\prime}
arises from
\underline{\mathcal{C}}

by removing all loop-entry transitions in
U_{1},\ldots,U_{n}
of
\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{1}
, …,
\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{n}
from
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
,
and then also removing all vertices and transitions that become unreachable;
in this case we write
\underline{\mathcal{C}}\mathrel{\mbox{$\Longrightarrow$\hskip-12.80365pt\raisebox{-0.275pt}{\hbox to5.52pt{\vbox to5.52pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.76074pt\lower-2.76074pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{{{}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}
}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 M 3.54 0 C 3.54 1.96 1.96 3.54 0 3.54 C -1.96 3.54 -3.54 1.96 -3.54 0 C -3.54 -1.96 -1.96 -3.54 0 -3.54 C 1.96 -3.54 3.54 -1.96 3.54 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\hskip 7.39755pt}\hskip-1.0pt_{\text{{elim}}}}\underline{\mathcal{C}}\hskip-0.4pt^{\prime}
,
and say that

\underline{\mathcal{C}}\hskip-0.4pt^{\prime}
results by a
multi-step loop elimination
from
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
.

Lemma 11
.

{\Rightarrow_{\text{{elim}}}}\,\subseteq\,{\mbox{$\Longrightarrow$\hskip-12.80365pt\raisebox{-0.275pt}{\hbox to5.52pt{\vbox to5.52pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.76074pt\lower-2.76074pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{{{}}

}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 M 3.54 0 C 3.54 1.96 1.96 3.54 0 3.54 C -1.96 3.54 -3.54 1.96 -3.54 0 C -3.54 -1.96 -1.96 -3.54 0 -3.54 C 1.96 -3.54 3.54 -1.96 3.54 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\hskip 7.39755pt}\hskip-1.0pt_{\text{{elim}}}}\,\subseteq\,{\Rightarrow_{\text{{elim}}}^{+}}
,
and consequently

{\Rightarrow_{\text{{elim}}}^{*}}={\mbox{$\Longrightarrow$\hskip-12.80365pt\raisebox{-0.275pt}{\hbox to5.52pt{\vbox to5.52pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.76074pt\lower-2.76074pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{{{}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}
}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 M 3.54 0 C 3.54 1.96 1.96 3.54 0 3.54 C -1.96 3.54 -3.54 1.96 -3.54 0 C -3.54 -1.96 -1.96 -3.54 0 -3.54 C 1.96 -3.54 3.54 -1.96 3.54 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\hskip 7.39755pt}\hskip-1.0pt_{\text{{elim}}}^{*}}
.

Proof 4.1
.

First,
{\Rightarrow_{\text{{elim}}}}\subseteq{\mbox{$\Longrightarrow$\hskip-12.80365pt\raisebox{-0.275pt}{\hbox to5.52pt{\vbox to5.52pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.76074pt\lower-2.76074pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{{{}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}
}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 M 3.54 0 C 3.54 1.96 1.96 3.54 0 3.54 C -1.96 3.54 -3.54 1.96 -3.54 0 C -3.54 -1.96 -1.96 -3.54 0 -3.54 C 1.96 -3.54 3.54 -1.96 3.54 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\hskip 7.39755pt}\hskip-1.0pt_{\text{{elim}}}}
holds because
every single-step loop elimination is also a multi-step loop elimination.
Crucially,
{\mbox{$\Longrightarrow$\hskip-12.80365pt\raisebox{-0.275pt}{\hbox to5.52pt{\vbox to5.52pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.76074pt\lower-2.76074pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{{{}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}
}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 M 3.54 0 C 3.54 1.96 1.96 3.54 0 3.54 C -1.96 3.54 -3.54 1.96 -3.54 0 C -3.54 -1.96 -1.96 -3.54 0 -3.54 C 1.96 -3.54 3.54 -1.96 3.54 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\hskip 7.39755pt}\hskip-1.0pt_{\text{{elim}}}}\subseteq{\Rightarrow_{\text{{elim}}}^{+}}
holds, because
every multi-step loop elimination of loop sub-
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-charts
\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{1},\ldots,\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{n}
in a
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart
\underline{\mathcal{C}}

with loop-entry transitions
U_{1},\ldots,U_{n}

can be implemented as a sequence of single-step loop eliminations of
\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{1},\ldots,\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{n}
irrespective of the chosen order:
hereby
(ms-1)
guarantees that every loop-entry transition belongs uniquely to one of
\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{1},\ldots,\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{n}

and thus is removed in precisely one step;
and
(ms-2)
ensures that, after the elimination of a loop sub-
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart
\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{i}
,
another one
\underline{\mathcal{L}}_{j}
with
j\neq i
that has not yet been eliminated is still a loop sub-
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart.
Finally these statements imply that the many-step versions of single-step and multi-step loop elimination coincide.

{defi}

[LEE and LLEE]
Let
\underline{\mathcal{C}}=\langle V,\mathit{A},{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1},v_{\hskip-0.5pt\text{s}},{{\to}},{{\downarrow}}\rangle
be a
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart.

We say that
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
has the
loop existence and elimination property
(
LEE
)
if repeated loop elimination started on
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
leads to a
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart without an infinite path,
that is, if there is multi-step loop elimination reduction sequence
\underline{\mathcal{C}}\mathrel{\mbox{$\Longrightarrow$\hskip-12.80365pt\raisebox{-0.275pt}{\hbox to5.52pt{\vbox to5.52pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.76074pt\lower-2.76074pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{{{}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}
}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 M 3.54 0 C 3.54 1.96 1.96 3.54 0 3.54 C -1.96 3.54 -3.54 1.96 -3.54 0 C -3.54 -1.96 -1.96 -3.54 0 -3.54 C 1.96 -3.54 3.54 -1.96 3.54 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\hskip 7.39755pt}\hskip-1.0pt_{\text{{elim}}}^{*}}\underline{\mathcal{C}}\hskip-0.4pt^{\prime}

(or by Lemma
11
equivalently,
a single-step loop elimination reduction sequence

\underline{\mathcal{C}}\mathrel{\Rightarrow_{\text{{elim}}}^{*}}\underline{\mathcal{C}}\hskip-0.4pt^{\prime}
)
that leads to a
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart
\underline{\mathcal{C}}\hskip-0.4pt^{\prime}
without an infinite path.

If, in a successful elimination sequence from a
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
,
loop-entry transitions are never removed that depart from a vertex in the body of a previously eliminated loop sub-
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart,
then we say that
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
satisfies
layered
LEE
(
LLEE
),
and that
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
is a
LLEE
-
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart
.

{exa}

In Figure
4
we have illustrated a successful run of the loop elimination procedure for the
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
there.
The loop-entry transitions of loop sub-
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-charts that are eliminated in the next step, respectively, are marked in bold.
We have neglected action labels there, except for indicating
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-transitions by dotted arrows.
Since the graph
\underline{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime\prime\prime}
that is reached
by three loop-subgraph elimination steps
\underline{\mathcal{C}}\mathrel{\Rightarrow_{\text{{elim}}}^{+}}\underline{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime\prime\prime}
from the
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
does not have
an infinite path, and since no loop-entry transitions have been removed from a previously eliminated loop sub-
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart,
we conclude that
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
satisfies
LEE
and
LLEE
.

In Figure
5
we illustrate two runs of the loop elimination procedure from a
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart
\underline{\mathcal{E}}
:
The one from
\underline{\mathcal{E}}
to the left only witnesses
LEE
but not
LLEE
,
since in the second elimination step
a loop-entry transition (drawn
red
) is removed from the body of the loop sub-
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart that is eliminated in the first step
(drawn in
green
).
The one from
\underline{\mathcal{E}}
to the right witnesses
LLEE
, because transitions are only removed sequentially at the same vertex,
and hence no loop-entry transition is removed from the body of a loop
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart that was eliminated before.

The two process graphs
G_{1}
and
G_{2}
on page
1
, which are not expressible by star expressions modulo bisimilarity,
do not satisfy
LLEE
nor
LEE
:
neither of them has a loop subchart (as argued in Example
4
),
yet both of them facilitate infinite paths.

{defi}

[LLEE-witness]
Let
\underline{\mathcal{C}}=\langle V,\mathit{A},{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1},v_{\hskip-0.5pt\text{s}},{{\to}},{{\downarrow}}\rangle
be a
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart.

By an
entry/body-labeling
of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}

we mean a
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart
\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt=\langle V,\mathit{A}\times{\rm Nature},{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1},v_{\hskip-0.5pt\text{s}},\hat{{{\to}}},{{\downarrow}}\rangle
with actions in
\mathit{A}\times{\rm Nature}

that results from
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
by attaching to every transition of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
an additional
marking label
in
{\rm Nature}

(the transitions in
\hat{{{\to}}}
are marking-labeled versions of the transitions in
{{\to}}
).

A
LLEE
-witness
\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt
of
a
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart
\underline{\mathcal{C}}

is an entry/body-labeling of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}

that is the recording of a
LLEE
-guaranteeing,
successful run

\underline{\mathcal{C}}\mathrel{\mbox{$\Longrightarrow$\hskip-12.80365pt\raisebox{-0.275pt}{\hbox to5.52pt{\vbox to5.52pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.76074pt\lower-2.76074pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{{{}}

}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\hskip 7.39755pt}\hskip-1.0pt_{\text{{elim}}}^{*}}\underline{\mathcal{C}}\hskip-0.4pt^{\prime}

of the multi-step loop elimination procedure on
\underline{\mathcal{C}}

that results by attaching to a transition
\tau
of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
the marking label
n
for
n\in{\rm Nature}^{+}

(in pictures indicated as
{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}[n]}}
, in steps as
{{\to}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}[n]}}}}
)
forming a
loop-entry transition

if
\tau
is eliminated in the
n
-th multi-step,
and by attaching marking label
0
to all other transitions of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}

(in pictures neglected, in steps indicated as
{{\to}_{\text{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}bo}}}}
)
forming a
body transition
.

We say that a LLEE-witness
\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt
of a
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart
\underline{\mathcal{C}}

is guarded

if the action labels of the loop-entry transitions of
\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt
are proper (different from
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
). We say that a LLEE -
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
is
guarded
if
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
has a guarded LLEE-witness.

The definition above of guardedness for LLEE-witnesses is justified in view of the fact that loop-entry transitions divide infinite paths in LLEE-witnesses into finite segments
that consist only of body transitions with perhaps a leading loop-entry transition.
This is a consequence of the fact that LLEE-witnesses do not permit infinite paths of body transitions (see Lemma
13
,
2
).
Therefore guarded LLEE-witnesses, in which loop-entry transitions must be proper,
do not permit infinite paths of
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-transitions.
It also follows that the underlying
(
LLEE
-)
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart
of a guarded LLEE-witness is weakly guarded.

.

{exa}

The entry/body-labelings
\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}_{1}\hskip-0.75pt
and
\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}_{3}\hskip-0.75pt
in Figure
6
of the
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
from Figure
4

are LLEE-witnesses that arise from the successful runs of the loop elimination procedure in Example
4
:

\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}_{1}\hskip-0.75pt
is the recording on
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
of the three single-step loop eliminations
(viewed as trivial multi-steps in order to apply the clause for a LLEE-witness in Definition
4
)
that lead to
\underline{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime\prime\prime}
,
and
\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}_{3}\hskip-0.75pt
is the recording on
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
of the two multi-step loop eliminations from
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
to
\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt^{\prime\prime\prime}
.
The entry/body-labeling
\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}_{2}\hskip-0.75pt
in Figure
6

is another LLEE-witness of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
that records the successful process of four elimination steps of four loop sub-
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-charts each of which is induced by only a single loop-entry transition.
The
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
in Figure
4

has a property that does not hold in general:

\underline{\mathcal{C}}
only admits layered
LEE
-witnesses.

Indeed, this does not hold for the
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart
\underline{\mathcal{E}}
in Figure
5
:
the entry/body-labeling
\hskip 0.2pt\widehat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{E}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\hskip-0.75pt
is not a layered LLEE-witness,
because it arises from a run of the loop elimination process in which in the second step a loop-entry transition

is eliminated from the body of a loop sub-
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart that was eliminated in the first step.
But the entry/body-labeling
\hskip 0.2pt\widehat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{E}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}\hskip-0.75pt
there is a layered LLEE-witness of
\underline{\mathcal{E}}
.
The situation is analogous for the two entry/body-labelings

\hskip 0.2pt\widehat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\hskip-0.75pt
and
\hskip 0.2pt\widehat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}\hskip-0.75pt

of the slightly more involved LLEE -
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart
\underline{\mathcal{E}}_{1}
in Figure
7
,

where
\hskip 0.2pt\widehat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}\hskip-0.75pt
is a layered LLEE-witness of
\underline{\mathcal{E}}_{1}
,
but
\hskip 0.2pt\widehat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\hskip-0.75pt
is a
LEE
-witness of
\underline{\mathcal{E}}
that is not layered.

Remark 12

(from
LEE
-witnesses to LLEE-witnesses)
.

It can be shown that every
LEE
-witness that is not layered can be transformed
into a layered
LEE
-witness

(LLEE-witness) of the same underlying
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart.
Indeed, the step from the (not layered)
LEE
-witness
\hskip 0.2pt\widehat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\hskip-0.75pt

to the LLEE-witness
\hskip 0.2pt\widehat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}\hskip-0.75pt
in the example in Figure
7
,
which transfers the loop-entry transition marking label
{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}[3]}}

from the transition from
w_{1}
to
w_{2}
over to the transition from
v
to
u
,
hints at the proof of this statement.
However, we do not need this result, because
we will be able to use the guaranteed existence of LLEE-witnesses (see Theorem
4
)
for the
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart interpretation below (see Definition
4
).

For the proofs in Section
6
we will need
the ‘descends-in-loop-to’ relation
{\curvearrowright}
as defined below,
and the fact that it constitutes a ‘descent’ in a LLEE-witness.
The latter is expressed by the subsequent lemma
together with termination of the body-step relation
{{\to}_{\text{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}bo}}}}
.
Both of these properties can be established by arguing with the successful runs of the loop sub-
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart elimination procedure
that underlies a LLEE-witness.

{defi}

Let
\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt
be a LLEE-witness of a
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart
\underline{\mathcal{C}}=\langle V,\mathit{A},{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1},v_{\hskip-0.5pt\text{s}},{{\to}},{{\downarrow}}\rangle
.

Let
v,w\in V
.
We denote by
v\mathrel{{\curvearrowright}}w
, and by
w\mathrel{{\curvearrowleft}}v
,
and say that
v

descends in a loop to

w
,
if
w
is in the body of the loop sub-
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart at
v
,
which means that there is a path
v\mathrel{{{\to}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}[n]}}}}}v^{\prime}\mathrel{{{\to}^{*}_{\text{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}bo}}}}}w

from
v
via a loop-entry transition and subsequent body transitions
without
encountering
v
again.

Lemma 13
.

The relations
{\curvearrowright}
and
{{\to}_{\text{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}bo}}}}
defined by a LLEE-witness
\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt

of a
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
satisfy:

(1)

{{\curvearrowleft}^{\hskip-0.8pt+}}
is a well-founded, strict partial order on
V
.

(2)

{\leftarrow_{\text{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}bo}}}^{+}}
is a well-founded strict partial order on
V
.

Proof 4.2
.

Well-foundedness and irreflexivity of each of
{{\curvearrowleft}^{\hskip-0.8pt+}}
and
{\leftarrow_{\text{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}bo}}}^{+}}

follows from termination of
{\curvearrowright}
and
{{\to}_{\text{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}bo}}}}
, respectively.
These termination properties can be established in the same way
as for LLEE-charts without
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-transitions,
for which they follow immediately from Lemma 5.2 in
[
GF20a
,
GF20b
]
.
Since
{{\curvearrowleft}^{\hskip-0.8pt+}}
and
{\leftarrow_{\text{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}bo}}}^{+}}
are transitive by definition,
it follows that both relations are well-founded strict partial orders.

While chart interpretations of ‘
1
-free’ star expressions always satisfy
LEE
, see
[
GF20b
]
),
we observed in
[
Gra21c
]

that this is not true for the chart interpretations of star expressions in general.
As a remedy for this failure of
LEE
for chart interpretations,
we introduced ‘
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart interpretations’ of star expressions

[
Gra21c
]
.
For such
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart interpretations we showed that
LEE
is guaranteed,
and that they refine chart interpretations in the sense that there always is a functional
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-bisimulation from the
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart interpretation of a star expression
to its chart interpretation (see Theorem
4
below).
For the definition of
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart interpretations we extended the syntax of star expressions
to obtain ‘stacked star expressions’, see the definition below.
The intuition behind the use of the ‘stack product’ symbol
{\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}\varstar}

is to keep track of when a transition has descended into the body of an iteration expression
such that the iteration can be interpreted as a loop sub-
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart or a tower of nested (and possibly partially overlapping) loop sub-
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-charts.
This feature of
{\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}\varstar}
makes a transition system specification possible (see Definition
4
)
which introduces
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-transitions only as ‘backlinks’
that lead from the body (the internal vertices) of some loop sub-
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart
\underline{\mathcal{L}}
of a
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart interpretation back to the start vertex of
\underline{\mathcal{L}}
.

{defi}

[stacked star expressions]
Let
\mathit{A}
be a set whose members we call
actions
.
The set
{\mathit{StExp}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(}{\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}\varstar}{\scriptscriptstyle)}}\hskip-1.5pt}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)
of
stacked star expressions over (actions in)
\mathit{A}
is defined by the grammar:

E\;\;\>{::=}\>\;\;e\>\mid\>{E}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{e}\>\mid\>{E}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}\varstar}}{{e^{*}}}\qquad\text{(where $e\in{\textit{StExp}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)$)}\hskip 1.5pt{.}

Note that the set
{\textit{StExp}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)
of star expressions would arise if the clause
{E}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}\varstar}}{{e^{*}}}
were dropped.

The
star height

|{E}|_{\scalebox{0.8}{$*$}}
of stacked star expressions
E
is defined by adding the two clauses

|{{E}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{e}}|_{\scalebox{0.8}{$*$}}\mathrel{{:=}}\max\left\{{|{E}|_{\scalebox{0.8}{$*$}},\,|{e}|_{\scalebox{0.8}{$*$}}}\right\}
,
and

|{{E}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}\varstar}}{{e^{*}}}}|_{\scalebox{0.8}{$*$}}\mathrel{{:=}}\max\left\{{|{E}|_{\scalebox{0.8}{$*$}},\,|{{e^{*}}}|_{\scalebox{0.8}{$*$}}}\right\}

to the definition of the star height of star expressions.

The
projection function

\pi\mathrel{:}{\mathit{StExp}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(}{\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}\varstar}{\scriptscriptstyle)}}\hskip-1.5pt}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)\to{\textit{StExp}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)

is defined by interpreting
{\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}\varstar}
as
{\cdot}
by the clauses:

{\pi}(\hskip-0.5pt{{E}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{e}}\hskip-0.5pt)\mathrel{{:=}}{{\pi}(\hskip-0.5pt{E}\hskip-0.5pt)}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{e}
,

{\pi}(\hskip-0.5pt{{E}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}\varstar}}{{e^{*}}}}\hskip-0.5pt)\mathrel{{:=}}{{\pi}(\hskip-0.5pt{E}\hskip-0.5pt)}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{e^{*}}}
,

and
{\pi}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)\mathrel{{:=}}e
,
for all stacked star expressions
E\in{\mathit{StExp}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(}{\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}\varstar}{\scriptscriptstyle)}}\hskip-1.5pt}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)
, and star expressions
e\in{\textit{StExp}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)
.

In line with
[
Gra21c
]
we introduce the
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart interpretation of a star expression
e

with notation
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)
as ‘the
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart induced by (the process interpretation of)
e
’.
For understanding the TSS in its definition below
it is key to note that, by the rules for iterations, the stacked product operation
{\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}\varstar}

helps to record that an expression has descended from the iteration expression on the right-hand side of
{\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}\varstar}
.
This feature is used by the rule for stacked product to introduce
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-transitions only as backlinks to expressions from which they have descended.
The rules for iteration expressions define loop-entry transitions and body transitions, respectively,
dependent on whether
e
is ‘strongly normed’ (symbolically denoted by
{{\textit{nd}^{+}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)
)
in the sense of facilitating a process trace to termination,
and hence dependent on whether an iteration induces a loop sub-
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart (outside of inner loop sub-
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-charts).

{defi}

[
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart interpretation of star expressions]
By the
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart interpretation

{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)
of a star expression
e

we mean the
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart that arises together with the entry/body-labeling
{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)

as the
e
-rooted labeled transition system with
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-transitions (
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-LTS) generated by
\left\{{e}\right\}

according to the following TSS on the set
\mathit{StExp}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(}{\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}\varstar}{\scriptscriptstyle)}}
of stacked star expressions,
where
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}l}}\in\left\{{\text{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}bo}}}\right\}\cup\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}[{n}]}}}\mathrel{|}{n\in{\rm Nature}^{+}}\right\}

are marking labels:

\begin{gathered}\begin{aligned} &\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 14.57222pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\phantom{a_{i}\>\mathrel{{\xrightarrow{a_{i}}}{{}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}\text{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}bo}}}}}}\>1}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox The condition
{{\textit{nd}^{+}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)
means a strengthening of normedness, namely, that

permits a positive length path to an expression
f
with
{f}{{\downarrow}}\,
;
it is definable by induction.
Immediate termination for expressions of
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)
is defined by the same rules
as in Definition

(for star expressions only, preventing immediate termination for expressions with stacked product
{\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}\varstar}

We note that finiteness of

-chart is guaranteed by Theorem

(ii)
, below.

Lemma 14
.

The action 1-derivatives
{\textit{\lx@text@underline{A\hskip-0.25pt$\partial$}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{E}\hskip-0.5pt)
of a stacked star expression
E
over actions in
\mathit{A}

satisfy the following recursive equations, for all
a\in\mathit{A}
,
e,e_{1},e_{2}\in{\textit{StExp}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)
,
and stacked star expressions
E_{1}
over actions in
\mathit{A}
:

\displaystyle{\textit{\lx@text@underline{A\hskip-0.25pt$\partial$}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{0}\hskip-0.5pt)

\displaystyle{}\mathrel{{:=}}{\textit{\lx@text@underline{A\hskip-0.25pt$\partial$}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{1}\hskip-0.5pt)\mathrel{{:=}}\varnothing\hskip 1.5pt{,}

\displaystyle{\textit{\lx@text@underline{A\hskip-0.25pt$\partial$}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{a}\hskip-0.5pt)

\displaystyle{}\mathrel{{:=}}\left\{{\langle a,\hskip 0.5pt1\rangle}\right\}\hskip 1.5pt{,}

\displaystyle{\textit{\lx@text@underline{A\hskip-0.25pt$\partial$}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e_{1}+e_{2}}\hskip-0.5pt)

\displaystyle{}\mathrel{{:=}}{\textit{\lx@text@underline{A\hskip-0.25pt$\partial$}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e_{1}}\hskip-0.5pt)\cup{\textit{\lx@text@underline{A\hskip-0.25pt$\partial$}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e_{2}}\hskip-0.5pt)\hskip 1.5pt{,}

\displaystyle{\textit{\lx@text@underline{A\hskip-0.25pt$\partial$}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{E_{1}\,{{\cdot}}\,e_{2}}\hskip-0.5pt)

\displaystyle{}\mathrel{{:=}}\begin{cases}\bigl\{{\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{a}},\hskip 0.5ptE^{\prime}_{1}\,{{\cdot}}\,e_{2}\rangle}\mathrel{\big|}{\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{a}},\hskip 0.5ptE^{\prime}_{1}\rangle\in{\textit{\lx@text@underline{A\hskip-0.25pt$\partial$}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{E_{1}}\hskip-0.5pt)}\bigr\}&\text{if ${E_{1}}{\ndownarrow}$,}\\
\bigl\{{\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{a}},\hskip 0.5ptE^{\prime}_{1}\,{{\cdot}}\,e_{2}\rangle}\mathrel{\big|}{\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{a}},\hskip 0.5ptE^{\prime}_{1}\rangle\in{\textit{\lx@text@underline{A\hskip-0.25pt$\partial$}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{E_{1}}\hskip-0.5pt)}\bigr\}\cup{\textit{\lx@text@underline{A\hskip-0.25pt$\partial$}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{E_{2}}\hskip-0.5pt)&\text{if ${E_{1}}{{\downarrow}}$,}\end{cases}

\displaystyle{\textit{\lx@text@underline{A\hskip-0.25pt$\partial$}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{E_{1}\,{{\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}\varstar}}\,e^{*}_{2}}\hskip-0.5pt)

\displaystyle{}\mathrel{{:=}}\begin{cases}\bigl\{{\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{a}},\hskip 0.5ptE^{\prime}_{1}\,{{\cdot}}\,e^{*}_{2}\rangle}\mathrel{\big|}{\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{a}},\hskip 0.5ptE^{\prime}_{1}\rangle\in{\textit{\lx@text@underline{A\hskip-0.25pt$\partial$}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{E_{1}}\hskip-0.5pt)}\bigr\}&\text{if ${E_{1}}{\ndownarrow}$,}\\
\bigl\{{\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{a}},\hskip 0.5ptE^{\prime}_{1}\,{{\cdot}}\,e^{*}_{2}\rangle}\mathrel{\big|}{\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{a}},\hskip 0.5ptE^{\prime}_{1}\rangle\in{\textit{\lx@text@underline{A\hskip-0.25pt$\partial$}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{E_{1}}\hskip-0.5pt)}\bigr\}\cup\left\{{\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1},\hskip 0.5pte^{*}_{2}\rangle}\right\}&\text{if ${E_{1}}{{\downarrow}}$,}\end{cases}

\displaystyle{\textit{\lx@text@underline{A\hskip-0.25pt$\partial$}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e^{*}}\hskip-0.5pt)

\displaystyle{}\mathrel{{:=}}\bigl\{{\langle a,\hskip 0.5ptE^{\prime}\,{{\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}\varstar}}\,e^{*}\rangle}\mathrel{\big|}{\langle a,\hskip 0.5ptE^{\prime}\rangle\in{\textit{\lx@text@underline{A\hskip-0.25pt$\partial$}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)}\bigr\}\hskip 1.5pt{.}

By case-wise inspection of the definition of the TSS in Def.

{thmC}

[
[
Gra20
,

]
]
For every

for the concepts as introduced in Definition

(i)

The entry/body-labeling
{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)

{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)

is a guarded LLEE-witness of
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)
.

(ii)

The projection function
\pi
defines a
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-bisimulation from the
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart interpretation
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)

to the chart interpretation
{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)
of

,
that is symbolically,
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)\hskip-1.5pt\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\hbox{\kern 0.21529pt\vbox{\hbox{\raise 0.43057pt\hbox{\kern-0.43057pt{$\rightarrow$}\kern-0.43057pt}}\hrule}\kern 0.43057pt}}_{\pi}}}{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)

and hence also
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\hbox{\kern 0.21529pt\vbox{\hbox{\raise 0.43057pt\hbox{\kern-0.43057pt{{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}$\rightarrow$}}\kern-0.43057pt}}\hrule}\kern 0.43057pt}}}{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)
.
Since the set of stacked star expressions that form the pre-image of a star expression under the projection function is always finite,
it follows that
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart interpretations of star expressions are always finite as well.

For the proof of Lemma

Its proof uses the first lemma, which crucially states that every star expression

-provably be written as a star expression

Proof 4.5
.

We establish the lemma by induction on the star height
|{E}|_{\scalebox{0.8}{$*$}}
of
E

with a subinduction on the syntactical structure of
E
.
All cases of stacked star expressions can be dealt with in a quite straightforward manner,
except for the case of star expressions with an outermost iteration.
There, an appeal to Lemma
15
is crucial.
We treat this case in detail below.

Suppose that
E\mathrel{{\equiv}}e^{*}
for some star expression
e\in{\textit{StExp}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)
(without occurrences of stacked product
{\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}\varstar}

in

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:1:lem:FT:onechart-int}{T_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)=\bigl\{{e\mathrel{{\xrightarrow{a_{i}}}}E^{\prime}_{i}}\mathrel{\big|}{i=1,\ldots n}\bigr\}\hskip 1.5pt{,}

for some stacked star expression

Then it follows, again from the TSS in Definition

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:3:lem:FT:onechart-int}{T_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{E}\hskip-0.5pt)}}(\hskip-0.5pt{E}\hskip-0.5pt)={T_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e^{*}}\hskip-0.5pt)}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e^{*}}\hskip-0.5pt)=\bigl\{{e^{*}\mathrel{{\xrightarrow{a_{i}}}}E^{\prime}_{i}\,{{\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}\varstar}}\,e^{*}}\mathrel{\big|}{i=1,\ldots n}\bigr\}\hskip 1.5pt{.}

(5)

We assume now that
{e}{{\downarrow}}
holds. (We will see that if

holds, the argumentation below becomes easier).
Then by Lemma

there is

From the latter it follows with (

):

\displaystyle{T_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{f}\hskip-0.5pt)}}(\hskip-0.5pt{f}\hskip-0.5pt)

\displaystyle{}=\bigl\{{f\mathrel{{\xrightarrow{a_{i}}}}F^{\prime}_{i}}\mathrel{\big|}{i=1,\ldots n}\bigr\}\hskip 1.5pt{,}

(6)

\displaystyle{\pi}(\hskip-0.5pt{F^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-0.5pt)

\displaystyle{}={\pi}(\hskip-0.5pt{E^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-0.5pt)\qquad\text{(for all $i=1,\ldots n$)}\hskip 1.5pt{,}

(7)

for some stacked star expression
F^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,F^{\prime}_{n}
, which are 1-derivatives of

.
Note again that only proper transitions can depart from the star expression

according to Definition

.
On the basis of these assumptions we can now argue as follows:

\displaystyle{\pi}(\hskip-0.5pt{E}\hskip-0.5pt)

\displaystyle\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{\equiv}}$}\>{\pi}(\hskip-0.5pt{e^{*}}\hskip-0.5pt)

\displaystyle\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{\equiv}}$}\>e^{*}

\displaystyle\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}$}\>(1+f)^{*}
