\displaystyle\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}$}\>f^{*}

{\text{trm-body}}(\hskip-0.5pt{{}^{*}}\hskip-0.5pt)
))

\displaystyle\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}$}\>1+f\,{{\cdot}}\,f^{*}

(by axiom (
{\text{rec}}(\hskip-0.5pt{{}^{*}}\hskip-0.5pt)
))

\displaystyle\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}$}\>1+f\,{{\cdot}}\,(1+f)^{*}

(by axiom (
{\text{trm-body}}(\hskip-0.5pt{{}^{*}}\hskip-0.5pt)
))

\displaystyle\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}$}\>1+f\,{{\cdot}}\,e^{*}

(by the choice of
f
with
1+f\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}e
)

\displaystyle\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}$}\>1+\bigl({\tau_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{f}\hskip-0.5pt)}}(\hskip-0.5pt{f}\hskip-0.5pt)+\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}\,{{\cdot}}\,{\pi}(\hskip-0.5pt{F^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-0.5pt)\bigr)\,{{\cdot}}\,e^{*}

(by the induction hypothesis, due to

(
|{f}|_{\scalebox{0.8}{$*$}}=|{e}|_{\scalebox{0.8}{$*$}}<|{e}|_{\scalebox{0.8}{$*$}}+1=|{e^{*}}|_{\scalebox{0.8}{$*$}}=|{E}|_{\scalebox{0.8}{$*$}}
,

(

and in view of (
6
))

\displaystyle\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{\equiv}}$}\>1+\bigl(0+\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}\,{{\cdot}}\,{\pi}(\hskip-0.5pt{E^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-0.5pt)\bigr)\,{{\cdot}}\,e^{*}

(by
{\tau_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{f}\hskip-0.5pt)}}(\hskip-0.5pt{f}\hskip-0.5pt)\mathrel{{\equiv}}0
due to
{f}{\ndownarrow}

and by using (
7
))

\displaystyle\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}$}\>{\tau_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e^{*}}\hskip-0.5pt)}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e^{*}}\hskip-0.5pt)+\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}\,{{\cdot}}\,({\pi}(\hskip-0.5pt{E^{\prime}_{i}}\hskip-0.5pt)\,{{\cdot}}\,e^{*})

(by axioms (
{\text{neutr}}(\hskip-0.5pt{+}\hskip-0.5pt)
), (
{\text{r-distr}}(\hskip-0.5pt{+,{\cdot}}\hskip-0.5pt)
),

(
{\text{assoc}}(\hskip-0.5pt{{\cdot}}\hskip-0.5pt)
),
{\tau_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e^{*}}\hskip-0.5pt)}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e^{*}}\hskip-0.5pt)\mathrel{{\equiv}}1
due to
{(e^{*})}{{\downarrow}}

\displaystyle\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{\equiv}}$}\>{\tau_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{E}\hskip-0.5pt)}}(\hskip-0.5pt{E}\hskip-0.5pt)+\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}\,{{\cdot}}\,{\pi}(\hskip-0.5pt{E^{\prime}_{i}\,{{\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}\varstar}}\,e^{*}}\hskip-0.5pt)

(by the definition of the projection
\pi
).

In view of (
5
),
this chain of
Mil
-
-provable equalities verifies the statement in the lemma
in this case
E=e^{*}
with
{e}{{\downarrow}}
.
If
{e}{\ndownarrow}
holds, then the detour via
f
is not necessary,
and the argument is much simpler. The statement of the lemma holds true then as well.

Lemma 17
.

For every star expression
e\in{\textit{StExp}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)

with
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart interpretation
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)=\langle{V\hskip-1.0pt}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt),\mathit{A},{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1},e,{{\to}},{{\downarrow}}\rangle

the star-expression function
s\mathrel{:}{V\hskip-1.0pt}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)\to{\textit{StExp}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)
,
E\mapsto{\pi}(\hskip-0.5pt{E}\hskip-0.5pt)

is a
Mil
-
-provable solution of
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)
with principal value
e
.

Proof 4.6
.

The statement of the lemma is an immediate consequence of Lemma
16
.

5.
Coinductive version of Milner’s proof system

In this section we motivate and define ‘coinductive proofs’, introduce coinductive versions of Milner’s system
Mil
,
and establish first interconnections between these proof systems.

\underline{\mathcal{C}}
{{{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e_{1}}\hskip-0.5pt)}/_{{=_{\text{\sf Mil}}\hskip 1.0pt}}}
{{{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e_{2}}\hskip-0.5pt)}/_{{=_{\text{\sf Mil}}\hskip 1.0pt}}}
LLEE
, guarded
\left[{L_{1}}\right]_{{=_{\text{\sf Mil}}\hskip 1.0pt}}
\left[{L_{2}}\right]_{{=_{\text{\sf Mil}}\hskip 1.0pt}}

Figure 8.

A coinductive proof
\langle\underline{\mathcal{C}},\hskip 0.5ptL\rangle
over
Mil
of
e_{1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}e_{2}

gives rise to functional
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-bisimulations to factor charts of
{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e_{1}}\hskip-0.5pt)
and
{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e_{2}}\hskip-0.5pt)

with respect to provability in
Mil
.

As the central concept we now introduce ‘coinductive proofs’ over
\mathcal{E\hskip-1.0ptL}
-based proof systems
{\mathcal{S}}
.
We have seen examples for such circular deductions earlier in Figure
1
and Figure
2
.
We define a coinductive proof over
{\mathcal{S}}
as a weakly guarded
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart
\underline{\mathcal{C}}

whose vertices are labeled by equations between star expressions
such that the left-, and the right-hand sides of the equations
form
{\mathcal{S}}
-provable solutions of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
.
The conclusion of such a proof is the equation that labels the start vertex of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
.
If
{\mathcal{S}}
is theorem-subsumed by
Mil
(formally,
{\mathcal{S}}\mathrel{{\precsim}}\text{\sf Mil}
holds),
then a coinductive proof with
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
, conclusion
e_{1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}e_{2}
,
and left- and right-hand side labeling functions
L_{1}
and
L_{2}

can be viewed, due to Proposition
10
,
as a pair of
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-bisimulations defined by
\left[{L_{1}}\right]_{{=_{\text{\sf Mil}}\hskip 1.0pt}}
and by
\left[{L_{2}}\right]_{{=_{\text{\sf Mil}}\hskip 1.0pt}}

from
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
to
{{{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e_{1}}\hskip-0.5pt)}/_{{=_{\text{\sf Mil}}\hskip 1.0pt}}}
, and to
{{{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e_{2}}\hskip-0.5pt)}/_{{=_{\text{\sf Mil}}\hskip 1.0pt}}}

(see Figure
8
).
In this case we can show that the conclusion
e_{1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}e_{2}
of the coinductive proof is semantically sound
(see Proposition
20
).
Indeed a stronger statement holds, and its proof will form the central part of Section
6
:
if
{\mathcal{S}}\mathrel{{\precsim}}\text{\sf Mil}
holds,
and the underlying chart of the coinductive proof of
e_{1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}e_{2}
over
{\mathcal{S}}
is a LLEE -
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart,
then that proof can be transformed into a proof of
e_{1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}e_{2}
in Milner’s system
Mil

(see Proposition
29
in the next section).

In order to guarantee that coinductive proofs over a proof system
{\mathcal{S}}
can only derive semantically valid equations,
it is necessary to demand that
{\mathcal{S}}
is sound for process-semantics equality
{=_{\scriptscriptstyle\llbracket{\cdot}\rrbracket_{\hskip-0.5ptP}}}

(for example if
{\mathcal{S}}\mathrel{{\precsim}}\text{\sf Mil}
, using Proposition
2
).
This notwithstanding, we do not include this requirement in the definition below, but add it later to statements when it is needed.
The reason is that in Section
7

we want to be able to show (see Lemma
36
) that even instances of the fixed-point rule
\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt
with premises that are not semantically valid
can be mimicked by coinductive proofs over appropriate proof systems that are unsound.

{defi}

[(
LLEE
-witnessed) coinductive proofs]
Let
\mathit{A}
be a set of actions.
Let
{\mathcal{S}}
be an
{\text{$\mathcal{E\hskip-1.0ptL}$}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)
-based proof system with
\text{\sf ACI}\mathrel{{\sqsubseteq}}{\mathcal{S}}
. Let
e_{1},e_{2}\in{\textit{StExp}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)
be star expressions.

By a
coinductive proof over
{\mathcal{S}}
of
e_{1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}e_{2}

we mean a pair
\mathcal{C\hskip-0.75ptP}=\langle\underline{\mathcal{C}},\hskip 0.5ptL\rangle

that consists of
a weakly guarded
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart
\underline{\mathcal{C}}=\langle V,\mathit{A},{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1},v_{\hskip-0.5pt\text{s}},{{\to}},{{\downarrow}}\rangle
,
and
a labeling function
L\mathrel{:}V\to{\textit{Eq}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)
of vertices of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
by formal equations over
\mathit{A}

such that for the functions

L_{1},L_{2}\mathrel{:}V\to{\textit{StExp}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)

that denote the star expressions
{L_{1}}(\hskip-0.5pt{v}\hskip-0.5pt)
, and
{L_{2}}(\hskip-0.5pt{v}\hskip-0.5pt)
,
on the left-, and on the right-hand side of the equation
{L}(\hskip-0.5pt{v}\hskip-0.5pt)
, respectively, the following conditions hold:

(cp1)

L_{1}
and
L_{2}
are
{\mathcal{S}}
-provable solutions of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
,

(cp2)

e_{1}\mathrel{{\equiv}}{L_{1}}(\hskip-0.5pt{v_{\hskip-0.5pt\text{s}}}\hskip-0.5pt)
and
e_{2}\mathrel{{\equiv}}{L_{2}}(\hskip-0.5pt{v_{\hskip-0.5pt\text{s}}}\hskip-0.5pt)

(
e_{1}
and
e_{2}
are principal values of
L_{1}
and
L_{2}
, respectively).

We denote by
e_{1}\mathrel{{\overset{\text{coind}}{=\joinrel=\joinrel=}_{{\mathcal{S}}}}}e_{2}

that there is a coinductive proof over
{\mathcal{S}}
of
e_{1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}e_{2}
.

By a
LLEE
-witnessed coinductive proof
over
{\mathcal{S}}
we mean a coinductive proof
\mathcal{C\hskip-0.75ptP}=\langle\underline{\mathcal{C}},\hskip 0.5ptL\rangle

where
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
is a guarded LLEE -
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart.
We denote by
e_{1}\mathrel{{\overset{\text{\scriptsize LLEE}}{=\joinrel=\joinrel=}_{{\mathcal{S}}}}}e_{2}

that there is a LLEE-witnessed coinductive proof over
{\mathcal{S}}
of
e_{1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}e_{2}
.

While the restriction to guardedness of the LLEE -
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart underlying LLEE-witnessed coinductive proofs could be relaxed to weak guardedness,
we have required guardedness in this definition in order to (somewhat) reduce technicalities in the proofs in Section
6
.

We provide two examples of LLEE-witnessed coinductive proofs. First we develop a new one, and
then we revisit and justify the example in Figure
1
from the introduction.

{exa}

In Figure
9
we have illustrated
a LLEE-witnessed coinductive proof over
Mil
-

of the statement

{({{a^{*}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{b^{*}}})^{*}}\mathrel{{\overset{\text{\scriptsize LLEE}}{=\joinrel=\joinrel=}_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}}}{({a}+{b})^{*}}
.
Formally this proof is of the form
\mathcal{C\hskip-0.75ptP}=\langle\underline{\mathcal{C}},\hskip 0.5ptL\rangle

where
\underline{\mathcal{C}}={\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{(a^{*}\,{{\cdot}}\,b^{*})^{*}}\hskip-0.5pt)

has the guarded LLEE-witness
{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}(\hskip-0.5pt{(a^{*}\,{{\cdot}}\,b^{*})^{*}}\hskip-0.5pt)
(see Theorem
4
) as indicated in Figure

where framed boxes contain vertex names.

In this illustration we have drawn the
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
that carries the equations with its start vertex below
in order to adhere to the prooftree intuition for the represented derivation, namely with the conclusion at the bottom.
We will do so repeatedly also below.
Solution correctness for the left-hand sides
L_{1}
of the equations
L
on
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
in Figure

follow from Lemma
17
, because
\underline{\mathcal{C}}={\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{(a^{*}\,{{\cdot}}\,b^{*})^{*}}\hskip-0.5pt)

where

is the left-hand side of the conclusion.
This notwithstanding, below we verify the correctness conditions in

for the left-hand side

and the right-hand side
L_{2}

of the equation labeling function

for the (most involved) case of vertex

{({({1}\hskip 1.0pt{{\cdot}}\hskip 1.0pt{{a^{*}}})}\hskip 1.0pt{{\cdot}}\hskip 1.0pt{{b^{*}}})}\hskip 1.0pt{{\cdot}}\hskip 1.0pt{{({{a^{*}}}\hskip 1.0pt{{\cdot}}\hskip 1.0pt{{b^{*}}})^{*}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}{1}\hskip 1.0pt{{\cdot}}\hskip 1.0pt{{({a}+{b})^{*}}}

{({{a^{*}}}\hskip 1.0pt{{\cdot}}\hskip 1.0pt{{b^{*}}})}\hskip 1.0pt{{\cdot}}\hskip 1.0pt{{({{a^{*}}}\hskip 1.0pt{{\cdot}}\hskip 1.0pt{{b^{*}}})^{*}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}{({a}+{b})^{*}}
{{b^{*}}}\hskip 1.0pt{{\cdot}}\hskip 1.0pt{{({{a^{*}}}\hskip 1.0pt{{\cdot}}\hskip 1.0pt{{b^{*}}})^{*}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}{({a}+{b})^{*}}

\displaystyle{L_{1}}(\hskip-0.5pt{v_{1}}\hskip-0.5pt)

{exa}

For the statement

g^{*}\,{{\cdot}}\,0\mathrel{{\equiv}}(a+b)^{*}\,{{\cdot}}\,0\mathrel{{\overset{\text{\scriptsize LLEE}}{=\joinrel=\joinrel=}_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}}}(a\,{{\cdot}}\,(a+b)+b)^{*}\,{{\cdot}}\,0\mathrel{{\equiv}}h^{*}\,{{\cdot}}\,0
,
we illustrated in Figure
2

the coinductive proof
\mathcal{C\hskip-0.75ptP}=\langle{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{h^{*}\,{{\cdot}}\,0}\hskip-0.5pt),\hskip 0.5ptL\rangle
over
Mil
-

with underlying guarded LLEE-witness
{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}(\hskip-0.5pt{h^{*}\,{{\cdot}}\,0}\hskip-0.5pt)
,
where
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{h^{*}\,{{\cdot}}\,0}\hskip-0.5pt)
and
{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}(\hskip-0.5pt{h^{*}\,{{\cdot}}\,0}\hskip-0.5pt)

is the
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart interpretation as defined according to Definition
4
,
and the equation-labeling function
L
on
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{h^{*}\,{{\cdot}}\,0}\hskip-0.5pt)
is defined as in the figure.

The correctness conditions at the start vertex (at the bottom) can be verified as follows:

\displaystyle g^{*}\,{{\cdot}}\,0

\displaystyle{}\mathrel{\parbox{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{\equiv}}$}}(a+b)^{*}\,{{\cdot}}\,0\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}(1+(a+b)\,{{\cdot}}\,(a+b)^{*})\,{{\cdot}}\,0\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}1\,{{\cdot}}\,0+((a+b)\,{{\cdot}}\,g^{*})\,{{\cdot}}\,0

\displaystyle{}\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}0+(a\,{{\cdot}}\,g^{*}+b\,{{\cdot}}\,g^{*})\,{{\cdot}}\,0\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}(a\,{{\cdot}}\,g^{*}+b\,{{\cdot}}\,g^{*})\,{{\cdot}}\,0

\displaystyle{}\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}(a\,{{\cdot}}\,g^{*})\,{{\cdot}}\,0+(b\,{{\cdot}}\,g^{*})\,{{\cdot}}\,0\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}a\,{{\cdot}}\,(g^{*}\,{{\cdot}}\,0)+b\,{{\cdot}}\,(g^{*}\,{{\cdot}}\,0)

\displaystyle{}\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}a\,{{\cdot}}\,((1\,{{\cdot}}\,g^{*})\,{{\cdot}}\,0)+b\,{{\cdot}}\,((1\,{{\cdot}}\,g^{*})\,{{\cdot}}\,0)\hskip 1.5pt{,}

\displaystyle h^{*}\,{{\cdot}}\,0

\displaystyle{}\mathrel{\parbox{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{\equiv}}$}}(a\,{{\cdot}}\,(a+b)+b)^{*}\,{{\cdot}}\,0\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}(1+(a\,{{\cdot}}\,(a+b)+b)\,{{\cdot}}\,(a\,{{\cdot}}\,(a+b)+b)^{*})\,{{\cdot}}\,0

\displaystyle{}\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}1\,{{\cdot}}\,0+((a\,{{\cdot}}\,(a+b)+b)\,{{\cdot}}\,h^{*})\,{{\cdot}}\,0\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}0+((a\,{{\cdot}}\,(a+b))\,{{\cdot}}\,h^{*}+b\,{{\cdot}}\,h^{*})\,{{\cdot}}\,0

\displaystyle{}\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}(a\,{{\cdot}}\,((a+b)\,{{\cdot}}\,h^{*})+b\,{{\cdot}}\,h^{*})\,{{\cdot}}\,0\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}((a\,{{\cdot}}\,((a+b)\,{{\cdot}}\,h^{*}))\,{{\cdot}}\,0+(b\,{{\cdot}}\,h^{*})\,{{\cdot}}\,0

\displaystyle{}\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}a\,{{\cdot}}\,((a+b)\,{{\cdot}}\,h^{*})\,{{\cdot}}\,0)+b\,{{\cdot}}\,(h^{*}\,{{\cdot}}\,0)

\displaystyle{}\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}a\,{{\cdot}}\,((1\,{{\cdot}}\,(a+b))\,{{\cdot}}\,h^{*})\,{{\cdot}}\,0)+b\,{{\cdot}}\,((1\,{{\cdot}}\,h^{*})\,{{\cdot}}\,0)\hskip 1.5pt{.}

From the provable equality for
g^{*}\,{{\cdot}}\,0

the correctness condition for
(1\,{{\cdot}}\,g^{*})\,{{\cdot}}\,0
at the left upper vertex of
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{h^{*}\,{{\cdot}}\,0}\hskip-0.5pt)

can be obtained by additional uses of the axiom (
{\text{id}_{\text{l}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{{\cdot}}\hskip-0.5pt)
).
The correctness condition for
((1\,{{\cdot}}\,(a+b))\,{{\cdot}}\,h^{*})\,{{\cdot}}\,0
at the left upper vertex of
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{h^{*}\,{{\cdot}}\,0}\hskip-0.5pt)

can be verified as follows:

\displaystyle((1\,{{\cdot}}\,(a+b))\,{{\cdot}}\,h^{*})\,{{\cdot}}\,0

\displaystyle{}\mathrel{\parbox{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}$}}((a+b)\,{{\cdot}}\,h^{*})\,{{\cdot}}\,0\mathrel{\parbox{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}$}}(a\,{{\cdot}}\,h^{*}+b\,{{\cdot}}\,h^{*})\,{{\cdot}}\,0

\displaystyle{}\mathrel{\parbox{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}$}}(a\,{{\cdot}}\,h^{*})\,{{\cdot}}\,0+(b\,{{\cdot}}\,h^{*})\,{{\cdot}}\,0\mathrel{\parbox{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}$}}a\,{{\cdot}}\,(h^{*}\,{{\cdot}}\,0)+b\,{{\cdot}}\,(h^{*}\,{{\cdot}}\,0)

\displaystyle{}\mathrel{\parbox{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}$}}a\,{{\cdot}}\,((1\,{{\cdot}}\,h^{*})\,{{\cdot}}\,0)+b\,{{\cdot}}\,((1\,{{\cdot}}\,h^{*})\,{{\cdot}}\,0)\hskip 1.5pt{.}

Finally, the correctness conditions at the right upper vertex of
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{h^{*}\,{{\cdot}}\,0}\hskip-0.5pt)

can be obtained by applications of the axiom (
{\text{id}_{\text{l}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{{\cdot}}\hskip-0.5pt)
) only.

As a direct consequence of Definition
5
, the following lemma
states that LLEE-witnessed coinductive provability of an equation implies its coinductive provability.
The subsequent lemma states easy observations about the composition of
coinductive provability over a proof system
{\mathcal{S}}
with provability
{=_{{\mathcal{S}}}\hskip 1.0pt}
in
{\mathcal{S}}
.

Lemma 18
.

e_{1}\mathrel{{\overset{\text{\scriptsize LLEE}}{=\joinrel=\joinrel=}_{{\mathcal{S}}}}}e_{2}

implies

e_{1}\mathrel{{\overset{\text{coind}}{=\joinrel=\joinrel=}_{{\mathcal{S}}}}}e_{2}
,
for all
e_{1},e_{2}\in{\textit{StExp}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)
, where
{\mathcal{S}}
is an
\mathcal{E\hskip-1.0ptL}
-based proof system over
{\textit{StExp}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)

with
\text{\sf ACI}\mathrel{{\sqsubseteq}}{\mathcal{S}}
that is sound with respect to
\mathrel{{=_{\scriptscriptstyle\llbracket{\cdot}\rrbracket_{\hskip-0.5ptP}}}}
.

Lemma 19
.

Let
{R}\in\bigl\{{{\overset{\text{coind}}{=\joinrel=\joinrel=}_{{\mathcal{S}}}},{\overset{\text{\scriptsize LLEE}}{=\joinrel=\joinrel=}_{{\mathcal{S}}}}}\bigr\}

for some
\mathcal{E\hskip-1.0ptL}
-based proof system
{\mathcal{S}}
with
\text{\sf ACI}\mathrel{{\sqsubseteq}}{\mathcal{S}}
.
Then
\mathrel{{R}}
is reflexive, symmetric, and
satisfies
{=_{{\mathcal{S}}}\hskip 1.0pt}\circ{R}\subseteq{R}
,

{R}\circ{=_{{\mathcal{S}}}\hskip 1.0pt}\subseteq{R}
, and

{=_{{\mathcal{S}}}\hskip 1.0pt}\subseteq{R}
.

The proposition below, and the subsequent remark
are evidence for the fact, mentioned at the start of this section,
that coinductive proofs over proof systems that are sound with respect to
{=_{\scriptscriptstyle\llbracket{\cdot}\rrbracket_{\hskip-0.5ptP}}}

derive semantically valid conclusions themselves.
Rather than formulating their statements for all semantically sound proof systems,
we restrict our attention to systems that are theorem-subsumed by
Mil
.

Proposition 20
.

Let
{\mathcal{S}}
be an
\mathcal{E\hskip-1.0ptL}
-based proof system over
{\textit{StExp}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)

(8)

That is, if there is a coinductive proof over

then the chart interpretations of
e_{1}
and

are bisimilar.

\underline{\mathcal{C}}
{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e_{1}}\hskip-0.5pt)
{{{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e_{1}}\hskip-0.5pt)}/_{\mathrel{{=_{\text{\sf Mil}}\hskip 1.0pt}}}}
{{{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e_{2}}\hskip-0.5pt)}/_{\mathrel{{=_{\text{\sf Mil}}\hskip 1.0pt}}}}
{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e_{2}}\hskip-0.5pt)
LLEE
, guarded
(by Lem.

)
\left[{L_{1}}\right]_{{=_{\text{\sf Mil}}\hskip 1.0pt}}
\left[{L_{2}}\right]_{{=_{\text{\sf Mil}}\hskip 1.0pt}}
(by Lem.

)
\left.\parbox{0.0pt}{{\large\hskip 0.0pt$\langle\underline{\mathcal{C}},\hskip 0.5ptL\rangle$\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{}}

Proof 5.1
.

We have illustrated the proof of this proposition in Figure
10
:
In every coinductive proof
\langle\underline{\mathcal{C}},\hskip 0.5ptL\rangle
over
{\mathcal{S}}
with
{\mathcal{S}}\mathrel{{\precsim}}\text{\sf Mil}
of an equation

the star-expression functions
L_{1}
and
L_{2}
are

-provable solutions of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
.
Then by using Proposition

and Lemma

we get the link of functional (
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-)bisimulations between
{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e_{1}}\hskip-0.5pt)
and
{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e_{2}}\hskip-0.5pt)

as drawn in that picture.
Since (functional) bisimulations compose with (functional)

-bisimulations to
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-bisimulations,
and
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-bisimulations between charts are bisimulations,
we obtain that
{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e_{1}}\hskip-0.5pt)\mathrel{\hbox{\kern 0.43057pt\vbox{\hbox{\raise 0.43057pt\hbox{\kern-0.43057pt{$\leftrightarrow$}\kern-0.43057pt}}\hrule}\kern 0.43057pt}\hskip 1.0pt}{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e_{2}}\hskip-0.5pt)
holds, and consequently, that
e_{1}\mathrel{{=_{\scriptscriptstyle\llbracket{\cdot}\rrbracket_{\hskip-0.5ptP}}}}e_{2}
holds.

We now define two proof systems
CLC
and
CC
for combining LLEE-witnessed coinductive provability.
Each of these systems consists of a single rule scheme, a more specific one for
CLC
, and a more liberal one for
CC
.
Instances of rules of these two schemes formalize LLEE-witnessed coinductive provability in
CLC
,
and respectively coinductive provability in
CC
,
of equations between star expressions from assumed equations.
Different from the exposition in
[
Gra21a
,
Gra21b
]
,
where we permitted entire coinductive proofs as formulas and as premises of rules,
we here keep the proof systems
{\textit{Eq}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)
-based
by externalizing coinductive proofs from the rules by ‘hiding’ them in side-conditions.
2
2

2

Keeping the systems equation-based (by avoiding coinductive proofs as formulas as in
[
Gra21a
,
Gra21b
]
)
permits us to then compare the coinductive proof systems
CLC
,
CC
, and later
cMil
, and
\overline{\text{\sf cMil}}
via rule derivability and admissibility to Milner’s system
Mil
and its variants
Mil
′
, and
\overline{\sf Mil}{}^{\prime}
.

The more restricted proof system
CLC
will form the core of our coinductive reformulation of Milner’s system.

Lemma 22
.

The following subsystem relationships hold
between the coinductive proof systems defined above:
{enumerate*}
[label=()]

\text{\sf C\hskip-0.5ptL\hskip-0.5ptC}\mathrel{{\sqsubseteq}}\text{\sf cMil}
,

{\text{{\sf cMil}${}_{1}$}}\mathrel{{\sqsubseteq}}\text{\sf cMil}
,

\text{\sf C\hskip-0.75ptC}\mathrel{{\sqsubseteq}}\text{$\overline{\text{\sf cMil}}$}
.

Lemma 23
.

The following theorem subsumption and theorem equivalence statements hold:

(i)

\text{\sf C\hskip-0.5ptL\hskip-0.5ptC}\mathrel{{\precsim}}\text{\sf C\hskip-0.75ptC}
,

(ii)

{\text{{\sf cMil}${}_{1}$}}\mathrel{{\precsim}}\text{\sf cMil}\mathrel{{\precsim}}\text{$\overline{\text{\sf cMil}}$}
,

(iii)

\text{\sf C\hskip-0.5ptL\hskip-0.5ptC}\mathrel{\sim}\text{\sf cMil}
,

(iv)

\text{\sf C\hskip-0.75ptC}\mathrel{\sim}\text{$\overline{\text{\sf cMil}}$}
.

Proof 5.2
.

We have argued for statement

above, below Definition
5
:
every instance of the rule

of

of

of an equation

, can be replaced by an instance of

with as result a derivation

.
The part

by Lemma

,

.

For statement

,

\text{\sf C\hskip-0.5ptL\hskip-0.5ptC}\mathrel{{\sqsubseteq}}\text{\sf cMil}

see Lemma

,

.
For showing the converse implication,
\text{\sf C\hskip-0.5ptL\hskip-0.5ptC}\mathrel{{\succsim}}\text{\sf cMil}
,
we will demonstrate the proof-transformation statement (
12
) below
by first showing its special case (
13
):

\displaystyle\left.\;\parbox{284.62395pt}{Every derivation $\mathcal{D}$ in \text{\sf cMil}\ can be transformed into a derivation $\mathcal{D}^{\prime}$ in \text{\sf C\hskip-0.5ptL\hskip-0.5ptC}\ \\
with the same conclusion.}\;\right\}

(12)

\displaystyle\left.\;\parbox{284.62395pt}{The transformation statement \eqref{eq:1:prf:it:3:lem:thmsubsumption:rels:coindproofsystems}
holds for every derivation $\mathcal{D}$ in \text{\sf cMil}\ with an instance of $\text{LCoProof${}_{n}$}$, for some $n\in{\rm Nature}$, at the bottom.}\;\right\}

(13)

The idea for both of these proof transformation statements
is to ‘hide’ derivation parts that consist of axioms and rules in
Mil
-
into the correctness statements of coinductive proofs that appear as side-conditions in instances of the coinductive rule in
CLC
.
More precisely, the idea is to replace derivation parts
\mathcal{D}_{0}
in
Mil
-
of a derivation
\mathcal{D}
in
cMil
,
where
\mathcal{D}_{0}
consists of the inference in
Mil
-
of an equation
g\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}h

from a set
\Gamma
of
m
assumption equations,
by an instance of the coinductive rule
LCoProof
m
that has the
m
assumptions of
\mathcal{D}_{0}
in
\Gamma
as its premises.
Then whenever
g\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}h
is needed to justify a correctness conditions
it can be reconstructed from the premises, provably in
Mil
-
, on the basis of the derivation
\mathcal{D}_{0}
in
Mil
-
.

We start by showing (
13
),
and proceed for this purpose by induction on the depth
0pt{\mathcal{D}}
of
\mathcal{D}
.
Suppose that
\mathcal{D}
is a derivation in
cMil
with an occurrence of an instance
\iota
of
LCoProof
n
at the bottom.
To perform the induction step for
\mathcal{D}
,
we need to transform
\mathcal{D}
into a derivation
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
in
CLC
with the same conclusion.
We may assume that the immediate subderivations
\mathcal{D}_{1}
, …,
\mathcal{D}_{n}
of
\mathcal{D}
(just above the instance
\iota
)
contain axioms and/or rules of
Mil
-
, because otherwise
\mathcal{D}
is already a derivation in
CLC
.
To keep the illustration of the transformation step simple,
we assume that only the
i
-th subderivation
\mathcal{D}_{i}
contains axioms and/or rules of
Mil
;
the general case will become clear through this example.
We assume that
\mathcal{D}
is of the form:

\mathcal{D}_{1}

g_{1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}h_{1}

\mathcal{D}_{i1}

(g_{i1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}h_{i1})

\;\cdots\;

\mathcal{D}_{im}

(g_{im}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}h_{im})

\mathcal{D}_{i0}

\ldots\hskip 12.91663ptg_{i}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}h_{i}\hskip 12.91663pt\ldots

\mathcal{D}_{n}

g_{n}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}h_{n}

LCoProof
n

e\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}f

(14)

\displaystyle\text{with side-con\-di\-tion}\;\;e\mathrel{{\overset{\text{\scriptsize LLEE}}{=\joinrel=\joinrel=}_{{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}{+}{\Gamma}}}}f\;\;\text{for $\Gamma=\left\{{g_{1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}h_{1},\ldots g_{n}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}h_{n}}\right\}$$\hskip 1.5pt{,}$}

(15)

where
\mathcal{D}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{D}_{i-1},\mathcal{D}_{i+1},\ldots\mathcal{D}_{n}
are already derivations in
CLC
(with bottommost instances of
LCoProof
{}_{n_{i}}
that are suggested by dashed lines),
but
\mathcal{D}_{i}
contains axioms and/or rule instances of
Mil
-
,
and can be construed with a bottom part
\mathcal{D}_{i0}
in
Mil
-
below
m
conclusions

g_{i1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}h_{i1}
, …,
g_{im}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}h_{im}

of instances of coinductive rules from
\left\{{\text{LCoProof${}_{j}$}}\right\}_{j\in{\rm Nature}}
.

Then we apply the induction hypothesis to the subderivations
\mathcal{D}_{i1},\ldots,\mathcal{D}_{im}
of
\mathcal{D}_{0}
,
which is possible due to
0pt{\mathcal{D}_{i1}},\ldots,0pt{\mathcal{D}_{im}}<0pt{\mathcal{D}_{i}}<0pt{\mathcal{D}}
,
to obtain derivations
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{i1},\ldots,\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{im}
in
CLC
with the same conclusions

g_{i1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}h_{i1}
, …,
g_{im}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}h_{im}
, respectively.
Then we transform
\mathcal{D}

by replacing the instance of
LCoProof
n
at the bottom
by an instance of
LCoProof
n+m-1
,
keeping the first
i-1
and the last
n+1-i
premises and their subderivations,
but replacing the
i
-th premise and its immediate subderivation
\mathcal{D}_{i}

by
m
additional premises with immediate subderivations
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{i1}
, …,
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{im}
,
thereby obtaining:

\mathcal{D}_{1}

g_{1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}h_{1}

\;\;\ldots\;\;

\mathord{}\mathord{}
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{i1}

\mathord{g_{i1}}\mathord{}
{}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}h_{i1}

\;\;\ldots\;\;

\mathord{}\mathord{}
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{im}

\mathord{g_{im}}\mathord{}
{}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}h_{im}

\;\;\ldots\;\;

\mathcal{D}_{n}

g_{n}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}h_{n}

LCoProof
n+m-1

e\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}f

(16)

However, we need to show the side-condition for the displayed instance of
LCoProof
n+m-1
:

e\mathrel{{\overset{\text{\scriptsize LLEE}}{=\joinrel=\joinrel=}_{{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}{+}{\Gamma^{\prime}}}}}f\;\;\text{with }\begin{aligned} &\Gamma^{\prime}=\bigl\{g_{1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}h_{1},\ldots g_{i-1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}h_{i-1},g_{i+1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}h_{i+1},\ldots,g_{n}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}h_{n}\bigr\}\cup\Delta\hskip 1.5pt{,}\\
&\text{ where: }\Delta\mathrel{{:=}}\begin{aligned} &\bigl\{g_{i1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}h_{i1},\ldots g_{im}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}h_{im}\bigr\}\\[-2.15277pt]
&\text{ the set of conclusions of $\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{i1},\ldots,\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{im}$.}\end{aligned}\end{aligned}\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:6:prf:it:3:lem:thmsubsumption:rels:coindproofsystems}

(17)

Now due to (
15
)
there is a LLEE-witnessed coinductive proof
\mathcal{L\hskip-0.5ptC\hskip-0.75ptP}
of
e\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}f
over
{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}{+}{\Gamma}
.
But now
\mathcal{L\hskip-0.5ptC\hskip-0.75ptP}
is also a LLEE-witnessed coinductive proof
of
e\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}f
over
{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}{+}{\Gamma^{\prime}}
,
and thus over a different set of premises, which we recognize as follows.
The equations in
\Delta
, which have been added to
\Gamma
in order to get
\Gamma^{\prime}
after removing
g_{i}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}h_{i}
,
are derivable in
{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}{+}{\Gamma}
by means for the derivation
\mathcal{D}_{0}
.
Therefore the correctness conditions for
\mathcal{L\hskip-0.5ptC\hskip-0.75ptP}
as a LLEE-witnessed coinductive proof over
{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}{+}{\Gamma}

imply the correctness conditions for
\mathcal{L\hskip-0.5ptC\hskip-0.75ptP}
as a LLEE-witnessed coinductive proof over
{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}{+}{\Gamma^{\prime}}
.
This shows (
17
).
Therefore the resulting derivation
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
in (
16
)
is a derivation in
CLC
with the same conclusion as
\mathcal{D}
.
(This transformation step can obviously be generalized to the situation in which not just
\mathcal{D}_{i}
,
but also others among the immediate subderivations
\mathcal{D}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{D}_{n}
of
\mathcal{D}
contain axioms and/or rules of
Mil
-
.)
In this way we have performed the induction step.

Finally we establish (
12
) in full generality.
For this we consider the remaining situation in which the derivation
\mathcal{D}
in
cMil
does not terminate with an instance of a coinductive rule.
Then
\mathcal{D}
can be construed with a part derivation
\mathcal{D}_{0}
in
Mil
-
above its conclusion,
and below
m\in{\rm Nature}
subderivations
\mathcal{D}_{1}
, …,
\mathcal{D}_{m}
, each of which terminates with a coinductive rule
(see below on the left). Note that
m=0
is possible if
\mathcal{D}
is a derivation in
Mil
-
.
By applying (
13
) to
\mathcal{D}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{D}_{m}

we obtain derivations
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{m}
in
CLC
with the same conclusions, respectively.
By combining these derivations in
CLC
with an instance of
LCoProof
m
we can perform the following transformation step
in order to obtain a derivation
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
in
CLC
:

\displaystyle\mbox{
\ignorespaces\ignorespaces
\ignorespaces \ignorespaces\ignorespaces
\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces
\ignorespaces\lower 36.41666pt\hbox{\vbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 12.90453pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathcal{D}_{1}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox{}\hbox to45.9341pt{\leaders{\hbox to7.96677pt{\hss\vrule width=3.98337pt,height=0.4pt,depth=0.0pt\hss}}{\hfill}}\lower-0.2pt\hbox{}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$(e_{1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}f_{1})$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\hbox{$\ldots$}\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 14.45952pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathcal{D}_{m}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox{}\hbox to52.15407pt{\leaders{\hbox to7.96677pt{\hss\vrule width=3.98337pt,height=0.4pt,depth=0.0pt\hss}}{\hfill}}\lower-0.2pt\hbox{}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$(e_{m}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}f_{m})$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{}\hbox{\vbox{\vskip 1.0pt}}\lower 0.0pt\hbox{}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 42.73157pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathcal{D}_{0}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 38.20207pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{}\hbox{\vbox{\vskip 1.0pt}}\lower 0.0pt\hbox{}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 38.20207pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$e\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}f$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}
\ignorespaces}\;\;\Longrightarrow\;\;\mbox{
\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 24.78856pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 9.1424pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{}\mathord{}$}\hbox{$\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox{}\hbox to38.1563pt{\leaders{\hbox to7.96677pt{\hss\vrule width=3.98337pt,height=0.4pt,depth=0.0pt\hss}}{\hfill}}\lower-0.2pt\hbox{}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{e_{1}}\mathord{}$}\hbox{${}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}f_{1}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\hbox{\hskip 4.30554pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{\ldots}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.30554pt}\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 12.25238pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{}\mathord{}$}\hbox{$\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{m}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox{}\hbox to44.37627pt{\leaders{\hbox to7.96677pt{\hss\vrule width=3.98337pt,height=0.4pt,depth=0.0pt\hss}}{\hfill}}\lower-0.2pt\hbox{}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{e_{m}}\mathord{}$}\hbox{${}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}f_{m}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\vskip-1.97035pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to106.64365pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 1.97035pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt$\text{LCoProof${}_{m}$}$}}\vskip-1.97035pt\hbox{\hskip 38.72981pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$e\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}f$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}
\ignorespaces}

(18)

Here we need to establish the following side-condition for the instance of
LCoProof
m
at the bottom of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
:

e\mathrel{{\overset{\text{\scriptsize LLEE}}{=\joinrel=\joinrel=}_{{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}{+}{\Gamma}}}}f\quad\text{where }\Gamma\mathrel{{:=}}\left\{{e_{1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}f_{1},\ldots,e_{m}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}f_{m}}\right\}\hskip 1.5pt{.}\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:8:prf:it:3:lem:thmsubsumption:rels:coindproofsystems}

(19)

We can establish this coinductive-provability statement
by recognizing that
\langle{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt),\hskip 0.5ptL\rangle

with
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)
the
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart interpretation of
e
, and with equation labeling function:

L\mathrel{:}{V\hskip-1.0pt}(\hskip-0.5pt{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)}\hskip-0.5pt)\to{\textit{StExp}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)\hskip 1.5pt{,}\;\;G\mapsto\begin{cases}G\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}G&\text{if $G\mathrel{{\not\equiv}}e$}\\[-2.15277pt]
e\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}f&\text{if $G\mathrel{{\equiv}}e$}\end{cases}

is a LLEE-witnessed coinductive proof over
{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}{+}{\Gamma}
of
e\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}f
.
To verify this statement,
we use that
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)
is a guarded LLEE -
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart by Theorem
4
,
(i)
, and
we have to check the correctness conditions for
L_{1}
and
L_{2}
with
L=\langle L_{1},\hskip 0.5ptL_{2}\rangle

to be
{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}{+}{\Gamma}
-provable solutions of
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)
.
We first note that
e\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}f
is provable in
{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}{+}{\Gamma}
(i.e.
e\mathrel{{=_{{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}{+}{\Gamma}}\hskip 1.0pt}}f
)
since
\mathcal{D}_{0}
is a derivation of
e\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}f
in
Mil
-
from the assumptions in
\Gamma
.
Then we argue as follows:

•

The correctness conditions for
L_{1}
to be a
({\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}{+}{\Gamma})
-provable solution of
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)

follow from the fact that
L_{1}
is a
Mil
-
-provable solution of
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)

due to Lemma
17
.

•

L_{2}
differs from
L_{1}
only in the value for the start vertex
e
,
where
{L_{2}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)\mathrel{{\equiv}}f
, but
{L_{1}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)\mathrel{{\equiv}}e
.
From this it follows, in view of
e\mathrel{{=_{{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}{+}{\Gamma}}\hskip 1.0pt}}f
,
that the correctness conditions for
L_{1}

imply the correctness conditions for
L_{2}
,
because they differ only up to expressions that are provably equal in
{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}{+}{\Gamma}

and also need to hold up to
({\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}{+}{\Gamma})
-provability.

This argument shows (
19
),
which justifies the side-condition of the instance of
LCoProof
m

at the bottom of the derivation
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
on the right in (
18
).
Therefore we have indeed obtained from
\mathcal{D}
a derivation in
CLC
with the same conclusion as
\mathcal{D}
.
In this way we have completed the proof of
\text{\sf cMil}\mathrel{{\precsim}}\text{\sf C\hskip-0.5ptL\hskip-0.5ptC}
, the remaining part of
(iii)
.

Statement
(iv)
can be shown entirely analogously as statement
(iii)
.

Remark 24

(completeness of
CC
,
\overline{\text{\sf cMil}}
,
\overline{\sf Mil}{}^{\prime}
)
.

The proof systems
CC
and
\overline{\text{\sf cMil}}
,
as well as the variant
\overline{\sf Mil}{}^{\prime}
of Milner’s system with the general unique solvability principle USP are complete for bisimilarity of star expressions.
This can be established along Salomaa’s completeness proof
for his inference system for language equality of regular expressions
[
Sal66
]
,
by an argument that we can outline as follows.
Given star expressions
e
and
f
with
{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)\mathrel{\hbox{\kern 0.43057pt\vbox{\hbox{\raise 0.43057pt\hbox{\kern-0.43057pt{$\leftrightarrow$}\kern-0.43057pt}}\hrule}\kern 0.43057pt}\hskip 1.0pt}{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{f}\hskip-0.5pt)
,

e
and
f
can be shown to be principal values of
Mil
-
-provable solutions of
{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)
and
{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{f}\hskip-0.5pt)
, respectively
(by a lemma for the chart interpretation similar to Lemma
17
).
These solutions can be transferred to the (
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-free) product chart
\mathcal{C}
of
{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)
and
{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{f}\hskip-0.5pt)
,
with
e
and
f
as principal values
of
Mil
-
-provable solutions
L_{1}
and
L_{2}
of
\mathcal{C}
, respectively.
From this we obtain a (not necessarily LLEE-witnessed) coinductive proof
\langle\mathcal{C},\hskip 0.5ptL\rangle
of
e=f
over
Mil
-
. It follows that
e=f
is provable in
CC
, and in
\overline{\text{\sf cMil}}
.
Now since the correctness conditions for the
Mil
-
-provable solutions
L_{1}
and
L_{2}
of
\mathcal{C}

at each of the vertices of
\mathcal{C}

together form a guarded system of linear equations to which the rule USP can be applied (as
\mathcal{C}
is
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-free),
we obtain that
e\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}f
is also provable in
\overline{\sf Mil}{}^{\prime}
.

6.
From LLEE-witnessed coinductive proofs to Milner’s system

In this Section we develop a proof-theoretic interpretation of the coinductive variant system
cMil
of
Mil
in Milner’s original system
Mil
.
Since
cMil
and
Mil
differ only
by the coinductive rule scheme
\left\{{\text{LCoProof${}_{n}$}}\right\}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
(which is part of
cMil
, but not of
Mil
),
and
by the fixed-point rule
\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt
(which is part of
Mil
, but not of
cMil
),
the crucial step for this proof transformation is to show that instances of
LCoProof
n
, for
n\in{\rm Nature}
,
can be mimicked in
Mil

if their premise equations are derivable in
Mil
.
We will do so by showing that the rules
LCoProof
n
, for
n\in{\rm Nature}
,
are correct for
Mil
.
This implies that these rules are admissible in
Mil
(by Lemma
1
),
and also, that they can be eliminated from derivations in
{\text{\sf Mil}}{+}{\left\{{\text{LCoProof${}_{n}$}}\right\}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}}
,
which is an extension of
cMil
.
In this way we obtain the proof-theoretic interpretation of
cMil
in
Mil
.

\underline{\mathcal{C}}
{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e_{1}}\hskip-0.5pt)
{{{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e_{1}}\hskip-0.5pt)}/_{\mathrel{{=_{\text{\sf Mil}}\hskip 1.0pt}}}}
{{{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e_{2}}\hskip-0.5pt)}/_{\mathrel{{=_{\text{\sf Mil}}\hskip 1.0pt}}}}
{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e_{2}}\hskip-0.5pt)
\left[{L_{1}}\right]_{{=_{\text{\sf Mil}}\hskip 1.0pt}}
\left[{L_{2}}\right]_{{=_{\text{\sf Mil}}\hskip 1.0pt}}
\left.\parbox{0.0pt}{{\large\hskip 0.0pt$\langle\underline{\mathcal{C}},\hskip 0.5ptL\rangle$\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{}}
\\
\hskip 0.0pt{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}\text{LLEE}}-witnessed\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{}
\\
\hskip 0.0ptcoinductive proof\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{}
\\
\hskip 0.0ptof $e_{1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}e_{2}$\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{}
\\
\hskip 0.0ptover ${\mathcal{S}}\mathrel{{\precsim}}\text{\sf Mil}$\hskip 0.0pt\mbox{}}\right\}
LLEE
, guarded
e_{1}\mathrel{{=_{\text{\sf Mil}}\hskip 1.0pt}}e_{2}
(Prop.
10
)

(Prop.
29
, together

(
with “
\Leftarrow
”
in Prop.
10

(
for
{\mathcal{S}}=\text{\sf Mil}
)

Figure 11.
Statement underlying the proof transformation from
cMil
to
Mil
.

For proving correctness of
LCoProof
n
for
Mil
, where
n\in{\rm Nature}
,
we will show that every LLEE-witnessed coinductive proof
\langle\underline{\mathcal{C}},\hskip 0.5ptL\rangle

over a proof system
{\mathcal{S}}
that is theorem-subsumed by
Mil
of an equation
e_{1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}e_{2}

can also be established by a proof of
e_{1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}e_{2}
in Milner’s system
Mil
.
Informally, this statement is illustrated in Figure
11

by informally employing the characterization in Proposition
10
of the provable solutions
in such LLEE-witnessed coinductive proofs. We establish the indicated informal second step in this section,
where it will be guaranteed by Proposition
29
.
In particular, our proof of this step will use the following two statements:

(SE)

(solution extraction)

from a LLEE-witness
\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt
of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
a
Mil
-provable solution
s_{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}
of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}

can be extracted (Lemma
26
), and

(SU)

(solution uniqueness)
every
Mil
-provable solution of the LLEE -
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
(such as
L_{1}
and
L_{2}
) is
Mil
-provably equal
to the solution
s_{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}
extracted from
\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt
(Lemma
28
).

By these statements we will obtain
for every LLEE-witnessed coinductive proof
\langle\underline{\mathcal{C}},\hskip 0.5ptL\rangle
of
e_{1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}e_{2}
,
assuming that
v_{\hskip-0.5pt\text{s}}
is the star vertex of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
and hence
{L_{1}}(\hskip-0.5pt{v_{\hskip-0.5pt\text{s}}}\hskip-0.5pt)\mathrel{{\equiv}}e_{1}
as well as
{L_{2}}(\hskip-0.5pt{v_{\hskip-0.5pt\text{s}}}\hskip-0.5pt)\mathrel{{\equiv}}e_{2}
hold,
that

e_{1}\mathrel{{\equiv}}{L_{1}}(\hskip-0.5pt{v_{\hskip-0.5pt\text{s}}}\hskip-0.5pt)\mathrel{{=_{\text{\sf Mil}}\hskip 1.0pt}}{s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{v_{\hskip-0.5pt\text{s}}}\hskip-0.5pt)\mathrel{{=_{\text{\sf Mil}}\hskip 1.0pt}}{L_{2}}(\hskip-0.5pt{v_{\hskip-0.5pt\text{s}}}\hskip-0.5pt)\mathrel{{\equiv}}e_{2}
holds,
and therefore
e_{1}\mathrel{{=_{\text{\sf Mil}}\hskip 1.0pt}}e_{2}
.

The proofs of the statements
(SE)
and
(SU)
below are adaptations to LLEE -
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-charts of proofs of analogous statements for LLEE-charts in Section 5 of
[
GF20a
,
GF20b
]
.
We have to, at places substantially, refine the extraction technique of star expressions from process graphs with
LLEE
that was first described in
[
GF20a
,
GF20b
]
.
However, we will use the simplification to only reason about guarded LLEE-witnesses,
in which loop-entry transition are proper transitions. We can do so because the
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart interpretation
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)
of a star expression
e
is guaranteed to have a guarded LLEE-witness
{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)
by Theorem
4
.

For developing and proving the extraction statement
(SE)
we use that the hierarchical loop structure of a
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart
\underline{\mathcal{C}}

with LLEE-witness
\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt

facilitates the extraction of a
Mil
-
-provable solution of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}

(see Lemma
26
). The reason is as follows. The process behavior at every vertex
w
in
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
can be split
into an iteration part that is induced via the loop-entry transitions from
w
in
\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt

(which induce loop sub-
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-charts with inner loop sub-
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-charts whose behavior can be synthesized recursively),
and an exit part that is induced via the body transitions from
w
in
\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt
.
This intuition leads us to the definition below.
We define the ‘extraction function’
s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}

of
\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt

from a ‘relative extraction function’
t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}

of
\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt
,
whose values
{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({w},\hskip 0.5pt{v})
capture the behavior at
w

in a loop sub-
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart at
v
until
v
is reached.

{defi}

[(relative) extraction function]
Let
\underline{\mathcal{C}}=\langle V,\mathit{A},{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1},v_{\hskip-0.5pt\text{s}},{{\to}},{{\downarrow}}\rangle
be a (guarded) LLEE -
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart with guarded LLEE-witness
\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt
.

The
extraction function

s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}\mathrel{:}V\to{\textit{StExp}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)

of
\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt

is defined from the

relative extraction function

t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}\mathrel{:}\left\{{\langle w,\hskip 0.5ptv\rangle}\mathrel{|}{w,v\in{V\hskip-1.0pt}(\hskip-0.5pt{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.5pt),\,w\mathrel{{{\curvearrowleft}^{\hskip-0.8pt=}}}v}\right\}\to{\textit{StExp}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)

of
\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt

for
w,v\in V
:

\displaystyle{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({w},\hskip 0.5pt{v})

\displaystyle{}\mathrel{{:=}}\begin{cases}1&\text{if $w=v$,}\\[-2.15277pt]
{\displaystyle{\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}{a_{i}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({w_{i}},\hskip 0.5pt{w})}\Bigr)^{*}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{\displaystyle\Bigl({\sum_{i=1}^{m}{{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{b}}_{i}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({u_{i}},\hskip 0.5pt{v})}}\Bigr)}&\text{if $w\mathrel{{\curvearrowleft}}v$,}\end{cases}

\displaystyle{s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)

\displaystyle{}\mathrel{{:=}}{{\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}{a_{i}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({w_{i}},\hskip 0.5pt{w})}\Bigr)^{*}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{\Bigl({{\tau_{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)}+{\sum_{i=1}^{m}{{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{b}}_{i}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{u_{i}}\hskip-0.5pt)}}\Bigr)}\hskip 1.5pt{,}

\displaystyle\hskip-34.44434pt\text{provided: }{T_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)=\begin{aligned} &\bigl\{{w\mathrel{{\xrightarrow{a_{i}}}{{}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}[{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}l}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}i}}]}}}}}w_{i}}\mathrel{\big|}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}l}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}i}}\in{\rm Nature}^{+},\,i\in\left\{{1,\ldots,n}\right\}}\bigr\}{\cup}\bigl\{{w\mathrel{{\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{b}}_{i}}}}{{}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}[\text{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}bo}}]}}}}}u_{i}}\mathrel{\big|}{i\in\left\{{1,\ldots,m}\right\}}\bigr\}\text{,}\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\hskip-34.44434pt\text{induction for $t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}$ on: }\begin{aligned} \langle w_{1},\hskip 0.5ptv_{1}\rangle\mathrel{{<}_{\text{lex}}}\langle w_{2},\hskip 0.5ptv_{2}\rangle\;\mathrel{:}\>\Longleftrightarrow\>v_{1}\mathrel{{{\curvearrowleft}^{\hskip-0.8pt+}}}v_{2}\mathrel{\vee}(\,v_{1}=v_{2}\mathrel{\wedge}w_{1}\mathrel{{\leftarrow_{\text{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}bo}}}^{+}}}w_{2}\,)\hskip 1.5pt{,}\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\hskip-34.44434pt\text{induction for $s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}$ on the strict partial order ${\leftarrow_{\text{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}bo}}}^{+}}$ (see Lemma\penalty\ \ref{lem:descsteps:bodysteps:wf})}\hskip 1.5pt{,}

where
{<}_{\text{lex}}
is a well-founded strict partial order due to Lemma
13
.
The choice of the list representations of action-target sets of
\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt
changes the definitions of these functions
only up to provability in
ACI
.

We exemplify the extraction process defined above by a concrete example.
