Figure 12.
Extraction of the principal value of a
Mil
-
-provable solution
s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}

from the LLEE-witness
\hskip 0.75pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\mathcal{C}}\hskip 0.0pt
in the coinductive proof
in Example
5
,
with
Mil
-
-provable simplifications.

We consider the
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
,
and the LLEE-witness
\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt
of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
,
in the LLEE-witnessed coinductive proof
\mathcal{C\hskip-0.75ptP}=\langle\underline{\mathcal{C}},\hskip 0.5ptL\rangle
of

{({{a^{*}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{b^{*}}})^{*}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}{({a}+{b})^{*}}

in Example
5
.
We detail in Figure
12
the process of computing
the principal value
{s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{v_{\hskip-0.5pt\text{s}}}\hskip-0.5pt)
of the
extraction function
s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}
of
\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt
. The statement of Lemma
26
below will guarantee that
s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}

is a
Mil
-
-provable solution of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
.

In order to show
that the extraction function of a guarded LLEE-witness of a
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart
\underline{\mathcal{C}}

defines a
Mil
-
-provable solution of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
,
see Lemma
26
and its proof later,
we first have to establish a
Mil
-
-provable connection
between the relative extraction function and the extraction function of a guarded LLEE-witness.
For this we prove the following lemma.

Lemma 25
.

Let
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
be a (guarded) LLEE -
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart with guarded LLEE-witness
\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt
.
Then

{s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}{{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({w},\hskip 0.5pt{v})}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{v}\hskip-0.5pt)}

holds
for all vertices
w,v\in{V\hskip-1.0pt}(\hskip-0.5pt{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.5pt)
such that
w\mathrel{{{\curvearrowleft}^{\hskip-0.8pt=}}}v
.

Proof 6.1
.

Let
\underline{\mathcal{C}}=\langle V,\mathit{A},{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1},v_{\hskip-0.5pt\text{s}},{{\to}},{{\downarrow}}\rangle
be a LLEE -
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart with guarded LLEE-witness
\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt
.

We have to show that

{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}{{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({w},\hskip 0.5pt{v})}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{v}\hskip-0.5pt)}

holds for all
w,v\in V
with
w\mathrel{{{\curvearrowleft}^{\hskip-0.8pt=}}}v
.
We first notice that this statement holds obviously for
w=v
,
due
{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({w},\hskip 0.5pt{v})\mathrel{{\equiv}}{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({v},\hskip 0.5pt{v})\mathrel{{\equiv}}1
,
and the presence of the axiom
({\text{id}_{\text{l}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{{\cdot}}\hskip-0.5pt))
in
Mil
-
.
Therefore it suffices to show, by also using this fact, that

{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}{{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({w},\hskip 0.5pt{v})}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{v}\hskip-0.5pt)}

holds for all
w,v\in V
with
w\mathrel{{\curvearrowleft}}v
.
We will show this by using the same induction as for the definition of the relative extraction function
t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}
in Definition
6
, that is,
by complete induction on the (converse) lexicographic partial order
{<}_{\text{lex}}
of
{{\curvearrowleft}^{\hskip-0.8pt+}}
and
{\leftarrow_{\text{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}bo}}}^{+}}

on
V\times V
defined by:

\langle w_{1},\hskip 0.5ptv_{1}\rangle\mathrel{{<}_{\text{lex}}}\langle w_{2},\hskip 0.5ptv_{2}\rangle\;\mathrel{:}\>\Longleftrightarrow\>v_{1}\mathrel{{{\curvearrowleft}^{\hskip-0.8pt+}}}v_{2}\mathrel{\vee}(\,v_{1}=v_{2}\mathrel{\wedge}w_{1}\mathrel{{\leftarrow_{\text{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}bo}}}^{+}}}w_{2}\,)
,
which is well-founded by Lemma
13
.
For our argument we assume to have given,
underlying the definition of the relative extraction function
t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}

and the extraction function
s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}
,
list

representations
{T_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)
of the transitions from
w
in
\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt

as in Definition
6
,
for all
w\in V
.

In order to carry out the induction step,
we let
w,v\in V
be arbitrary, but such that
w\mathrel{{\curvearrowleft}}v
.
On the basis of the form of
{T_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)

as in Definition
6

we argue as follows,
starting with a step in which we use the definition of
s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}
,
and followed by a second step in which we use that
{\tau_{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)\mathrel{{\equiv}}0
holds,
because
w
cannot have immediate termination as due to
w\mathrel{{\curvearrowleft}}v
it is
in the body of the loop at
v
(cf. condition (L3) for loop
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-charts in Section
4
):

\displaystyle{s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)

\displaystyle\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{\equiv}}$}\>{\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}{a_{i}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({w_{i}},\hskip 0.5pt{w})}\Bigr)^{*}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{\Bigr({\Bigr(\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{m}{{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{b}}_{i}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{u_{i}}\hskip-0.5pt)}\Bigl)}+{{\tau_{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)}\Bigl)}

\displaystyle\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{\equiv}}$}\>{\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}{a_{i}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({w_{i}},\hskip 0.5pt{w})}\Bigr)^{*}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{\Bigr({\Bigr(\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{m}{{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{b}}_{i}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{u_{i}}\hskip-0.5pt)}\Bigl)}+{0}\Bigl)}

\displaystyle\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{$\>\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}$}\>{\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}{a_{i}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({w_{i}},\hskip 0.5pt{w})}\Bigr)^{*}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{\Bigr(\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{m}{{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{b}}_{i}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{u_{i}}\hskip-0.5pt)}\Bigl)}

(by axiom
({\text{neutr}}(\hskip-0.5pt{+}\hskip-0.5pt))
)

\displaystyle\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}$}\>{\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}{a_{i}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({w_{i}},\hskip 0.5pt{w})}\Bigr)^{*}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{\sum_{i=1}^{m}{{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{b}}_{i}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{({t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({u_{i}},\hskip 0.5pt{v})}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{v}\hskip-0.5pt))}}}

\bigl(
if
u_{i}=v
, then

{s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{u_{i}}\hskip-0.5pt)\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}{{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({u_{i}},\hskip 0.5pt{v})}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s_{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{v}\hskip-0.5pt))}

due to
{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({v},\hskip 0.5pt{v})=1
;

(
if
u_{i}\neq v
, we can apply the induction hypothesis
to
{s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{u_{i}}\hskip-0.5pt)
,

(if
as
w\mathrel{{{\to}_{\text{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}bo}}}}}u_{i}
(see
{T_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)
as in Def.
6
)
and
u_{i}\neq v

(if
imply
u_{i}\mathrel{{\curvearrowleft}}v
,
and
u_{i}\mathrel{{\leftarrow_{\text{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}bo}}}}}w

entails
\langle u_{i},\hskip 0.5ptv\rangle\mathrel{{<}_{\text{lex}}}\langle w,\hskip 0.5ptv\rangle
)

\displaystyle\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}$}\>{\Bigl(\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}{a_{i}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({w_{i}},\hskip 0.5pt{w})}\Bigr)^{*}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{\Bigr(\sum_{i=1}^{m}{{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{b}}_{i}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({u_{i}},\hskip 0.5pt{v})}\Bigl)\Bigr)}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{v}\hskip-0.5pt)}}

(by axioms
({\text{r-distr}}(\hskip-0.5pt{+,{\cdot}}\hskip-0.5pt))
, and
({\text{assoc}}(\hskip-0.5pt{{\cdot}}\hskip-0.5pt))
)

\displaystyle\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{\equiv}}$}\>{{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({w},\hskip 0.5pt{v})}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{v}\hskip-0.5pt)}

(by
w\mathrel{{\curvearrowleft}}v
,
and the definition of
{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({w},\hskip 0.5pt{v})
in Def.
6
)

We note that this reasoning also applies for the special cases
n=0
,
and with a slight change also for
m=0
,
where

\sum_{i=1}^{m}{{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{b}}_{i}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{u_{i}}\hskip-0.5pt)}\mathrel{{\equiv}}\sum_{i=1}^{m}{{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{b}}_{i}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{({t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({u_{i}},\hskip 0.5pt{v})}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{v}\hskip-0.5pt))}}\mathrel{{\equiv}}\sum_{i=1}^{m}{{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{b}}_{i}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({u_{i}},\hskip 0.5pt{v})}\mathrel{{\equiv}}0
,
and then an axiom (
deadlock
) has to be used.
In this way we have shown, due to
\text{\sf ACI}\mathrel{{\sqsubseteq}}\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}
, the desired
Mil
-
-provable equality

{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}{{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({w},\hskip 0.5pt{v})}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{v}\hskip-0.5pt)}

for the vertices
v
and
w
that we picked with the property
w\mathrel{{\curvearrowleft}}v
.

Since
w,v\in V
with
w\mathrel{{\curvearrowleft}}v
were arbitrary above, we have successfully carried out the proof by induction that

{s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}{{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({w},\hskip 0.5pt{v})}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s_{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{v}\hskip-0.5pt)}

holds for all
w,v\in V
with
w\mathrel{{\curvearrowleft}}v
.
As we have argued that the statement also holds for
w=v
, we have proved the lemma.

Lemma 26

(extracted function is provable solution)
.

Let
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
be a LLEE -
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart with guarded LLEE-witness
\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt
.
Then the extraction function
s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}
of
\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt

is a
Mil
-
-provable solution of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
.

Proof 6.2
.

Let
\underline{\mathcal{C}}=\langle V,\mathit{A},{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1},v_{\hskip-0.5pt\text{s}},{{\to}},{{\downarrow}}\rangle
be a (guarded) LLEE -
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart with guarded LLEE-witness
\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt
.

We show that the extraction function
s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}
of
\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt
is a
Mil
-
-provable solution of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}

by verifying the
Mil
-
-provable correctness conditions for
s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}

at every vertex
w\in V
.
For the argument we assume to have given,
underlying the definition of the relative extraction function
t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}

and

the extraction function
s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}
,
list representations
{T_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)
of the transitions from
w
in
\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt

as written in Definition
6
,
for all vertices
w\in V
.

We let
w\in V
be arbitrary.
Starting from the definition of
s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}
in Definition
6

on the basis of the form of
{T_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)
,
we argue by the following steps:

\displaystyle{s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)

\displaystyle\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{$\>\mathrel{{\equiv}}\>$}\>{\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}{a_{i}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({w_{i}},\hskip 0.5pt{w})}\Bigr)^{*}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{\>\Bigr(\displaystyle{\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{m}{{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{b}}_{i}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{u_{i}}\hskip-0.5pt)}\Bigr)}+{{\tau_{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)}\Bigl)}

\displaystyle\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}$}\>{\Bigl({{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}}+{{\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}{a_{i}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({w_{i}},\hskip 0.5pt{w})}\Bigr)\hskip-2.0pt}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{\hskip-2.0pt\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}{a_{i}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({w_{i}},\hskip 0.5pt{w})}\Bigr)^{*}}}\Bigr)\hskip-2.0pt}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{\hskip-2.0pt\Bigr(\displaystyle{\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{m}{{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{b}}_{i}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{u_{i}}\hskip-0.5pt)}\Bigr)}+{{\tau_{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)}\Bigl)}

(by axiom
({\text{rec}}(\hskip-0.5pt{{}^{*}}\hskip-0.5pt))
)

\displaystyle\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}$}\>\begin{aligned} &\Bigr({\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{m}{{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{b}}_{i}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{u_{i}}\hskip-0.5pt)}\Bigr)}+{{\tau_{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)}\Bigl)\\[-4.30554pt]
&{}+{{\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}{a_{i}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({w_{i}},\hskip 0.5pt{w})}\Bigr)}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{\Bigl(\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}{a_{i}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({w_{i}},\hskip 0.5pt{w})}\Bigr)^{*}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{\Bigl({\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{m}{{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{b}}_{i}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{u_{i}}\hskip-0.5pt)}\Bigr)}+{{\tau_{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)}\Bigr)}\Bigr)}}\end{aligned}

(by axioms
({\text{r-distr}}(\hskip-0.5pt{+,{\cdot}}\hskip-0.5pt))
,
({\text{id}_{\text{l}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{{\cdot}}\hskip-0.5pt))
, and
({\text{assoc}}(\hskip-0.5pt{{\cdot}}\hskip-0.5pt))
)

\displaystyle\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{\equiv}}$}\>{\Bigr({\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{m}{{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{b}}_{i}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{u_{i}}\hskip-0.5pt)}\Bigr)}+{{\tau_{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)}\Bigl)}+{{\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}{a_{i}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({w_{i}},\hskip 0.5pt{w})}\Bigr)}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)}}

(by definition of
{s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)
in Def.
6
)

\displaystyle\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}$}\>{\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}{a_{i}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{\bigl({t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({w_{i}},\hskip 0.5pt{w})}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)\bigr)}}\Bigr)}+{\Bigr({\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{m}{{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{b}}_{i}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{u_{i}}\hskip-0.5pt)}\Bigr)}+{{\tau_{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)}\Bigl)}

(by axioms
({\text{comm}}(\hskip-0.5pt{+}\hskip-0.5pt))
,
({\text{r-distr}}(\hskip-0.5pt{+,{\cdot}}\hskip-0.5pt))
, and
({\text{assoc}}(\hskip-0.5pt{{\cdot}}\hskip-0.5pt))
)

\displaystyle\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}$}\>{\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}{a_{i}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{w_{i}}\hskip-0.5pt)}\Bigr)}+{{\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{m}{{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{b}}_{i}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{u_{i}}\hskip-0.5pt)}\Bigr)}+{{\tau_{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)}}

(by Lemma
25
, due to
w_{i}\mathrel{{{\curvearrowleft}^{\hskip-0.8pt=}}}w
,
which follows from
w\mathrel{{\rightarrow_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}l}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}i}}}]}}}}w_{i}

(
(see
{T_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)

as in Def.
6
),
and by axioms
({\text{assoc}}(\hskip-0.5pt{+}\hskip-0.5pt))\,
).

\displaystyle\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{=_{\text{\sf ACI}}\hskip 1.0pt}}$}\>{{\tau_{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)}+{{\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}{a_{i}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{w_{i}}\hskip-0.5pt)}\Bigr)}+{\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{m}{{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{b}}_{i}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{u_{i}}\hskip-0.5pt)}\Bigr)}}\hskip 1.5pt{.}

Since
\text{\sf ACI}\mathrel{{\sqsubseteq}}\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}
,
this chain of equalities yields a
Mil
-
-provable equality
that establishes,
in view of
{T_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)
as in Definition
6
,
the correctness condition for
s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}
to be a
Mil
-
-provable solution
at the vertex
w
that we picked.

Since
w\in V
was arbitrary,
we have established that the extraction function
s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}
of
\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt

is a
Mil
-
-provable solution of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
.

For showing the solution uniqueness statement
(SU)
we can also use the hierarchical loop structure of a
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
with LLEE-witness
\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt

for carrying out proofs by induction.
We repurpose the two-step approach of the proof of Lemma
26

that used the
Mil
-provable relationship between
the extraction function
s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}
of
\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt
and the relative

extraction function
t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}
of
\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt

in Lemma
25
.
In doing so

we first establish, for every
Mil
-provable solution
s
of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
,
a connection with the relative extraction function
t_{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}
of
\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt
,
see Lemma
27
below.
The proof of this lemma proceeds by an induction that starts at innermost loop sub-
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-charts of the given LLEE-witness,
and then progresses to outer loop sub-
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-charts.
Different from Lemma
25
,
it will be crucial here to employ the fixed-point rule
\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt
of
Mil
in the proof.

Lemma 27
.

Let
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
be a LLEE -
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart with guarded LLEE-witness
\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt
.
Furthermore, let
{\mathcal{S}}
be an
{\text{$\mathcal{E\hskip-1.0ptL}$}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)
-based proof system such that
\text{\sf ACI}\mathrel{{\sqsubseteq}}{\mathcal{S}}\mathrel{{\precsim}}\text{\sf Mil}
.

Let
s\mathrel{:}{V\hskip-1.0pt}(\hskip-0.5pt{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.5pt)\to{\textit{StExp}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)

be an
{\mathcal{S}}
-provable solution of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
.
Then

{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)\mathrel{{=_{\text{\sf Mil}}\hskip 1.0pt}}{{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({w},\hskip 0.5pt{v})}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{v}\hskip-0.5pt)}

holds for all vertices
w,v\in{V\hskip-1.0pt}(\hskip-0.5pt{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.5pt)
with
w\mathrel{{{\curvearrowleft}^{\hskip-0.8pt=}}}v
.

Proof 6.3
.

Let
\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt
be a guarded LLEE-witness of a (guarded) LLEE -
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart
\underline{\mathcal{C}}=\langle V,\mathit{A},{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1},v_{\hskip-0.5pt\text{s}},{{\to}},{{\downarrow}}\rangle
.
Let
s\mathrel{:}V\to{\textit{StExp}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)
be an
{\mathcal{S}}
-provable solution of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
.

We have to show that

{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)\mathrel{{=_{\text{\sf Mil}}\hskip 1.0pt}}{{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({w},\hskip 0.5pt{v})}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{v}\hskip-0.5pt)}

holds for all
w,v\in V
with
w\mathrel{{{\curvearrowleft}^{\hskip-0.8pt=}}}v
.
We first notice that this statement holds obviously for
w=v
,
due
{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({w},\hskip 0.5pt{v})\mathrel{{\equiv}}{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({v},\hskip 0.5pt{v})\mathrel{{\equiv}}1
.
Therefore it suffices to show, by also using this fact, that

{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)\mathrel{{=_{\text{\sf Mil}}\hskip 1.0pt}}{{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({w},\hskip 0.5pt{v})}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{v}\hskip-0.5pt)}

holds for all
w,v\in V
with
w\mathrel{{\curvearrowleft}}v
.
We will show this by using the same induction as for the definition of the relative extraction function in Definition
6
, that is,
by complete induction on the (converse) lexicographic partial order
{<}_{\text{lex}}
of
{{\curvearrowleft}^{\hskip-0.8pt+}}
and
{\leftarrow_{\text{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}bo}}}^{+}}

on
V\times V
defined by:

\langle w_{1},\hskip 0.5ptv_{1}\rangle\mathrel{{<}_{\text{lex}}}\langle w_{2},\hskip 0.5ptv_{2}\rangle\;\mathrel{:}\>\Longleftrightarrow\>v_{1}\mathrel{{{\curvearrowleft}^{\hskip-0.8pt+}}}v_{2}\mathrel{\vee}(\,v_{1}=v_{2}\mathrel{\wedge}w_{1}\mathrel{{\leftarrow_{\text{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}bo}}}^{+}}}w_{2}\,)
,
which is well-founded due to Lemma
13
.
For our argument we suppose to have given,
underlying the definition of the relative extraction function
t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}

and the extraction function
s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}
,
list representations
{T_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)
of the transitions from
w
in
\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt

as written in Definition
6
,
for all
w\in V
.

In order to carry out the induction step,
we let
w,v\in V
be arbitrary such that
w\mathrel{{\curvearrowleft}}v
.
On the basis of the form of
{T_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)
as in Definition
6

we argue as follows,
starting with a step in which we use that
s
is an
{\mathcal{S}}
-provable solution of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
,
and followed by a second step in which we use that
{\tau_{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)\mathrel{{\equiv}}0
holds,
because
w
cannot have immediate termination as due to
w\mathrel{{\curvearrowleft}}v
it is
in the body of the loop at
v
(see condition (L3) for loop
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-charts in Section
4
):

\displaystyle{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)

\displaystyle\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{=_{{\mathcal{S}}}\hskip 1.0pt}}$}\>{{\tau_{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)}+{{\Bigl(\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}{a_{i}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{w_{i}}\hskip-0.5pt)}\Bigr)}+{\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{m}{{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{b}}_{i}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{u_{i}}\hskip-0.5pt)}\Bigr)\Bigr)}}

\displaystyle\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}$}\>{\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}{a_{i}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{w_{i}}\hskip-0.5pt)}\Bigr)}+{\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{m}{{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{b}}_{i}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{u_{i}}\hskip-0.5pt)}\Bigr)}

\displaystyle\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{=_{\text{\sf Mil}}\hskip 1.0pt}}$}\>{\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}{a_{i}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{\bigl({{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({w_{i}},\hskip 0.5pt{w})}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)}\bigr)}\Bigr)}+{\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{m}{{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{b}}_{i}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{u_{i}}\hskip-0.5pt)}\Bigr)}

\bigl(
if
w_{i}=w
, then

{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{w_{i}}\hskip-0.5pt)\mathrel{{=_{\text{\sf Mil}}\hskip 1.0pt}}{{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({w_{i}},\hskip 0.5pt{w})}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt))}

due to
{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({w},\hskip 0.5pt{w})=1
;

(
if
w_{i}\neq w
, we can apply the induction hypothesis
to
{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{w_{i}}\hskip-0.5pt)
,

(if
because then
w\mathrel{{\rightarrow_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}l}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}i}}}]}}}}w_{i}

(see
{T_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)
as in Def.
6
) implies

w_{i}\mathrel{{\curvearrowleft}}w
,

(if
and due to
w\mathrel{{\curvearrowleft}}v
we get
\langle w_{i},\hskip 0.5ptw\rangle\mathrel{{<}_{\text{lex}}}\langle w,\hskip 0.5ptv\rangle
)

\displaystyle\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{=_{\text{\sf Mil}}\hskip 1.0pt}}$}\>{\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}{a_{i}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{\bigl({{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({w_{i}},\hskip 0.5pt{w})}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)}\bigr)}\Bigr)}+{\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{m}{{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{b}}_{i}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{\bigl({{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({u_{i}},\hskip 0.5pt{v})}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{v}\hskip-0.5pt)}\bigr)}\Bigr)}

\bigl(
if
u_{i}=v
, then

{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{u_{i}}\hskip-0.5pt)\mathrel{{=_{\text{\sf Mil}}\hskip 1.0pt}}{{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({u_{i}},\hskip 0.5pt{v})}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{v}\hskip-0.5pt))}

due to
{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({v},\hskip 0.5pt{v})=1
;

(
if
u_{i}\neq v
, we can apply the induction hypothesis
to
{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{u_{i}}\hskip-0.5pt)
,

(if
as
w\mathrel{{{\to}_{\text{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}bo}}}}}u_{i}
(see
{T_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)
as in Def.
6
)
and
u_{i}\neq v

(if
imply
u_{i}\mathrel{{\curvearrowleft}}v
,
and
u_{i}\mathrel{{\leftarrow_{\text{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}bo}}}}}w

entails
\langle u_{i},\hskip 0.5ptv\rangle\mathrel{{<}_{\text{lex}}}\langle w,\hskip 0.5ptv\rangle
)

\displaystyle\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}$}\>{{\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}{a_{i}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({w_{i}},\hskip 0.5pt{w})}\Bigr)}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)}}+{{\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{m}{{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{b}}_{i}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({u_{i}},\hskip 0.5pt{v})}\Bigr)}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{v}\hskip-0.5pt)}}

(by axioms
({\text{assoc}}(\hskip-0.5pt{{\cdot}}\hskip-0.5pt))
, and
({\text{r-distr}}(\hskip-0.5pt{+,{\cdot}}\hskip-0.5pt))
).

We note that these equalities also hold for the special cases in which
n=0
or/and
m=0
,
where in the case
m=0
an axiom (
deadlock
) needs to be used in the last step.
Since
\text{\sf ACI}\mathrel{{\sqsubseteq}}{\mathcal{S}}\mathrel{{\precsim}}\text{\sf Mil}
, and
\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}\mathrel{{\sqsubseteq}}\text{\sf Mil}
,
we have obtained the following provable equality:

\displaystyle{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)

\displaystyle\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{=_{\text{\sf Mil}}\hskip 1.0pt}}$}\>{{\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}{a_{i}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({w_{i}},\hskip 0.5pt{w})}\Bigr)}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)}}+{{\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{m}{{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{b}}_{i}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({u_{i}},\hskip 0.5pt{v})}\Bigr)}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{v}\hskip-0.5pt)}}\hskip 1.5pt{,}

Since

{\bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}{a_{i}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({w_{i}},\hskip 0.5pt{w})}\bigr)}{\ndownarrow}

holds, we can apply
\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt
in order to obtain, and reason further:

\displaystyle{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)

\displaystyle\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{=_{\text{\sf Mil}}\hskip 1.0pt}}$}\>{{\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}{a_{i}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({w_{i}},\hskip 0.5pt{w})}\Bigr)^{\!*}\!}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{\!\Bigl({\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{m}{{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{b}}_{i}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({u_{i}},\hskip 0.5pt{v})}\Bigr)}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{v}\hskip-0.5pt)}\Bigr)}

\displaystyle\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}$}\>{\Bigl({\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}{a_{i}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({w_{i}},\hskip 0.5pt{w})}\Bigr)^{\!*}\!}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{\!\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{m}{{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{b}}_{i}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({u_{i}},\hskip 0.5pt{v})}\Bigr)}\Bigr)}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{v}\hskip-0.5pt)}

(by axiom
({\text{assoc}}(\hskip-0.5pt{{\cdot}}\hskip-0.5pt))
)

\displaystyle\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{\equiv}}$}\>{{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({w},\hskip 0.5pt{v})}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{v}\hskip-0.5pt)}

(by
w\mathrel{{\curvearrowleft}}v
, and the definition of
{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({w},\hskip 0.5pt{v})

in Def.
6
)

In this way we have shown, due to
\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}\mathrel{{\sqsubseteq}}\text{\sf Mil}
, the desired
Mil
-provable equality

{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)\mathrel{{=_{\text{\sf Mil}}\hskip 1.0pt}}{{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({w},\hskip 0.5pt{v})}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{v}\hskip-0.5pt)}

for the vertices
v
and
w
that we picked with the property
w\mathrel{{\curvearrowleft}}v
.

Since
w,v\in V
with
w\mathrel{{\curvearrowleft}}v
were arbitrary for this argument,
we have successfully carried out the proof by induction

{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)\mathrel{{=_{\text{\sf Mil}}\hskip 1.0pt}}{{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({w},\hskip 0.5pt{v})}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{v}\hskip-0.5pt)}

holds for all
w,v\in V
with
w\mathrel{{\curvearrowleft}}v
.
As we have argued that the statement also holds for
w=v
, we have proved the lemma.

{defi}

For an
{\textit{Eq}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)
-based proof system
{\mathcal{S}}
we say
that two star expression functions
s_{1},s_{2}\mathrel{:}V\to{\textit{StExp}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)

are
{\mathcal{S}}
-provably equal
if
{s_{1}}(\hskip-0.5pt{v}\hskip-0.5pt)\mathrel{{=_{{\mathcal{S}}}\hskip 1.0pt}}{s_{2}}(\hskip-0.5pt{v}\hskip-0.5pt)
holds for all
v\in V
.

Now we use the relationship of arbitrary
Mil
-provable solutions of a guarded LLEE-witness
\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt

with the relative extraction function
s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}
of
\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt
as stated by Lemma
13

in order to demonstrate the solution uniqueness statement
(SU)
.
The proof can be viewed as proceeding on the maximal length of body transition paths from vertices
v
,
where for descents from
v
via a loop-entry transition into an inner loop the statement of Lemma
27
is used.
Again the use of the fixed-point rule
\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt
of
Mil
-
is crucial,
because any two
Mil
-
-provable solutions of a guarded LLEE -
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart cannot be expected to be
Mil
-
-provably equal in general.

3

3

As a simple example, the use of
\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt
is necessary for proving equal in
Mil
the two
Mil
-
-provable solutions
of the guarded LLEE -
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{(a\cdot a)^{*}\cdot 0}\hskip-0.5pt)

with the principal values
a^{*}\cdot 0
and

, respectively.

Lemma 28

(provable equality of solutions of LLEE -
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-charts)
.

Let
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
be a guarded LLEE -
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart,
and let
{\mathcal{S}}
be an
\mathcal{E\hskip-1.0ptL}
-based proof system over
{\textit{StExp}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)
such that
\text{\sf ACI}\mathrel{{\sqsubseteq}}{\mathcal{S}}\mathrel{{\precsim}}\text{\sf Mil}
.

Then any two
{\mathcal{S}}
-provable solutions of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
are
Mil
-provably equal.

Proof 6.4
.

Let
\underline{\mathcal{C}}=\langle V,\mathit{A},{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1},v_{\hskip-0.5pt\text{s}},{{\to}},{{\downarrow}}\rangle

be a LLEE -
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart with guarded LLEE-witness
\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt
,
and let
{\mathcal{S}}
an
\mathcal{E\hskip-1.0ptL}
-based proof system as assumed in the lemma.
In order to show that any two
{\mathcal{S}}
-provable solutions of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}

are
Mil
-provably equal,
it suffices to show that every
{\mathcal{S}}
-provable solution of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
is
Mil
-provably equal
to the extraction function
s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}
of
\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt
.

For demonstrating this,
let
s\mathrel{:}V\to{\textit{StExp}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)
be an
{\mathcal{S}}
-provable solution of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
.
We have to show that

{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)\mathrel{{=_{\text{\sf Mil}}\hskip 1.0pt}}{s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)

holds for all
w\in V
.
We proceed

by complete induction on the well-founded relation
{\leftarrow_{\text{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}bo}}}^{+}}
(see Lemma
13
,

2
),
which does not require us to treat base cases separately.
For our argument we assume to have given,
underlying the definition of the relative extraction function
t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}

and the extraction function
s_{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}
,
list

representations
{T_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)
of the transitions from
w
in
\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt

as written in Definition
6
,
for all
w\in V
.

Let
w\in V
be arbitrary.
On the basis of
{T_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)
as in Definition
6

we argue as follows,
starting with a step in which we use that
s
is an
{\mathcal{S}}
-provable solution of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}

in view of the assumed form of
{T_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)\,
:

\displaystyle{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)

\displaystyle\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{=_{{\mathcal{S}}}\hskip 1.0pt}}$}\>{{\tau_{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)}+{\Bigl({\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}{a_{i}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{w_{i}}\hskip-0.5pt)}\Bigr)}+{\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{m}{{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{b}}_{i}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{u_{i}}\hskip-0.5pt)}\Bigr)}\Bigr)}

\displaystyle\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{=_{\text{\sf ACI}}\hskip 1.0pt}}$}\>{\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}{a_{i}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{w_{i}}\hskip-0.5pt)}\Bigr)}+{\Bigl({\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{m}{{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{b}}_{i}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{u_{i}}\hskip-0.5pt)}\Bigr)}+{{\tau_{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)\Bigr)}\Bigr)}

\displaystyle\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{=_{\text{\sf Mil}}\hskip 1.0pt}}$}\>{\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}{a_{i}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{({{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({w_{i}},\hskip 0.5pt{w})}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)})}\Bigr)}+{\Bigl({\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{m}{{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{b}}_{i}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{u_{i}}\hskip-0.5pt)}\Bigr)}+{{\tau_{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)}\Bigr)}

(
w\mathrel{{\rightarrow_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}l}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}i}}}]}}}}w_{i}

(due to
{T_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)
as in Definition
6
) implies
w_{i}\mathrel{{{\curvearrowleft}^{\hskip-0.8pt=}}}w
,

(
from which Lemma
27
yields

{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{w_{i}}\hskip-0.5pt)\mathrel{{=_{\text{\sf Mil}}\hskip 1.0pt}}{{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({w_{i}},\hskip 0.5pt{w})}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)}\,
)

\displaystyle\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{$\>\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}$}\>{{\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}{a_{i}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({w_{i}},\hskip 0.5pt{w})}\Bigr)}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)}}+{\Bigl({\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{m}{{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{b}}_{i}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{u_{i}}\hskip-0.5pt)}\Bigr)}+{{\tau_{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)}\Bigr)}

(by axioms
({\text{assoc}}(\hskip-0.5pt{{\cdot}}\hskip-0.5pt))
and
({\text{r-distr}}(\hskip-0.5pt{+,{\cdot}}\hskip-0.5pt))
)

\displaystyle\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{$\>\mathrel{{=_{\text{\sf Mil}}\hskip 1.0pt}}$}\>{{\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}{a_{i}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({w_{i}},\hskip 0.5pt{w})}\Bigr)}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)}}+{\Bigl({\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{m}{{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{b}}_{i}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{u_{i}}\hskip-0.5pt)}\Bigr)}+{{\tau_{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)}\Bigr)}

(due to
w\mathrel{{{\to}_{\text{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}bo}}}}}u_{i}
(see
{T_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)
as in Definition
6
),
and hence
u_{i}\mathrel{{\leftarrow_{\text{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}bo}}}}}w
,

(
{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{u_{i}}\hskip-0.5pt)\mathrel{{=_{\text{\sf Mil}}\hskip 1.0pt}}{s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{u_{i}}\hskip-0.5pt)
follows from the induction hypothesis).

Since
\text{\sf ACI}\mathrel{{\sqsubseteq}}{\mathcal{S}}\mathrel{{\precsim}}\text{\sf Mil}
, and
\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}\mathrel{{\sqsubseteq}}\text{\sf Mil}
,
we have obtained the following provable equality:

\displaystyle{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)

\displaystyle\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{=_{\text{\sf Mil}}\hskip 1.0pt}}$}\>{{\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}{a_{i}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({w_{i}},\hskip 0.5pt{w})}\Bigr)}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)}}+{\Bigl({\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{m}{{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{b}}_{i}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{u_{i}}\hskip-0.5pt)}\Bigr)}+{{\tau_{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)}\Bigr)}

Now since

{\bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}{a_{i}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({w_{i}},\hskip 0.5pt{w})}\bigr)}{\ndownarrow}

holds, we can apply the rule
\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt
to this in order to obtain:

\displaystyle{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)

\displaystyle\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{$\>\mathrel{{=_{\text{\sf Mil}}\hskip 1.0pt}}$}\>{\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}{a_{i}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{t_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}({w_{i}},\hskip 0.5pt{w})}\Bigr)^{\!*}\!}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{\Bigl({\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{m}{{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{b}}_{i}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{u_{i}}\hskip-0.5pt)}\Bigr)}+{{\tau_{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)}\Bigr)}

\displaystyle\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{\equiv}}$}\>{s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{\mbox{}}\>\text{(by the definition of $s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}$ in Definition\penalty\ \ref{def:extrsoluntil:extrsol})}

Thus we have verified the proof obligation

{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)\mathrel{{=_{\text{\sf Mil}}\hskip 1.0pt}}{s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)

for the induction step, for the vertex
w
as picked.

By having performed the induction step, we have successfully carried out the proof by complete induction on
{\leftarrow_{\text{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}bo}}}}

that

{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)\mathrel{{=_{\text{\sf Mil}}\hskip 1.0pt}}{s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{w}\hskip-0.5pt)
holds for all
w\in V
,
and for an arbitrary
{\mathcal{S}}
-provable solution
s
of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
.
This implies the statement of the lemma, that any two
{\mathcal{S}}
-provable solutions of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
are
Mil
-provably equal.

Proposition 29
.

For every
\mathcal{E\hskip-1.0ptL}
-based proof system
{\mathcal{S}}
over
{\textit{StExp}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)
with
\text{\sf ACI}\mathrel{{\sqsubseteq}}{\mathcal{S}}\mathrel{{\precsim}}\text{\sf Mil}
,
provability by LLEE-witnessed coinductive proofs over
{\mathcal{S}}
implies derivability in
Mil
:

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:prop:LLEEcoindproofeq:impl:milnersyseq}\bigl(\;e_{1}\mathrel{{\overset{\text{\scriptsize LLEE}}{=\joinrel=\joinrel=}_{{\mathcal{S}}}}}e_{2}\quad\;\Longrightarrow\quad\;e_{1}\mathrel{{=_{\text{\sf Mil}}\hskip 1.0pt}}e_{2}\;\bigr)\qquad\text{for all $e_{1},e_{2}\in{\textit{StExp}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)$.}

(20)

Proof 6.5
.

For showing (
20
),
let
e,f\in{\textit{StExp}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)
be such that

e\mathrel{{\overset{\text{\scriptsize LLEE}}{=\joinrel=\joinrel=}_{{\mathcal{S}}}}}f
.
Then there is a LLEE-witnessed coinductive proof
\mathcal{L\hskip-0.5ptC\hskip-0.75ptP}=\langle\underline{\mathcal{C}},\hskip 0.5ptL\rangle
of
e_{1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}e_{2}
over
{\mathcal{S}}
,
which consists of a guarded LLEE -
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
and

{\mathcal{S}}
-provable solutions
L_{1},L_{2}\mathrel{:}{V\hskip-1.0pt}(\hskip-0.5pt{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.5pt)\to{\textit{StExp}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)
of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}

with
e_{1}\mathrel{{\equiv}}{L_{1}}(\hskip-0.5pt{v_{\hskip-0.5pt\text{s}}}\hskip-0.5pt)
and
e_{2}\mathrel{{\equiv}}{L_{2}}(\hskip-0.5pt{v_{\hskip-0.5pt\text{s}}}\hskip-0.5pt)
.
By applying Lemma
28
to
\mathcal{L\hskip-0.5ptC\hskip-0.75ptP}

we find that
L_{1}
and
L_{2}
are
Mil
-provably equal.
This entails

e_{1}\mathrel{{\equiv}}{L_{1}}(\hskip-0.5pt{v_{\hskip-0.5pt\text{s}}}\hskip-0.5pt)\mathrel{{=_{\text{\sf Mil}}\hskip 1.0pt}}{L_{2}}(\hskip-0.5pt{v_{\hskip-0.5pt\text{s}}}\hskip-0.5pt)\mathrel{{\equiv}}e_{2}
,
and thus
e_{1}\mathrel{{=_{\text{\sf Mil}}\hskip 1.0pt}}e_{2}
.

Figure 13.
Showing
for the coinductive proof
\langle\underline{\mathcal{C}},\hskip 0.5ptL\rangle
in Example
5

that the principal value
{L_{i}}(\hskip-0.5pt{v_{\hskip-0.5pt\text{s}}}\hskip-0.5pt)
of the
Mil
-
-provable solution
L_{i}
for
i\in\left\{{1,2}\right\}

is
Mil
-provably equal to the principal value
{s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}}(\hskip-0.5pt{v_{\hskip-0.5pt\text{s}}}\hskip-0.5pt)
of the solution
s_{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}

extracted the underlying LLEE-witness
\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt
.

We consider again the LLEE-witnessed coinductive proof
\mathcal{C\hskip-0.75ptP}=\langle\underline{\mathcal{C}},\hskip 0.5ptL\rangle
of

{({{a^{*}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{b^{*}}})^{*}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}{({a}+{b})^{*}}

in Example
5
.
In Figure
13
we exhibit the extraction process of derivations in
Mil

of
{L_{1}}(\hskip-0.5pt{v_{\hskip-0.5pt\text{s}}}\hskip-0.5pt)\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}{s_{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{v}\hskip-0.5pt)

and
{L_{2}}(\hskip-0.5pt{v_{\hskip-0.5pt\text{s}}}\hskip-0.5pt)\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}{s_{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{v}\hskip-0.5pt)

from the guarded LLEE-witness
\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt
of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
.
These two derivations in
Mil
can be combined by using
\mathcal{E\hskip-1.0ptL}
rules in order to obtain a derivation in
Mil
of

{({{a^{*}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{{b^{*}}})^{*}}\mathrel{{\equiv}}{L_{1}}(\hskip-0.5pt{v_{\hskip-0.5pt\text{s}}}\hskip-0.5pt)\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}{L_{2}}(\hskip-0.5pt{v_{\hskip-0.5pt\text{s}}}\hskip-0.5pt)\mathrel{{\equiv}}{({a}+{b})^{*}}
.

Lemma 30
.

The rules
LCoProof
n
are correct for
Mil
, for all
n\in{\rm Nature}
.
This statement also holds effectively:
Every derivation
\mathcal{D}
in
{\text{\sf Mil}}{+}{\text{LCoProof${}_{n}$}}

that consists of a bottommost instance of
LCoProof
n
, where
n\in{\rm Nature}
,
whose immediate subderivations are derivations in
Mil

can be transformed effectively into a derivation
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
in
Mil
that has the same conclusion as
\mathcal{D}
.

Proof 6.6
.

We let
n\in{\rm Nature}
.
In order to show correctness of the rule
LCoProof
n
for
Mil
,
we consider a derivation
\mathcal{D}
in
{\text{\sf Mil}}{+}{\text{LCoProof${}_{n}$}}

that has immediate subderivations
\mathcal{D}_{1}
, …,
\mathcal{D}_{n}
in
Mil
,
and that terminates with an instance
\iota
of
LCoProof
n
,
where
\Gamma\mathrel{{:=}}\left\{{g_{1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}h_{1},\ldots,g_{1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}h_{1}}\right\}\,
:

\mathcal{D}_{1}

g_{1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}h_{1}

…

\mathcal{D}_{n}

g_{n}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}h_{n}

\iota

LCoProof
n

e\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}f

and
e\mathrel{{\overset{\text{\scriptsize LLEE}}{=\joinrel=\joinrel=}_{{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}{+}{\Gamma}}}}f
holds as side-condition on the instance
\iota
of
LCoProof
n
.
Then there is a LLEE-witnessed coinductive proof
\mathcal{L\hskip-0.5ptC\hskip-0.75ptP}=\langle\underline{\mathcal{C}},\hskip 0.5ptL\rangle
of
e\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}f

over
{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}{+}{\Gamma}
.
We have to show that there is a derivation
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
in
Mil
with the same conclusion
e\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}f
.

Since
\mathcal{D}_{1}
, …,
\mathcal{D}_{n}
are derivations in
Mil
,
their conclusions in
\Gamma
are derivable in
Mil
.
This implies
{\text{\sf Mil}}{+}{\Gamma}\mathrel{{\precsim}}\text{\sf Mil}
.
It follows that
\mathcal{L\hskip-0.5ptC\hskip-0.75ptP}
is also a LLEE-witnessed coinductive proof of
e\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}f
over
Mil
,
that is, it holds:

e\mathrel{{\overset{\text{\scriptsize LLEE}}{=\joinrel=\joinrel=}_{\text{\sf Mil}}}}f\hskip 1.5pt{.}

From this we obtain
e\mathrel{{=_{\text{\sf Mil}}\hskip 1.0pt}}f
by applying Proposition
29
,
which guarantees a derivation
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
in
Mil
with conclusion
e\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}f
as desired.
The proof of Proposition
29
furthermore guarantees
that such a derivation
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
in
Mil
can be constructed effectively from
the coinductive proof of
e\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}f
over
{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}{+}{\Gamma}
(and hence over
Mil
)
and the derivations
\mathcal{D}_{1}
, …,
\mathcal{D}_{n}
in
Mil
.

Theorem 31
.

\text{\sf cMil}\mathrel{{\precsim}}\text{\sf Mil}
. Moreover, every derivation in
cMil
with conclusion
e\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}f

can be transformed effectively
into a derivation in
Mil
that has the same conclusion.

Proof 6.7
.

Due to Lemma
30
,
every rule
LCoProof
n
, for
n\in{\rm Nature}
, is correct for
Mil
.
Then by using Lemma
1
,
(i)
,
we find that each of these rules are admissible in
Mil
.
This means that
{\text{\sf Mil}}{+}{\text{LCoProof${}_{n}$}}\mathrel{\sim}\text{\sf Mil}
holds for all
n\in{\rm Nature}
,
which implies, with an argument by induction on the prooftree size of derivations in
{\text{\sf Mil}}{+}{\left\{{\text{LCoProof${}_{n}$}}\right\}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}}
,
that
{\text{\sf Mil}}{+}{\left\{{\text{LCoProof${}_{n}$}}\right\}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}}\mathrel{\sim}\text{\sf Mil}
holds as well.
With this statement we can now argue as follows:

cMil

\displaystyle{}={\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}{+}{\left\{{\text{LCoProof${}_{n}$}}\right\}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}}

(by Definition
5
)

\displaystyle{}\mathrel{{\sqsubseteq}}{({\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}{+}{\left\{{\text{LCoProof${}_{n}$}}\right\}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}})}{+}{\text{$\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt$}}\hskip 12.50002pt

(by extension via adding the rule
\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt
)

\displaystyle{}={({\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}{+}{\text{$\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt$}})}{+}{\left\{{\text{LCoProof${}_{n}$}}\right\}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}}

(by construing the same system differently)

\displaystyle{}={{\text{\sf Mil}}}{+}{\left\{{\text{LCoProof${}_{n}$}}\right\}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}}

(by Definition
2
)

\displaystyle{}\mathrel{\sim}\text{\sf Mil}

\displaystyle\text{(as argued above)}\hskip 1.5pt{.}

From this we obtain
\text{\sf cMil}\mathrel{{\precsim}}\text{\sf Mil}

in view of

({{\sqsubseteq}}\cdot{{\sim}})\>\subseteq\>({{\precsim}}\cdot{{\sim}})\>\subseteq\>{\precsim}
.

For demonstrating the effective transformation statement of the theorem
we use the transformation from the proof of the implication “
\Leftarrow
”
in Lemma
1
,
(i)
,
which states that correct rules are also admissible.
We have to show that every derivation
\mathcal{D}
in
cMil
can be transformed effectively
into a derivation
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
in
Mil
with the same conclusion.
In order to establish this statement by induction we prove it for all derivations
\mathcal{D}

in the extension
{\text{\sf cMil}}{+}{\text{$\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt$}}={{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}{+}{\left\{{\text{LCoProof${}_{n}$}}\right\}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}}}{+}{\text{$\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt$}}={\text{\sf Mil}}{+}{\left\{{\text{LCoProof${}_{n}$}}\right\}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}}

of
cMil
.

We proceed by induction on the number of instances of rules
LCoProof
n
, for
n\in{\rm Nature}
, in
\mathcal{D}
.
Let
\mathcal{D}
be a derivation
{{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}{+}{\left\{{\text{LCoProof${}_{n}$}}\right\}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}}}{+}{\text{$\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt$}}
.
If
\mathcal{D}
does not contain an instance of
LCoProof
n
with
n\in{\rm Nature}
,
then
\mathcal{D}
is already a derivation in
\text{\sf Mil}={\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}{+}{\text{$\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt$}}
,
and no further transformation is necessary.
Otherwise
\mathcal{D}
contains at least one instance of
LCoProof
n
with
n\in{\rm Nature}
.
We pick an instance
\iota
in
\mathcal{D}
of a rule
LCoProof
{}_{n_{0}}
with
n_{0}\in{\rm Nature}

that is topmost among the instances of the coinductive rule in
\mathcal{D}
, that is,
none of the immediate subderivations of the instance
\iota
in
\mathcal{D}

contains any instance of a rule
LCoProof
n
for
n\in{\rm Nature}
.
Let
\mathcal{D}_{0}
be the subderivation of
\mathcal{D}
that ends in
\iota
.
Since
\iota
is topmost,
all of the immediate subderivations of
\iota
and
\mathcal{D}_{0}
in
\mathcal{D}
are derivations in
\text{\sf Mil}={\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}{+}{\text{$\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt$}}
,
and
\mathcal{D}_{0}
is a derivation in
{{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}{+}{\text{LCoProof${}_{n_{0}}$}}}{+}{\text{$\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt$}}={\text{\sf Mil}}{+}{\text{LCoProof${}_{n_{0}}$}}
.
Therefore we can apply the effective part of Lemma
30

to the subderivation
\mathcal{D}_{0}
.
We obtain a derivation

in

with the same conclusion as
\iota

\mathcal{D}_{0}

Then by replacing

in

\tilde{\mathcal{D}}
in

that has the same conclusion as
\mathcal{D}
, but that has one instance of a coinductive rule less than
\mathcal{D}
.
Now we can apply the induction hypothesis to
\tilde{\mathcal{D}}

in order to effectively transform it in a derivation
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
in
Mil
that has the same conclusion as
\mathcal{D}
.
In this way we have performed the induction step.

Remark 34
.

While both of the statements
33
and
33

of Corollary
33

were important pieces of the puzzle for constructing the completeness proof for
Mil
as sketched in
[
Gra22a
]
,
neither of them yields such a proof directly.
This is because of the following two facts:
First, two bisimilar chart interpretations do not always have a guarded LLEE -
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart as their joint expansion along

\leftarrow

.
This can be demonstrated with the counterexample of the charts in Example 4.1 of
[
GF20a
]
.
As a consequence,
33
is sometimes not applicable.
Second,
two bisimilar chart interpretations do not in general have a guarded LLEE -
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart as their joint minimization along

\rightarrow

.
This is an easy consequence of the counterexample that is described in
[
Gra22a
, Sect. 6]
.
Therefore direct application of
33
is also excluded in some situations.

Yet more sophisticated applications of
33
and
33

can still turn out to be expedient.
Indeed,
a strengthening of
33
has helped us settle a subcase,
and a refinement of
33

has led to completeness proof for
Mil
as sketched in
[
Gra22a
]
.
In
[
GF20a
]
,
Fokkink and I have employed the stronger and more specific version
of the joint minimization idea
in Corollary
33
,
33
,
for a completeness proof of an adaptation
BBP
by Bergstra, Bethke, and Ponse of
Mil
to ‘
1
-free star expressions’
(without
1
, and with binary iteration instead of unary iteration).
Concretely,
we used that bisimilar chart interpretations of
1
-free star expressions have LLEE-charts as their bisimulation collapses
(and thus have guarded LLEE -
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-charts as joint minimizations).
The completeness proof for
Mil
as summarized in
[
Gra22a
]

has been built around a more involved argument that employs ‘crystallized’ LLEE -
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart approximations of the joint bisimulation collapse
of bisimilar chart interpretations.

7.
From Milner’s system to LLEE-witnessed coinductive proofs

In this section we develop a proof-theoretic interpretation of
Mil

in the subsystem
cMil
1
of
cMil
,
and hence also a proof-theoretic interpretation of
Mil
in
cMil
. Since
Mil
and
cMil
1
differ only by the fixed-point rule
\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt
(which is part of
Mil
, but not of
cMil
)
and the rule
LCoProof
1
(which is part of
cMil
1
, but not of
Mil
),
the crucial step for this proof transformation is to show that instances of
\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt
can be mimicked in
cMil
1
.

We will do so by showing that instances of
\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt
are derivable in
cMil
1
,
and in particular, can be mimicked by instances of
LCoProof
1
.
More precisely, we will show that every instance
\iota
of the fixed-point rule
\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt
of
Mil
can be mimicked by an instance of
LCoProof
1
that has the same premise and conclusion,
and that uses as its side-condition a LLEE-witnessed coinductive proof over
Mil
-
in which the premise of
\iota
may be used.
Still more explicitly, we show that every
\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt
-instance with premise
e=f\,{{\cdot}}\,e+g
such that
{f}{{\downarrow}}
and with conclusion
e=f^{*}\,{{\cdot}}\,g

gives rise to a coinductive proof of
e=f^{*}\,{{\cdot}}\,g
over
{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}{+}{\left\{{e=f\,{{\cdot}}\,e+g}\right\}}

with underlying
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{f^{*}\,{{\cdot}}\,g}\hskip-0.5pt)
and guarded LLEE-witness
{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}(\hskip-0.5pt{f^{*}\,{{\cdot}}\,g}\hskip-0.5pt)
.

We first illustrate this mimicking step by a concrete example (see Example
7
),
in order to motivate and convey the idea of this proof transformation.
It will be built on three auxiliary statements
(see after Example
7
) two of which we have shown already in Section
4
.
Subsequently we prove the remaining crucial auxiliary statement (Lemma
35
),
and then establish the transformation by showing that
\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt
is a derivable rule in
cMil
1

(Lemma
37
, using Lemma
36
).
Finally we use derivability of
\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt
in
cMil
1
in order to obtain the proof transformation from
Mil
to
cMil
1

(see Theorem
38
).

By examining the steps that we used in the example above,
we find that three main auxiliary statements were used
for the construction of a LLEE-witnessed coinductive proof
that mimics an instance of the fixed-point rule
\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt
by an instance of
LCoProof
1
.
In relation to an instance of
\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt
of the generic form as in Definition
2
,
these are the statements that, for all star expressions
e
,
f
, and
g
, it holds:

(a)

The
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart interpretation
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)
of a star expressions
e
is a guarded LLEE -
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart.

(b)

e
is the principal value of a
Mil
-
-provable solution of the
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart interpretation
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)
of
e
.

(c)

e
is the principal value of a
({\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}{+}{\left\{{e=f\,{{\cdot}}\,e+g}\right\}})
-provable solution
of the
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart interpretation
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{f^{*}\,{{\cdot}}\,g}\hskip-0.5pt)
of
f^{*}\,{{\cdot}}\,g
.

While
(a)
is guaranteed by Theorem
4
,
and
(b)
by Lemma
17
,
we are now going to justify the central statement
(c)
by proving the following lemma.

Lemma 35
.

Let
e,f,g\in{\textit{StExp}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)
with
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{f}{\ndownarrow}}
,
and let
\Gamma\mathrel{{:=}}\left\{{e={{f}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{e}}+{g}}\right\}
.
Then
e
is the principal value of a

({\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}{+}{\Gamma})
-provable solution of
the
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart interpretation
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{{{f^{*}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{g}}\hskip-0.5pt)
of
{f^{*}}{\,{{\cdot}}\,}{g}
.

Proof 7.1
.

First, it can be verified that the vertices of
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{f^{*}\cdot g}\hskip-0.5pt)
are of either of three forms:

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:1:lem:lem:mimic:RSPstar}{V\hskip-1.0pt}(\hskip-0.5pt{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{f^{*}\cdot g}\hskip-0.5pt)}\hskip-0.5pt)=\left\{{f^{*}\cdot g}\right\}\cup\bigl\{{(F\,{{\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}\varstar}}\,f^{*})\cdot g}\mathrel{\big|}{F\in{{\underline{\partial}^{+}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{f}\hskip-0.5pt)}\bigr\}\cup\left\{{G}\mathrel{|}{G\in{{\underline{\partial}^{+}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{g}\hskip-0.5pt)}\right\}\hskip 1.5pt{,}

(23)

where
{{\underline{\partial}^{+}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{f}\hskip-0.5pt)
means the set of iterated 1-derivatives of
f
according to the TSS in Def.
4
.

This facilitates to define a function
s\mathrel{:}{V\hskip-1.0pt}(\hskip-0.5pt{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{f^{*}\cdot g}\hskip-0.5pt)}\hskip-0.5pt)\to{\textit{StExp}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)

on
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{f^{*}\cdot g}\hskip-0.5pt)
by:

\displaystyle{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{f^{*}\,{{\cdot}}\,g}\hskip-0.5pt)

\displaystyle{}\mathrel{{:=}}e\hskip 1.5pt{,}

\displaystyle{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{(F\,{{\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}\varstar}}\,f^{*})\,{{\cdot}}\,g}\hskip-0.5pt)

\displaystyle{}\mathrel{{:=}}{\pi}(\hskip-0.5pt{F}\hskip-0.5pt)\,{{\cdot}}\,e\hskip 1.5pt{,}

(for
F\in{{\underline{\partial}^{+}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{f}\hskip-0.5pt)
),

\displaystyle{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{G}\hskip-0.5pt)

\displaystyle{}\mathrel{{:=}}{\pi}(\hskip-0.5pt{G}\hskip-0.5pt)

(for
G\in{{\underline{\partial}^{+}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{g}\hskip-0.5pt)
),

We will show that
s
is a
({\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}{+}{\Gamma})
-provable solution of
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{f^{*}\cdot g}\hskip-0.5pt)
.
Instead of verifying the correctness conditions for
s
for list representations of transitions,
we will argue more loosely with sums over action 1-derivatives sets
{\textit{\lx@text@underline{A\hskip-0.25pt$\partial$}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{H}\hskip-0.5pt)
of stacked star expressions
H

where such sums are only well-defined up to
ACI
. Due to
\text{\sf ACI}\mathrel{{\sqsubseteq}}\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}
such an argumentation
is possible. Specifically we will demonstrate, for all
E\in{V\hskip-1.0pt}(\hskip-0.5pt{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{f^{*}\cdot g}\hskip-0.5pt)}\hskip-0.5pt)
,
that
s
is a
({\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}{+}{\Gamma})
-provable solution at
E
, that is, that it holds:

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:2:lem:lem:mimic:RSPstar}{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{E}\hskip-0.5pt)\mathrel{{=_{{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}{+}{\Gamma}}\hskip 1.0pt}}{\tau_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{E}\hskip-0.5pt)}}(\hskip-0.5pt{E}\hskip-0.5pt)+\hskip-16.14578pt\sum_{\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{a}},\hskip 0.5ptE^{\prime}\rangle\in{\textit{\lx@text@underline{A\hskip-0.25pt$\partial$}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{E}\hskip-0.5pt)}\hskip-16.14578pt{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{a}}\,{{\cdot}}\,{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{E^{\prime}}\hskip-0.5pt)\hskip 1.5pt{,}

(24)

where by the sum on the right-hand side we mean an arbitrary representative
of the
ACI
equivalence class of star expressions that is described by the sum expression of this form.

For showing (
24
),
we distinguish the three cases of vertices
E\in{V\hskip-1.0pt}(\hskip-0.5pt{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{f^{*}\,{{\cdot}}\,g}\hskip-0.5pt)}\hskip-0.5pt)

according to (
23
),
that is,
E\mathrel{{\equiv}}f^{*}\,{{\cdot}}\,g
,

E\mathrel{{\equiv}}(F\,{{\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}\varstar}}\,f^{*})\,{{\cdot}}\,g
for some
F\in{{\underline{\partial}^{+}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{f}\hskip-0.5pt)
,
and
E\mathrel{{\equiv}}G
for some
G\in{{\underline{\partial}^{+}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{g}\hskip-0.5pt)
.
We will see that the assumption
\Gamma
will only be needed for the treatment of the first case.

In the first case, we consider
E\mathrel{{\equiv}}f^{*}\,{{\cdot}}\,g
.
We find by Lemma
14

(or by inspecting the TSS in Definition
4
), and in view of (
23
):

\displaystyle{\textit{\lx@text@underline{A\hskip-0.25pt$\partial$}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{f^{*}\,{{\cdot}}\,g}\hskip-0.5pt)

\displaystyle{}=\left\{{\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{a}},\hskip 0.5pt(F\,{{\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}\varstar}}\,f^{*})\,{{\cdot}}\,g\rangle}\mathrel{|}{\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{a}},\hskip 0.5ptF\rangle\in{\textit{\lx@text@underline{A\hskip-0.25pt$\partial$}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{f}\hskip-0.5pt)}\right\}\cup{\textit{\lx@text@underline{A\hskip-0.25pt$\partial$}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{g}\hskip-0.5pt)\hskip 1.5pt{,}

(25)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{{\underline{\partial}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{f^{*}\,{{\cdot}}\,g}\hskip-0.5pt)&{}=\left\{{(F\,{{\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}\varstar}}\,f^{*})\,{{\cdot}}\,g}\mathrel{|}{F\in{{\underline{\partial}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{f}\hskip-0.5pt)}\right\}\cup{{\underline{\partial}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{g}\hskip-0.5pt)\\
&{}\subseteq\left\{{(F\,{{\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}\varstar}}\,f^{*})\,{{\cdot}}\,g}\mathrel{|}{F\in{{\underline{\partial}^{+}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{f}\hskip-0.5pt)}\right\}\cup{{\underline{\partial}^{+}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{g}\hskip-0.5pt)\subseteq{V\hskip-1.0pt}(\hskip-0.5pt{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{f^{*}\,{{\cdot}}\,g}\hskip-0.5pt)}\hskip-0.5pt)\hskip 1.5pt{.}\end{split}

(26)

Then (
26
) guarantees that
s
is defined for all partial 1-derivatives of
E\mathrel{{\equiv}}f^{*}\cdot g
. With this knowledge we can argue as follows:

\displaystyle{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{E}\hskip-0.5pt)

\displaystyle\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{\equiv}}$}\>{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{f^{*}\,{{\cdot}}\,g}\hskip-0.5pt)\qquad\quad\text{(by $E\mathrel{{\equiv}}f^{*}\,{{\cdot}}\,g$)}

\displaystyle\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{\equiv}}$}\>e\phantom{{}\cdot e+g}\qquad\quad\text{(by the definition of $s$)}

\displaystyle\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{=_{{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}{+}{\Gamma}}\hskip 1.0pt}}$}\>f\,{{\cdot}}\,e+g\qquad\quad\text{(since $\Gamma=\left\{{e=f\,{{\cdot}}\,e+g}\right\}$)}

\displaystyle\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}$}\>\Big({\tau_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{f}\hskip-0.5pt)}}(\hskip-0.5pt{f}\hskip-0.5pt)+\hskip-16.14578pt\sum_{\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{a}},\hskip 0.5ptF\rangle\in{\textit{\lx@text@underline{A\hskip-0.25pt$\partial$}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{f}\hskip-0.5pt)}\hskip-15.0694pt{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{a}}\,{{\cdot}}\,{\pi}(\hskip-0.5pt{F}\hskip-0.5pt)\Big)\,{{\cdot}}\,e+\Big({\tau_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{g}\hskip-0.5pt)}}(\hskip-0.5pt{g}\hskip-0.5pt)+\hskip-16.14578pt\sum_{\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{a}},\hskip 0.5ptG\rangle\in{\textit{\lx@text@underline{A\hskip-0.25pt$\partial$}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{g}\hskip-0.5pt)}\hskip-15.0694pt{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{a}}\,{{\cdot}}\,{\pi}(\hskip-0.5pt{G}\hskip-0.5pt)\Big)

(by using Lemma

\displaystyle\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}$}\>\Big({\tau_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{f}\hskip-0.5pt)}}(\hskip-0.5pt{f}\hskip-0.5pt)\,{{\cdot}}\,e+\hskip-16.14578pt\sum_{\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{a}},\hskip 0.5ptF\rangle\in{\textit{\lx@text@underline{A\hskip-0.25pt$\partial$}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{f}\hskip-0.5pt)}\hskip-15.0694pt{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{a}}\,{{\cdot}}\,({\pi}(\hskip-0.5pt{F}\hskip-0.5pt)\,{{\cdot}}\,e)\Big)+\Big({\tau_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{g}\hskip-0.5pt)}}(\hskip-0.5pt{g}\hskip-0.5pt)+\hskip-16.14578pt\sum_{\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{a}},\hskip 0.5ptG\rangle\in{\textit{\lx@text@underline{A\hskip-0.25pt$\partial$}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{g}\hskip-0.5pt)}\hskip-15.0694pt{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{a}}\,{{\cdot}}\,{\pi}(\hskip-0.5pt{G}\hskip-0.5pt)\Big)

\displaystyle\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}$}\>\Big(\hskip-16.14578pt\sum_{\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{a}},\hskip 0.5ptF\rangle\in{\textit{\lx@text@underline{A\hskip-0.25pt$\partial$}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{f}\hskip-0.5pt)}\hskip-15.0694pt{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{a}}\,{{\cdot}}\,({\pi}(\hskip-0.5pt{F}\hskip-0.5pt)\,{{\cdot}}\,e)\Big)+\Bigl({\tau_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{f^{*}\,{{\cdot}}\,g}\hskip-0.5pt)}}(\hskip-0.5pt{f^{*}\,{{\cdot}}\,g}\hskip-0.5pt)+\hskip-16.14578pt\sum_{\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{a}},\hskip 0.5ptG\rangle\in{\textit{\lx@text@underline{A\hskip-0.25pt$\partial$}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{g}\hskip-0.5pt)}\hskip-15.0694pt{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{a}}\,{{\cdot}}\,{\pi}(\hskip-0.5pt{G}\hskip-0.5pt)\Bigr)

,
by using axiom (

and
{\tau_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{f^{*}\,{{\cdot}}\,g}\hskip-0.5pt)}}(\hskip-0.5pt{f^{*}\,{{\cdot}}\,g}\hskip-0.5pt)\mathrel{{\equiv}}{\tau_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{g}\hskip-0.5pt)}}(\hskip-0.5pt{g}\hskip-0.5pt)

\displaystyle\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{=_{\text{\sf ACI}}\hskip 1.0pt}}$}\>{\tau_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{f^{*}\,{{\cdot}}\,g}\hskip-0.5pt)}}(\hskip-0.5pt{f^{*}\,{{\cdot}}\,g}\hskip-0.5pt)+\Big(\hskip-16.14578pt\sum_{\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{a}},\hskip 0.5ptF\rangle\in{\textit{\lx@text@underline{A\hskip-0.25pt$\partial$}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{f}\hskip-0.5pt)}\hskip-15.0694pt{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{a}}\,{{\cdot}}\,({s}(\hskip-0.5pt{(F\,{{\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}\varstar}}\,f^{*})\cdot g}\hskip-0.5pt))\Big)+\hskip-16.14578pt\sum_{\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{a}},\hskip 0.5ptG\rangle\in{\textit{\lx@text@underline{A\hskip-0.25pt$\partial$}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{g}\hskip-0.5pt)}\hskip-15.0694pt{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{a}}\,{{\cdot}}\,{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{G}\hskip-0.5pt)

, axioms (

\displaystyle\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{=_{\text{\sf ACI}}\hskip 1.0pt}}$}\>{\tau_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{E}\hskip-0.5pt)}}(\hskip-0.5pt{E}\hskip-0.5pt)+\hskip-24.75687pt\sum_{\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{a}},\hskip 0.5ptE^{\prime}\rangle\in{\textit{\lx@text@underline{A\hskip-0.25pt$\partial$}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{f^{*}\cdot g}\hskip-0.5pt)={\textit{\lx@text@underline{A\hskip-0.25pt$\partial$}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{E}\hskip-0.5pt)}\hskip-23.68048pt{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{a}}\,{{\cdot}}\,{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{E^{\prime}}\hskip-0.5pt)

{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}{+}{\Gamma}
,

({\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}{+}{\Gamma})

In the second case we consider

{\tau_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{E}\hskip-0.5pt)}}(\hskip-0.5pt{E}\hskip-0.5pt)\mathrel{{\equiv}}{\tau_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{(F\,{{\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}\varstar}}\,f^{*})\,{{\cdot}}\,g}\hskip-0.5pt)}}(\hskip-0.5pt{(F\,{{\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}\varstar}}\,f^{*})\,{{\cdot}}\,g}\hskip-0.5pt)\mathrel{{\equiv}}0

\displaystyle{}=\left\{{\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{a}},\hskip 0.5pt(F^{\prime}\,{{\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}\varstar}}\,f^{*})\,{{\cdot}}\,g\rangle}\mathrel{|}{\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{a}},\hskip 0.5ptF^{\prime}\rangle\in{\textit{\lx@text@underline{A\hskip-0.25pt$\partial$}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{F}\hskip-0.5pt)}\right\}\hskip 1.5pt{,}

\displaystyle\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{\equiv}}$}\>{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{(F\,{{\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}\varstar}}\,f^{*})\,{{\cdot}}\,g}\hskip-0.5pt)
