.
With this knowledge we can argue as follows:

\displaystyle{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{E}\hskip-0.5pt)

\displaystyle\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{\equiv}}$}\>{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{G}\hskip-0.5pt)

(by
E\mathrel{{\equiv}}G
)

\displaystyle\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{\equiv}}$}\>{\pi}(\hskip-0.5pt{G}\hskip-0.5pt)

(by the definition of
s
)

\displaystyle\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}$}\>{\tau_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{G}\hskip-0.5pt)}}(\hskip-0.5pt{G}\hskip-0.5pt)+\hskip-16.14578pt\sum_{\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{a}},\hskip 0.5ptG^{\prime}\rangle\in{\textit{\lx@text@underline{A\hskip-0.25pt$\partial$}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{G}\hskip-0.5pt)}\hskip-15.0694pt{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{a}}\,{{\cdot}}\,{\pi}(\hskip-0.5pt{G^{\prime}}\hskip-0.5pt)

(by using Lemma
16
)

\displaystyle\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{=_{\text{\sf ACI}}\hskip 1.0pt}}$}\>{\tau_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{G}\hskip-0.5pt)}}(\hskip-0.5pt{G}\hskip-0.5pt)+\hskip-16.14578pt\sum_{\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{a}},\hskip 0.5ptG^{\prime}\rangle\in{\textit{\lx@text@underline{A\hskip-0.25pt$\partial$}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{G}\hskip-0.5pt)}\hskip-15.0694pt{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{a}}\,{{\cdot}}\,{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{G^{\prime}}\hskip-0.5pt)

(by the definition of
s
)

\displaystyle\;\,\parbox[t]{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{=_{\text{\sf ACI}}\hskip 1.0pt}}$}\>{\tau_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{E}\hskip-0.5pt)}}(\hskip-0.5pt{E}\hskip-0.5pt)+\hskip-16.14578pt\sum_{\langle{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{a}},\hskip 0.5ptE^{\prime}\rangle\in{\textit{\lx@text@underline{A\hskip-0.25pt$\partial$}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{E}\hskip-0.5pt)}\hskip-15.0694pt{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}\underline{a}}\,{{\cdot}}\,{s}(\hskip-0.5pt{E^{\prime}}\hskip-0.5pt)

(by
E\mathrel{{\equiv}}G
).

Due to
\text{\sf ACI}\mathrel{{\sqsubseteq}}\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}\mathrel{{\sqsubseteq}}{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}{+}{\Gamma}

this chain of equalities verifies (
24
) also in this case.

By having established (
24
) for the, according to (
23
), three possible forms of stacked star expressions
that are vertices of
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{f^{*}\,{{\cdot}}\,g}\hskip-0.5pt)
, we have shown
that
s
is indeed a
({\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}{+}{\Gamma})
-provable solution of
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{f^{*}\,{{\cdot}}\,g}\hskip-0.5pt)
.

After having proved statement
(c)
,
we can combine the statements
(a)
,
(b)
, and
(c)

as above in order construct LLEE-witnessed coinductive proofs
with which instances of
\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt
can be mimicked by instances of
LCoProof
1
.
This leads us to Lemma
36
below,
and, as it can show derivability of
\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt
in
cMil
1
, to Lemma
37
.

Lemma 36
.

Let
e,f,g\in{\textit{StExp}}(\hskip-0.5pt{\mathit{A}}\hskip-0.5pt)
with
{f}{\ndownarrow}
,
and let
\Gamma\mathrel{{:=}}\left\{{e\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}{{f}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{e}}+{g}}\right\}
.
Then it holds that

e\mathrel{{\overset{\text{\scriptsize LLEE}}{=\joinrel=\joinrel=}_{({\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}{+}{\Gamma})}}}{{f^{*}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{g}
.

Proof 7.2
.

First, there is a
{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}{+}{\Gamma}
-provable solution

s_{1}
of
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{{{f^{*}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{g}}\hskip-0.5pt)
with

{s_{1}}(\hskip-0.5pt{{{f^{*}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{g}}\hskip-0.5pt)\mathrel{{\equiv}}e
,
due to Lemma
35
.
Second, there is a
Mil
-
-provable solution
s_{2}
of
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{{{f^{*}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{g}}\hskip-0.5pt)

with
{s_{2}}(\hskip-0.5pt{{{f^{*}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{g}}\hskip-0.5pt)\mathrel{{\equiv}}{{f^{*}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{g}
,
due to Lemma
17
.
Then
\langle{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{f^{*}{\,{{\cdot}}\,}g}\hskip-0.5pt),\hskip 0.5ptL\rangle

with
{L}(\hskip-0.5pt{v}\hskip-0.5pt)\mathrel{{:=}}{s_{1}}(\hskip-0.5pt{v}\hskip-0.5pt)\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}{s_{2}}(\hskip-0.5pt{v}\hskip-0.5pt)

for all
v\in{V\hskip-1.0pt}(\hskip-0.5pt{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{f^{*}{\,{{\cdot}}\,}g}\hskip-0.5pt)}\hskip-0.5pt)

is a LLEE-witnessed coinductive proof
of
e\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}f^{*}{\,{{\cdot}}\,}g

over
{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}{+}{\Gamma}
,
because
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{{{f^{*}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{g}}\hskip-0.5pt)

has the guarded LLEE-witness
{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}(\hskip-0.5pt{{{f^{*}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{g}}\hskip-0.5pt)
by Theorem
4
.

We note that the equation in the set
\Gamma
in the assumption of Lemma
36

does not need to be sound semantically.
Therefore it was crucial for the formulation of this lemma that
we did not require proof systems
{\mathcal{S}}
to be sound with respect to
\mathrel{{=_{\scriptscriptstyle\llbracket{\cdot}\rrbracket_{\hskip-0.5ptP}}}}

for the definition of coinductive proofs in Definition
5
.
Indeed we have done so there in order to be able to formulate this lemma,
which states that also instances of the fixed-point rule
\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt
with premises that are not semantically sound can be mimicked by appropriate coinductive proofs.

Lemma 37
.

\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt
is a derivable rule in
cMil
.

Proof 7.3
.

Every instance
\iota
of
\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt
can be replaced by a mimicking derivation
\mathcal{D}_{\iota}
in
cMil
1

according to the following step,
where
{f}{\ndownarrow}
holds as the side-condition of the instance of
\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt
:

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:prf:lem:RSPstar:derivable:coindmilnersysone}\begin{aligned} \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 11.53116pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 6.53937pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{e}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}\mathord{}$}\hbox{${{f}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{e}}+{g}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip-0.94107pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 1.6509pt\hbox{{$\iota$}\hskip 3.0pt}\hbox to49.52435pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 2.94106pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt$\text{$\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt$}$}}\vskip-0.94106pt\hbox{\hskip 6.53937pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathord{e}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}\mathord{}$}\hbox{${{f^{*}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{g}\rule[-5.0pt]{0.0pt}{7.5pt}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\qquad\Longmapsto\qquad\begin{aligned} \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 10.50186pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$e\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}{{f}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{e}}+{g}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\vskip-1.97035pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to49.52435pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 1.97035pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt\text{LCoProof${}_{1}$}}}\vskip-1.97035pt\hbox{\hskip 3.91843pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$e\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}{{f^{*}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{g}\rule[-5.0pt]{0.0pt}{7.5pt}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}

(31)

Here the side-condition
e\mathrel{{\overset{\text{\scriptsize LLEE}}{=\joinrel=\joinrel=}_{({\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}{+}{\left\{{e\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}{{f}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{e}}+{g}}\right\}})}}}{{f^{*}}}\mathrel{{\cdot}}{g}

of the instance of
LCoProof
1
in the derivation
\mathcal{D}_{\iota}
on the right is guaranteed by Lemma
36
.

We now can show the main result of this section,
the proof transformation from
Mil
to
cMil
1
.
We obtain this transformation by using derivability of
\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt
in
cMil
1
as stated by this lemma,
and by combining basic proof-theoretic transformations
that eliminate derivable, and hence correct and admissible, rules from derivations
as described in the proof of Lemma
1
.

Theorem 38
.

\text{\sf Mil}\mathrel{{\precsim}}{\text{{\sf cMil}${}_{1}$}}
.
What is more,
every derivation in
Mil
with conclusion
e\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}f

can be transformed effectively
into a derivation with conclusion
e\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}f
in
cMil
1
.

Proof 7.4
.

Due to Lemma
37
,

\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt
is a derivable rule in
cMil
1
.
Then by Lemma
1
,
(ii)
,

\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt
is also an admissible rule in
cMil
1
,
and hence
{{\text{{\sf cMil}${}_{1}$}}}{+}{\text{$\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt$}}\mathrel{\sim}{\text{{\sf cMil}${}_{1}$}}
holds.
With that we can now argue as follows:

\displaystyle\text{\sf Mil}={\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}{+}{\text{$\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt$}}

\displaystyle{}\mathrel{{\sqsubseteq}}{({\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}{+}{\text{$\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt$}})}{+}{\text{LCoProof${}_{1}$}}

\displaystyle{}={({\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}{+}{\text{LCoProof${}_{1}$}})}{+}{\text{$\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt$}}={{\text{{\sf cMil}${}_{1}$}}}{+}{\text{$\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt$}}\mathrel{\sim}{\text{{\sf cMil}${}_{1}$}}\hskip 1.5pt{.}

From this we can infer
\text{\sf Mil}\mathrel{{\precsim}}{\text{{\sf cMil}${}_{1}$}}
,
because
{\sqsubseteq}
implies
{\precsim}
, and
{{\precsim}}\cdot{{\sim}}\subseteq{\sim}
.
That every derivation
\mathcal{D}
in
Mil
can be transformed effectively
into a derivation
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
in
cMil
with the same conclusion
follows from derivability of
\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt
in
cMil
:
then in
\mathcal{D}
every instance of
\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt
can be replaced by a corresponding instance of
LCoProof
1

as described in (
31
)
of the proof of Lemma
37

with as result a derivation
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
in
cMil
1
with the same conclusion as
\mathcal{D}
.
This argument instantiates the implication from rule derivability to rule admissibility,
and the transformations explained
in the proof of Lemma
1
,
(i)
and
(ii)
,
specifically (
1
).

{exa}

In Figure
2

we provided a first illustration for translating
an instance of the fixed-point rule into a coinductive proof in Figure
2
on page
2
.
Specifically, we mimicked the instance
\iota
(see below) of the fixed-point rule
\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt
in Milner’s system
\text{\sf Mil}={\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}{+}{\text{$\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt$}}

by a coinductive proof over
{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}{+}{\left\{{\text{premise of $\iota$}}\right\}}
with LLEE-witness
{\hskip 0.2pt\hat{\hskip-0.75pt\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\hskip-0.75pt}(\hskip-0.5pt{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}f^{*}}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}0}}\hskip-0.5pt)
.

The correctness conditions that have to be satisfied for the right-hand sides
in order to recognize this prooftree as a LLEE-witnessed coinductive proof
{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}{+}{\left\{{\text{premise of $\iota$}}\right\}}

are the same as those
that we have verified for the right-hand sides of the coinductive proof over
Mil
-
with the same LLEE-witness in Example
5
.
Note that the premise of
\iota
is not used for the correctness conditions of the right-hand sides.
The correctness condition for the left-hand side
{\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}{e_{0}^{*}}\,{{\cdot}}\,0}
at the bottom vertex of
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}f^{*}}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}0}}\hskip-0.5pt)

can be verified as follows, now making use of the premise of the considered instance
\iota
of
\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt
:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}{e_{0}^{*}}\,{{\cdot}}\,0}

\displaystyle\;\;{\mathrel{{=_{\left\{{\text{premise of $\iota$}}\right\}}\hskip 1.0pt}}}\;\;{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}f}\,{{\cdot}}\,({\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}{e_{0}^{*}}\,{{\cdot}}\,0})+{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}0}

\displaystyle\;\;\parbox{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}$}\;\;(a\,{{\cdot}}\,(a+b)+b)\,{{\cdot}}\,({\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}{e_{0}^{*}}\,{{\cdot}}\,0})

\displaystyle\;\;\parbox{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}$}\;\;(a\,{{\cdot}}\,(a+b))\,{{\cdot}}\,({\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}{e_{0}^{*}}\,{{\cdot}}\,0})+b\,{{\cdot}}\,({\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}{e_{0}^{*}}\,{{\cdot}}\,0})

\displaystyle\;\;\parbox{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}$}\;\;a\,{{\cdot}}\,((a+b)\,{{\cdot}}\,({\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}{e_{0}^{*}}\,{{\cdot}}\,0}))+b\,{{\cdot}}\,(1\,{{\cdot}}\,({\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}{e_{0}^{*}}\,{{\cdot}}\,0}))

\displaystyle\;\;\parbox{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}$}\;\;a\,{{\cdot}}\,((1\,{{\cdot}}\,(a+b))\,{{\cdot}}\,({\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}{e_{0}^{*}}\,{{\cdot}}\,0}))+b\,{{\cdot}}\,(1\,{{\cdot}}\,({\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}{e_{0}^{*}}\,{{\cdot}}\,0}))

Together this yields the provable equation:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}{e_{0}^{*}}\,{{\cdot}}\,0}

\displaystyle\;\;{\mathrel{{=_{{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}{+}{\left\{{\text{premise of $\iota$}}\right\}}}\hskip 1.0pt}}}\;\;a\,{{\cdot}}\,((1\,{{\cdot}}\,(a+b))\,{{\cdot}}\,({\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}{e_{0}^{*}}\,{{\cdot}}\,0}))+b\,{{\cdot}}\,(1\,{{\cdot}}\,({\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}{e_{0}^{*}}\,{{\cdot}}\,0}))\hskip 1.5pt{,}

which demonstrates the correctness condition for
the left-hand side
{\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}{e_{0}^{*}}\,{{\cdot}}\,0}
at the bottom vertex of
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}f^{*}}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}0}}\hskip-0.5pt)
.
The correctness condition for the left-hand side
a\,{{\cdot}}\,((1\,{{\cdot}}\,(a+b))
at the top left vertex of
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}f^{*}}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}0}}\hskip-0.5pt)

can be verified without using the premise of
\iota
as follows:

\displaystyle((1\,{{\cdot}}\,(a+b))\,{{\cdot}}\,({\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}{e_{0}^{*}}\,{{\cdot}}\,0})

\displaystyle{}\mathrel{\parbox{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}$}}((a+b)\,{{\cdot}}\,({\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}{e_{0}^{*}}\,{{\cdot}}\,0})\mathrel{\parbox{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}$}}a\,{{\cdot}}\,({\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}{e_{0}^{*}}\,{{\cdot}}\,0})+b\,{{\cdot}}\,({\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}{e_{0}^{*}}\,{{\cdot}}\,0})

\displaystyle{}\mathrel{\parbox{0.0pt}{$\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}$}}a\,{{\cdot}}\,(1\,{{\cdot}}\,({\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}{e_{0}^{*}}\,{{\cdot}}\,0}))+b\,{{\cdot}}\,(1\,{{\cdot}}\,({\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}{e_{0}^{*}}\,{{\cdot}}\,0}))\hskip 1.5pt{.}

Finally, the correctness condition of the left-hand side
1\,{{\cdot}}\,({\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}{e_{0}^{*}}\,{{\cdot}}\,0})
at the right upper vertex of
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}f^{*}}\,{{\cdot}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}0}}\hskip-0.5pt)

can be obtained by an application of the axiom (
{\text{id}_{\text{l}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{{\cdot}}\hskip-0.5pt)
) only.

We close this section by giving an example that provides an additional sanity check
for the proof transformation from
Mil
to
cMil
that we developed above.
The example below shows that the construction of a LLEE-witnessed coinductive proof fails
for an inference that is
not
an instance of the fixed-point rule
\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt
because the side-condition is violated.

Non-Example 39
.

We consider the following (not semantically valid) inference
according to a (semantically unsound) extension
\textrm{\sout{RSP}}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt
of the fixed-point rule
\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt
that does not require the guardedness side-condition
{f}{\ndownarrow}
(see Definition
2
):

\mathord{\overbrace{(a+c)^{*}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}e}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}\mathord{}
\overbrace{(a+1)}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}f}}\cdot\overbrace{(a+c)^{*}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}e}}+\overbrace{1}^{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}g}}

\textrm{\sout{RSP}}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt

(but not
\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt
since
f\mathrel{{\equiv}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(a+1)}}{{\downarrow}}}
)

\mathord{(a+c)^{*}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}\mathord{}
\underbrace{(a+1)^{*}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}f^{*}}}\cdot\underbrace{1}_{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}g}}

The premise is semantically valid by Proposition
2
because it is provable in
Mil
-
:

\displaystyle(a+c)^{*}

\displaystyle\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}1+(a+c)\cdot(a+c)^{*}\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}1+a\cdot(a+c)^{*}+c\cdot(a+c)^{*}

\displaystyle\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}1+a\cdot(a+c)^{*}+1+a\cdot(a+c)^{*}+c\cdot(a+c)^{*}

\displaystyle\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}1+a\cdot(a+c)^{*}+(a+c)^{*}\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}1+a\cdot(a+c)^{*}+1\cdot(a+c)^{*}

\displaystyle\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}1+(a+1)\cdot(a+c)^{*}\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}(a+1)\cdot(a+c)^{*}+1\hskip 1.5pt{.}

But the conclusion of the inference is obviously not valid semantically,
because its left-hand side can iterate
{c}
-transitions, while its right-hand side does not permit
{c}
-transitions.

{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}f^{*}}}\hskip-0.5pt)
1\,{\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}\varstar}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}f^{*}}
\underbrace{(a+1)^{*}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}f^{*}}}
a
{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}[1]}}
1\,{\cdot}\,\overbrace{{\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}e}}^{(a+c)^{*}}
\underbrace{\underbrace{(a+1)}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}f}}\,{\cdot}\,\underbrace{(a+c)^{*}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}e}}+\underbrace{1}_{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}g}}}_{\>=\>{\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}e}\;\;\text{(by rule assumption)}}
a
{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}[1]}}
\boldsymbol{\times}
(1\,{\cdot}\,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}f^{*}})\,{\cdot}\,{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}g}
\underbrace{(a+1)^{*}}_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}f^{*}}}\,{\cdot}\,\underbrace{1}_{{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}g}}
a
{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,1}[1]}}

Figure 17.

Failure of the construction a
Mil
-
-provable solution
for a coinductive proof that would mimic an instance of a fixed-point rule like
\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt
without side-condition.

Now by mechanically performing the same construction of a coinductive proof
as we illustrated it in Example
7
and in Example
7
, we obtain the LLEE -
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{f^{*}}\hskip-0.5pt)
and the star expression assignments to it as in Figure
17
.
There we recognize that, while

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}f^{*}}\,{{\cdot}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}g}

is the principal value of a
Mil
-
-provable solution of
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}f^{*}}\,{{\cdot}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}g}}\hskip-0.5pt)
(see on the right),
we have not obtained a
{\text{\sf Mil}}{+}{\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}e}={\color[rgb]{1,0,0}f}\,{{\cdot}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}e}+{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}g}}\right\}}
-provable
solution of
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}(\hskip-0.5pt{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}f^{*}}\,{{\cdot}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}g}}\hskip-0.5pt)

with principal value
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}f}\,{{\cdot}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.82,0.41,0.12}e}+{\color[rgb]{0.13,0.55,0.13}g}
(see in the middle).
This is because the correctness condition is violated at the bottom vertex,
because
(a+1)\,{{\cdot}}\,(a+c)^{*}+1\mathrel{{=_{\text{\text{\sf Mil}${}^{\boldsymbol{-}}$}}\hskip 1.0pt}}1+a\,{{\cdot}}\,(1\,{{\cdot}}\,(a+c)^{*})

does not hold: otherwise it would have to be semantically valid by Proposition
2
,
but it is not, because only the left-hand side permits an initial
{c}
-transition.

Therefore the construction does not give rise to a LLEE-witnessed coinductive proof of the (not semantically valid) formal equation

(a+c)^{*}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}(a+1)\cdot(a+c)^{*}+1
.

Based on the proof transformations that we have developed in this section and earlier in Section
6
,
we now obtain our main result. It justifies that we called the proof system
cMil
a reformulation of Milner’s system
Mil
.

Theorem 40
.

\text{\sf Mil}\mathrel{\sim}{\text{{\sf cMil}${}_{1}$}}\mathrel{\sim}\text{\sf cMil}\mathrel{\sim}\text{\sf C\hskip-0.5ptL\hskip-0.5ptC}
,
i.e. these proof systems are theorem-equivalent.

Proof 7.5
.

By combining the statements of
Theorem
38
,
Lemma
23
,
and Theorem
31

we obtain the theorem-subsumption statements

\text{\sf Mil}\mathrel{{\precsim}}{\text{{\sf cMil}${}_{1}$}}\mathrel{{\precsim}}\text{\sf cMil}\>(\mathrel{\sim}\text{\sf C\hskip-0.5ptL\hskip-0.5ptC})\mathrel{{\precsim}}\text{\sf Mil}
,
which together justify the theorem-equivalence of
Mil
with each of
cMil
1
,
cMil
, and
CLC
.

8.
Summary and Conclusion

We set out on a proof-theoretic investigation of the problem of whether Milner’s system
Mil
is complete
for process-semantics equality
\mathrel{{=_{\scriptscriptstyle\llbracket{\cdot}\rrbracket_{\hskip-0.5ptP}}}}
on regular expressions
(which Milner calls ‘star expressions’ for disambiguation when interpreted according to the process semantics).
Specifically we aimed at characterizing the derivational power that the fixed-point rule
\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt
in
Mil

adds to its purely equational part
Mil
-
.

In order to define a substitute for the rule
\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt
we based ours on two
results that we have obtained earlier (the first in joint work with Fokkink):

(S/U)

Linear specifications of the shape
4

(S)

\leftrightarrow

).

(U)

uniquely solvable by star expressions modulo provability in

(and mod.

\leftrightarrow

).

These statements were crucial steps
in the completeness proof
[

,

by Fokkink and myself
for a tailored restriction

of
Mil
to ‘
1

(IV)
1

{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e}\hskip-0.5pt)

Hereby

Now in order to obtain results for

we have generalized these statements to guarded

(S/U)

(U)

(SU)

and

, and Lemma

, respectively.

These statements motivated us to define ‘coinductive proofs’ as pairs of solutions for guarded LLEE -
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-charts,
because every pair of
Mil
-
-provable, or
Mil
-provable solutions can be proved equal in
Mil
.
On the basis of this idea we developed the following concepts and results
(we emphasize earlier introduced terminology again here below):

1

LLEE
-witnessed coinductive proof over an equational proof system
{\mathcal{S}}
(Definition
5
):
We defined a coinductive proof as a weakly guarded LLEE -
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-chart
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
whose vertices are labeled by equations
between the values of two
{\mathcal{S}}
-provable solutions of
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
.

2

Coinductive reformulation
cMil
of Milner’s system
Mil
(Definition
5
):
We defined
cMil
as the result of replacing the fixed-point rule
\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt
in
Mil
with rules of the scheme
\left\{{\text{LCoProof${}_{i}$}}\right\}_{i\in{\rm Nature}}

that formalize provability by LLEE-witnessed coinductive proofs.

\triangleright

As the ‘kernel’ of
cMil
we defined the system
CLC
(Definition
5
)
for combining LLEE-witnessed coinductive proofs
with only the rules of the scheme
\left\{{\text{LCoProof${}_{i}$}}\right\}_{i\in{\rm Nature}}
.

3

Proof transformations
between
cMil
and
Mil

that show their theorem-equivalence.

\triangleright

We showed that the rules of
\left\{{\text{LCoProof${}_{i}$}}\right\}_{i\in{\rm Nature}}
are correct and admissible for
Mil
,
and that this implies that every derivation in
cMil
can be transformed into one in
Mil
with the same conclusion
by eliminating occurrences of these rules. (See Section
6
.)

\triangleright

We showed that the fixed-point rule
\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt
of
Mil
is derivable in
cMil
,
because every instance
\iota
can be mimicked by an instance of
LCoProof
1

that uses the premise of
\iota
,
and, as a side-condition, a LLEE-witnessed coinductive proof over
Mil
-
.
As a consequence we showed that every derivation in
Mil
can be transformed into one in
cMil
with the same conclusion
by eliminating occurrences of
\textrm{RSP}^{*}\hskip-1.0pt
. (See Section
7
.)

4

Coinductive reformulation
\overline{\text{\sf cMil}}

of the variant
\overline{\sf Mil}{}^{\prime}
(with the powerful rule USP, see Definition
2
) of
Mil
:
We formulated systems
\overline{\text{\sf cMil}}
(Definition
5
) and
CC
(Definition
5
)
based on coinductive proofs without LLEE-witnesses.
The systems
\overline{\text{\sf cMil}}
and
CC
can be recognized as complete variants of
cMil
and
CLC
.
We have, however, only argued that in Remark
24
(on the basis of earlier work
[
Gra06
]
),
but not proved it in detail here.

5

Proof transformations
between
cMil
and
CLC
, and
\overline{\text{\sf cMil}}
and
CC
that show their theorem-equivalence.
Here the idea of the non-trivial transformations from
cMil
to
CLC
was to ‘hide’ all derivation parts that consist of axioms or rules of the purely equational part
Mil
-
of
Mil
into the correctness conditions of solutions in LLEE-witnessed coinductive proofs,
which occur as side-conditions of instances of rules of
\left\{{\text{LCoProof${}_{i}$}}\right\}_{i\in{\rm Nature}}
in
CLC
.
The transformation from
\overline{\text{\sf cMil}}
to
CC
operates analogously.
(See Lemma
23
,
(iii)
, and
(iv)
.)

we have illustrated the web of proof transformations between,

,

Figure 19.
The coinductive proof system
CLC
(next to
cMil
) as potential beachhead for a completeness proof of
Mil
.
We label with a question mark the transformation that is not defined here
(it can be extracted from the completeness proof of
Mil
).

The coinductive reformulation
cMil
of
Mil
, and its coinductive kernel
CLC
,
can be looked upon as being situated roughly half-way in between
Mil
and bisimulations between chart interpretations of star expressions.
We illustrate this in Figure
19
.
This picture arises from the highest level in Figure
18
,
when instantiated for specified conclusions
e_{1}\mathrel{{=_{\text{\sf Mil}}\hskip 1.0pt}}e_{2}
,
e_{1}\mathrel{{=_{\text{\sf cMil}}\hskip 1.0pt}}e_{2}
,
and
e_{1}\mathrel{{=_{\text{\sf C\hskip-0.5ptL\hskip-0.5ptC}}\hskip 1.0pt}}e_{2}
,
and by extending it further to
{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e_{1}}\hskip-0.5pt)\mathrel{\hbox{\kern 0.43057pt\vbox{\hbox{\raise 0.43057pt\hbox{\kern-0.43057pt{$\leftrightarrow$}\kern-0.43057pt}}\hrule}\kern 0.43057pt}\hskip 1.0pt}{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e_{2}}\hskip-0.5pt)
, and hence to
e_{1}\mathrel{{=_{\scriptscriptstyle\llbracket{\cdot}\rrbracket_{\hskip-0.5ptP}}}}e_{2}
.
For the last step we use soundness of
CLC
,
which follows from soundness of
Mil
with respect to
\mathrel{{=_{\scriptscriptstyle\llbracket{\cdot}\rrbracket_{\hskip-0.5ptP}}}}
, see Proposition
2
,
in view of the proof transformations that link
CLC
via
cMil
to
Mil
.
Now we note that derivations of
CLC
represent proof-trees of coinductive proofs
each of which defines a bisimulation up to provability
by Proposition
20
,
and Remark
21
.
Therefore we can argue that prooftrees in
CLC
, and by proof-theoretic association also prooftrees in
cMil
,
are situated roughly half-way in between prooftrees in
Mil
and bisimulations between chart interpretation of star expressions.
We think that Figure
19
provides a reasonable suggestion of the proof-theoretic closeness of these systems.

The proof-theoretic connections of
CLC
with
cMil
and
Mil
guarantee that completeness of
Mil
(with respect to
\mathrel{{=_{\scriptscriptstyle\llbracket{\cdot}\rrbracket_{\hskip-0.5ptP}}}}
)
is equivalent to completeness of
cMil
, and also to completeness of
CLC
. Stronger still, the proof transformations between
CLC
,
cMil
, and
Mil
guarantee that every completeness proof of
Mil
can be ‘routed through’
CLC
(and also through
cMil
).
Such a ‘rerouting’ through
CLC
of a completeness proof of
Mil
does, however, not need to be equally natural as a ‘direct’ completeness proof of
Mil
.
But since
CLC
is intuitively much closer to
\mathrel{{=_{\scriptscriptstyle\llbracket{\cdot}\rrbracket_{\hskip-0.5ptP}}}}
than
Mil
(as suggested by Figure
19
),
much hope was warranted to obtain a completeness proof for
Mil
by finding a completeness proof for
CLC
first,
or to at least to use concepts that we have introduced here.
(The latter hope turned out to be justified.)

Indeed, since the proof systems
cMil
and
CLC
are tied to process graphs via the circular deductions they permit and to bisimulations up to provability,
and since
cMil
and
CLC
are theorem-equivalent with
Mil
,
they were conceived as (and still can be) natural beachheads for a completeness proof of Milner’s system.
In Figure
19
we have indicated the step to
CLC
that is missing
here
for a completeness proof of
Mil
with a question mark:
a completeness proof of
CLC
, and that is, an argument
that, for every bisimulation between
{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e_{1}}\hskip-0.5pt)
and
{\mathcal{C}}(\hskip-0.5pt{e_{2}}\hskip-0.5pt)
where
e_{1}
and
e_{2}
are given star expressions,
yields a prooftree in
CLC
with conclusion
e_{1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.2539,0.4102,0.8828}=}}e_{2}
.

Due to their feature of permitting derivations that can be construed as combinations of
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-bisimulations (up to provability),
we were confident that the proof system
cMil
and its coinductive kernel
CLC
substantially increase the space for graph-based approaches to finding a completeness proof of
Mil
.
A concrete indication for this expectation was the following.
By closely analyzing the completeness proof in
[
GF20b
,
GF20a
]
for the tailored restriction
BBP
of
Mil
to ‘
1
-free’ star expressions
we find:

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline ons:grab:fokk:2020:lics}\left.\;\parbox{293.2521pt}{Valid equations between $1$-free\ star expressions
admit derivations in \text{\sf C\hskip-0.5ptL\hskip-0.5ptC}\ of depth less than or equal to 2.}\;\>\right\}

(32)

This fact suggests the following research question:

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline esearch:question}\left.\;\parbox{293.2521pt}{Can derivations in \text{\sf C\hskip-0.5ptL\hskip-0.5ptC}\ (derivations in $\text{\sf cMil}$) always be simplified to some kind of normal form
that is of bounded depth (respectively, of bounded nesting depth of {LLEE}-wit\-nessed\ coinductive proofs)?}\;\>\right\}

(33)

Despite of the fact that this question admits a trivial answer in view of the completeness proof of
Mil
in
[
Gra22a
]
,
see
(C2)
and
(C3)
below,
it can be desirable to also find an answer
that is based on a proof-theoretic analysis,
and that leads to a
workable concept of ‘normal form’ for derivations in
CLC
or in
cMil
.
Intuitions for finding such a concept
may be found in developing simplification steps of
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-charts with
LLEE
under
{\color[rgb]{0.6875,0.0977,0.0977}1}
-bisimilarity,
as those are used in the completeness proofs for
BBP
with respect to ‘
1
-free’ star expressions in
[
GF20b
,
GF20a
]
,
and for
Mil
with respect to all star expressions in
[
Gra22a
,
Gra22b
]
.
We close by listing consequences of the latter (one is direct, the other two are a bit technical).

Consequences of the completeness proof of
Mil
in
[
Gra22a
]
.

Below we list the most important consequences that the completeness proof of
Mil
as summarized in
[
Gra22a
]

has for the line of investigation on coinductive versions of
Mil
as reported here:

(
C
1)

The coinductive versions
cMil
and
CLC
of Milner’s system
Mil
are (just like
Mil
)
complete with respect to process semantics equality
\mathrel{{=_{\scriptscriptstyle\llbracket{\cdot}\rrbracket_{\hskip-0.5ptP}}}}
of star expressions.
(This follows directly from Theorem 5.1 in
[
Gra22a
]

in view of Theorem
40
here.)

(
C
2)

Valid equations between star expressions
admit derivations in
CLC
of depth less than or equal to
2
.
(This generalization of (
32
)
can be shown by a close analysis of the structure of the completeness proof of
Mil
in Section 5 of
[
Gra22a
]
.
The proof is similar to that of Corollary
33
,
33
,
and also similar to the argumentation for the analogous statement concerning ‘
1
-free’ star expressions and the system
BBP
as reported above.)

(
C
3)

As a consequence of
(C2)

the research question (
33
) admits the trivial answer “yes”,
albeit one that avoids a close proof-theoretic analysis.
(Yet we still think that an answer that is based on a fine-grained proof-theoretic analysis would be more desirable).
